- **Sources:** official Bandcamp release and credits (5 tracks, 1 Oct. 2025): https://kakumasound.bandcamp.com/album/africa-yetu ; Kakuma Sound official history/ownership: https://www.kakumasound.org/about.html ; official camp recording studio and festival details: https://www.kakumasound.org/events.html ; project overview and ownership statement: https://www.kakumasound.org/ .

### Tibetan exile cassette culture — Dharamsala, Mundgod and Tibetan settlements in India (1988 onward)
- **Status:** CONTEXT ONLY for the iconic commercial cassettes; PROMISING archive route for lesser-circulated diaspora and amateur recordings.
- **Early exile cassette albums:**
  - **Rangzen Shönu — *Modern Tibetan Folk Songs* (1988):** three young Tibetan refugees living in Dharamsala recorded and produced the first cassette of original Tibetan songs influenced by folk-rock. Songs addressed political independence and nomadic life. The cassette became iconic in Tibetan exile culture and was smuggled into Tibet; CONTEXT ONLY for obscurity.
  - **Tsering Paljor Phupatsang — *Rangzen Sön Tsa* (1990):** solo cassette recorded in a Delhi studio over two nights; about 2,000 copies sold at the Kalachakra ceremony in Varanasi and another 1,000 distributed through Tibetan shops in settlements. It contains some repeats from the 1988 cassette and new original songs. CONTEXT ONLY.
  - **Tibetan Institute of Performing Arts modern music group — *Tibetan Songs* (1990):** an experimental cassette celebrating the Dalai Lama's Nobel Peace Prize, blending traditional and new songs; institution-led, CONTEXT ONLY.
  - **Yak Band — *Rangzen* (1995):** original songs recorded over two weeks in a small cement house in the Dharamsala region with unreliable power; sold to pilgrims and distributed through Tibetan shops in India/Nepal. Homemade dubs appeared quickly, including a Singapore pirate copy with member names and photos removed. The band was a prominent exile act; CONTEXT ONLY, but the pirate-copy trail is useful evidence of diaspora cassette circulation.
  - **Ah-Ka-Ma Band — *Modern Tibetan Songs* (1995):** TIPA's rock band blended Western instruments with traditional Tibetan melodies/instruments; institution-linked and CONTEXT ONLY.
  - **Tsering Gyurme — *Tsawai Lama* (1996–97):** cassette release marking a new stage in Tibetan exile pop; widely circulated, CONTEXT ONLY.
- **Archive route:** Trace Foundation's Latse Library holds approximately 4,000 Tibetan performing-arts audiovisual objects dated 1980–2012, covering music videos, events, drama, opera, religious, folk, dance and popular music. Its scope includes Tibetan diaspora in India and beyond, as well as Tibetan areas of China, Bhutan and Ladakh. This is a high-value item-level search route for lesser-known amateur recordings, private cassettes, diaspora bands and regional performers.
- **Related family-exile recording:** the *Shining Spirit* project (2006; film released 2009) recorded members of Jamyang Yeshi's Amdo Tibetan musical family over three weeks, connecting relatives separated by political exile and geography (Dharamsala, Canada and the United States). Digital Himalaya hosts an open-access trailer; this is a public documentary project, CONTEXT ONLY.
- **Cautions / next:** do not treat all Tibetan exile music as outsider music; these are culturally significant, often locally popular works. Prioritise unissued, private and amateur diaspora recordings in the Latse collection, not the iconic cassette series. Respect the fact that some performers and composers inside Tibet are nameless in scholarship because of personal risk.
- **Sources:** Keila Diehl, *Echoes from Dharamsala: Music in the Life of a Tibetan Refugee Community* (2002), especially Chapter 5: https://dokumen.pub/echoes-from-dharamsala-music-in-the-life-of-a-tibetan-refugee-community-9780520936003.html ; academic overview of exile pop cassette history: https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/popular-music/article/locating-music-in-capitalism-a-view-from-exile-tibet1/0CC6360DABCA788542A9378B980E0F8E ; Latse Library AV collection (4,000 objects, 1980–2012): https://registry.clir.org/projects/1906/ ; *Shining Spirit* Digital Himalaya record: https://www.ndl.iitkgp.ac.in/ch_document/digital_himalaya/digitalhimalaya_avm/1_02 .


## User-led psychiatric-survivor and peer-run creative spaces — first recording-focused screen (2026-11-15)

### Netværkstedet Thorvaldsen / Rooster Music and RadioTi — Frederiksberg, Copenhagen, Denmark
- **Status:** HIGH PRIORITY archive/recording-ecosystem lead; no individual album or track yet identified.
- **Organisation:** Netværkstedet Thorvaldsen describes itself as a user-led activity space for people with experience of psychiatric services and psychosocial vulnerability, explicitly without therapeutic/pedagogical staff. It was co-founded in 1995.
- **Direct music-production evidence:** Copenhagen Municipality's 2021 description calls Rooster Music the centre's creative flagship: a professional sound studio and music publisher where members can record and release music with professional producers. It also reports annual concerts and tours under the name “Thorvaldsens talrige talenter” (Thorvaldsen's many talents). A separate municipal document describes RadioTi as a local radio station “for and with” people with psychosocial problems, closely collaborating with Thorvaldsen and Rooster Music. A biography in a Copenhagen municipal attachment says psychiatric service users produce their own music at Rooster Music.
- **Why this is unusually strong:** unlike a generic arts workshop, the evidence confirms an actual member recording/release pipeline, a named studio/imprint, concerts and a community radio station. The output could be singles, CDs, demos, radio sessions or a back catalogue not discoverable through ordinary commercial music search.
- **Next:** identify Rooster Music's producer(s), catalogue/release names, physical CD-Rs and digital releases; search old Thorvaldsen newsletters, RadioTi programme archives and municipal grant files; trace the “Thorvaldsens talrige talenter” concert/tour recordings. Confirm which releases are member-created, which are collaborative productions and who controls rights. Current Thorvaldsen website still lists Rooster Music contact details but does not expose a catalogue. No contact made and no audio auditioned.
- **Sources:** Copenhagen Municipality, *Netværksstedet Thorvaldsen* (2021): https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/778bca12-35f7-444c-b45f-ae8468218d17/6f6045da-10b6-492a-88e6-4f54f852d2cb-bilag-2.pdf ; Copenhagen Municipality, *Liste over ansøgninger* (RadioTi, Rooster Music and Thorvaldsen relationship): https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/5e774b7c-79d1-4b97-930a-48ff56cdcff8/693a4c11-bde8-414b-96c9-272aea108a39-bilag-2.pdf ; Thorvaldsen official site: https://www.thorvaldsen.dk/ ; contact page: https://www.thorvaldsen.dk/kontakt ; municipal biography confirming user-produced music at Rooster Music: https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/226901a3-c283-48fa-af6e-09397392fe36/9a2201ee-fdc8-46b0-9f1b-36db5851b284-bilag-2.pdf .

### Frente de Artistas del Borda — Hospital José T. Borda, Buenos Aires, Argentina
- **Status:** VERIFIED resident/participant-performed album; PROMISING, with obscurity and circulation to assess. A second later track is independently documented.
- **Album:** *Música para la oreja izquierda* (also rendered *Canciones para la oreja izquierda* in press; “Music/Songs for the Left Ear”), 16 tracks, 1,000-copy pressing, released in September 2008 with support from Argentina's Secretaría de Cultura. Contemporary reporting says all tracks were performed by hospital inpatients; the Frente's own blog advertised the CD as the product of its music workshop and announced a concert based on its songs. The official grant description states that the album consisted of songs performed by workshop members, who included people living in or attending the hospital, and that the music workshop worked with artistic coordinators, psychologists and other community members. This is not simply an outside musician's recording at the hospital.
- **Related later recording:** “La sangre borda” (3:07), credited on the 2017 digital compilation *Épocas* to “Acuerdos entre cuerdas y las vueltas del tambor (taller de música del Frente de Artistas del Borda).” A former workshop facilitator told Rock.com.ar that the song was made after the 2008 disc, remained unreleased, and was later recorded by workshop members for *Épocas*. Treat this as a later group composition, separate from the 2008 album.
- **Obscurity:** the 2008 CD had a 1,000-copy run and some Buenos Aires press coverage; it is not an undiscovered artefact, but is poorly represented in broad music catalogues and deserves a compendium entry as an overlooked participant-made recording. *Épocas* is a public digital compilation and its track is context rather than a rare artefact.
- **Cautions / next:** obtain the full 2008 tracklist, identify the original CD/cover and current custodian, clarify exact participant credits without guessing identities, and compare the 2017 version of “La sangre borda” with the workshop's earlier unreleased version. The Frente includes artists and psychologists from inside and outside the hospital; attribute the recording to the music workshop rather than assuming every contributor was an inpatient. No audio auditioned and no contact made.
- **Sources:** Argentina Secretaría de Cultura official project description: https://www.infoleg.gob.ar/basehome/actos_gobierno/actosdegobierno10-11-2008-3.htm ; Frente de Artistas del Borda official blog (2008 release/concert notice): https://frentedeartistasdelborda.blogspot.com/2008/09/ ; *Página/12*, 7 Oct. 2008 (16 tracks, 1,000 copies, performed by inpatients): https://www.pagina12.com.ar/diario/suplementos/espectaculos/2-11560-2008-10-07.html ; *Épocas* Bandcamp (2017 track credit and audio listing): https://epocas.bandcamp.com/album/pocas ; Rock.com.ar (former workshop facilitator's account of “La sangre borda”): https://rock.com.ar/notas/un-grito-colectivo-contra-el-neoliberalismo/ ; 2009 Italian profile identifying the CD: https://www.rivistamissioniconsolata.it/2009/08/01/quando-i-matti-regalano-la-felicita/ .

### Maskine Maskine Amager — Amager, Copenhagen, Denmark
- **Status:** VERIFIED venue-run audio/video documentation and community music activity; not yet a verified participant-made recording corpus. CONTEXT / research route.
- **Organisation:** the centre describes itself as a patient-run workshop and community space, operating since January 2019, with creative workshops and member-led activities.
- **Audio/video archive:** its official *MMusik* page contains filmed live performances at the venue: El Ray (official opening, 4 Sep. 2020); Nicolai Agerholm performing Tudeprinsen songs (2023); Tudeprinsen and Love Against The Machine Allstars (2024); A + band performing “My Heart Will Go On” and Farmand (2024); and a 2025 event. These establish a recurring recording practice but do not yet establish that the performers or composers are members/participants rather than invited guests.
- **New 2026 item:** *Virelai* is a 54-minute concert film recorded at Maskine Maskine Amager and premiered at Cinemateket on 11 Nov. 2026. The official event description calls it a musical document of the patient-created and patient-run venue, but does not identify the band's membership or whether the group is made up of participants.
- **Why retain:** the centre has its own film-production unit (MMAfilm), a growing archive of concerts and other audiovisual material, and a member-run creative environment. The video archive and production unit may preserve music-making not issued elsewhere.
- **Next:** establish who Tudeprinsen, Love Against The Machine Allstars, El Ray and Virelai are; distinguish member bands from visiting performers; search MMAfilm's unpublished short clips, MMANYT newsletters (2019–22), internal event records and any member-produced recordings. No contact or audio audition made.
- **Sources:** MM Amager official about page: https://mmamager.dk/om ; official resources / MMAfilm / MMusik archive: https://mmamager.dk/resurser ; official *Virelai* concert-film event: https://www.sst.dk/en-af-os/arrangementer/virelai-koncertfilm .

### KAOS / Black Fuel Ensemble / Introspection Music Experience — Brussels, Belgium
- **Status:** VERIFIED collaborative music and recording project in a psychiatric context; PROMISING for participant contributions, but not a pure resident-authored album.
- **In-house ensemble:** KAOS's residency documentation names the in-house music ensemble **Black Fuel Ensemble**. The EU project report says visiting musician KRANKLAND / Thomas Werbrouck jammed with the ensemble at a Free Podium night at the Psycho-Social Centre, then recorded several sessions with people he met during his residency and used those recordings as samples in his own songs. This confirms recorded sound created in collaboration with people connected to the psychiatric context, but the resulting artist-owned songs are not automatically participant-made music.
- **Introspection Music Experience (IME):** KAOS, INTRAS (Spain) and EDRA (Greece) produced a 16-track album involving 12 solo artists/bands and composers with lived experience of mental vulnerability. KAOS describes the aim as creating new music through exchange and collaboration; an EU report says the music labs involved artists and mental-health-service-user songwriters sharing experiences and collecting source material for songs. Six tracks were written by external solo artists in the initial Belgian project phase (Chloë Nols, KRANKLAND, Korneel Muylle); the exact authorship and recording credits for all 16 tracks need cross-checking.
- **Obscurity:** the international IME project had a public tour, videos and documentary; CONTEXT ONLY as a project. Black Fuel Ensemble sessions and raw source recordings may be less visible and deserve item-level investigation.
- **Next:** identify Black Fuel Ensemble members and surviving live/session recordings; map IME album track credits, distinguish lived-experience songwriter contributions from visiting artist compositions, and check whether participants retain copies or rights to raw session recordings. No audio audition or contact made.
- **Sources:** KAOS official IME page (artists, 16-track album and collaborators): https://www.vzwkaos.be/nl/in-de-kijker/introspection-music-experience ; EU project report on Black Fuel Ensemble and recording sessions: https://ec.europa.eu/programmes/creative-europe/project-result-content/0a99e6c7-d830-4340-9386-ec31e2a6acf6/13._IME_IntroMusicLabsReportingTemplate_Descriptions.pdf ; KAOS residency description: https://www.vzwkaos.be/en/artist-in-residence ; VI.BE project release report: https://vi.be/nieuws/artiesten-releasen-nieuwe-nummers-na-kunstenaarsresidentie-in-psychiatrie .


## Danish peer-run and lived-experience creative spaces — second screen (2026-11-15)

### Nationalt Center for Kunst og Mental Sundhed (CKMS) — Copenhagen / Frederiksberg, Denmark
- **Status:** PROMISING contemporary sound-art and collaborative-music archive lead; no standalone obscure release identified yet.
- **Lydminder:** CKMS documents a workshop project using sound and memory with people who use psychiatric services. An official post says sound artist Ulrik Vincent worked with participants on “Lydminder”; the post embeds a video in which he discusses the work. A later project description says participants collected environmental sounds on their phones, processed them and transferred them to living plants equipped with microchips, making the plants audible repositories for difficult sound memories. This is participant-generated sound art, but not yet a discrete album or freely accessible master.
- **Lyden Ud:** CKMS's 2026 workshop programme explicitly invites participants to record, edit and compose environmental sound, voice, objects and other sources into soundscapes and small collective works. The 15-session series began 12 Aug. 2026 and is facilitated by composers Ulrik Vincent and Puyain Sanati. The official page includes teaser/intro videos; whether complete workshop works are archived or released is unknown.
- **Det Åbne Ensemble:** an improvisation group of people using psychiatric services, in collaboration with CKMS and the Rhythmic Music Conservatory. Participants collectively play instruments and take turns conducting through a light-mixer system. Roskilde Festival's 4 July 2026 programme confirms the ensemble performed on the Platform stage and describes the group as a process-oriented collective music-making project. No recording of the performance or a commercial release was identified in this pass.
- **Recovery Radio:** launched in 2024 as an audio platform for conversations and creative processes; the initial two mini-series concerned emotions and were hosted by Kiki Skovmand and Eini Carina Grønvold. A launch event included a concert. This is primarily a spoken podcast, not a music candidate, but could hold embedded original music or sound works in individual episodes.
- **Why retain:** the centre has documented participant-generated sound recordings, soundscape composition and collaborative improvisation, not just visiting performances. It is a strong route to contemporary sound works by people with lived experience, although current output is likely publicly promoted and therefore not automatically obscure.
- **Next:** identify the exact “Lydminder” and “Lyden Ud” works, file/track names, dates, participant roles, whether recordings survive beyond video teasers, archive custodian, and participant/artist rights. Determine whether Det Åbne Ensemble's Roskilde performance was recorded. Separate participant-made source recordings from compositions/editing by professional facilitators. No audio audition or contact made.
- **Sources:** CKMS “Lydminder”: https://ckms.dk/vi-arbejder-med-lydminder/ ; CKMS “Grønne planter i centrum i anden del af lydworkshop”: https://ckms.dk/groenne-planter-i-centrum-i-anden-del-af-lydworkshop/ ; CKMS “Lyden Ud”: https://ckms.dk/9764/ ; CKMS sound groups: https://ckms.dk/lydgruppe/ ; CKMS Recovery Radio launch: https://ckms.dk/recovery-radio-release-18-juni-17-19/ ; CKMS open scene: https://ckms.dk/aaben-scene-2/ ; Roskilde Festival, Det Åbne Ensemble (4 July 2026): https://www.roskilde-festival.dk/program/kunst-aktivisme/det-aabne-ensemble ; CKMS “Lydminder” interview: https://www.youtube.com/ (linked from official post; exact video URL not recovered in this pass).

### Galebevægelsen / Kanal 1 — Danish psychiatric-survivor movement and public-access media
- **Status:** CONTEXT / promising media-archive route; no verified participant-authored music release yet.
- **Movement and media:** Galebevægelsen began in 1979 with current/former psychiatric patients, relatives and professionals. Kanal 1's official page says people associated with Netværkstedet Thorvaldsen have produced programmes for the channel for more than ten years, including psychiatry, portraits and debate.
- **Music-related items:** the same official page identifies rapper **Rytme-Hans (Hans Martin Hjortsø)** among the musicians and lists “Rytme Hans – et portræt” (4 Jan. 2014), four “Chefen for det hele” programmes hosted by Hans Martin Hjortsø, and a Kim Sonders music video, “The color on my wall” (4 Jan. 2014). The Kanal 1 page does not establish that all these performers are members/service users; the Rytme-Hans identity is connected to the Thorvaldsen community page but individual biography and authorship still need verification. The music video is an audiovisual object, but its relation to Galebevægelsen/Thorvaldsen is not yet clear.
- **Next:** inspect the Vimeo embeds and credits for “Rytme Hans – et portræt” and Kim Sonders; search Kanal 1's “Musik” archive and local radio/TV archives for original songs, sessions and programme audio. Confirm artist's own account of their relationship to the community before including as a participant-made recording. No audio audition or contact made.
- **Sources:** Kanal 1 / Galebevægelsen: https://kanal-1.dk/galebevaegelsen/ ; Rytme-Hans portrait video: https://kanal-1.dk/video/rytme-hans-et-portraet/ ; Kim Sonders music video: https://kanal-1.dk/video/kim-sonders-the-color-on-my-wall/ ; Kanal 1 music archive: https://kanal-1.dk/musik/ ; Galebevægelsen historical overview: https://lex.dk/Galebev%C3%A6gelsen .

### Fountain House København / Ungehuset Fontana — Copenhagen, Denmark
- **Status:** CONTEXT / member music-making route; no qualifying audio recording identified in this pass.
- **Evidence:** Fountain House København describes its membership model and says members in Ungehuset Fontana work creatively with visual art, ceramics and music. Its official 2025 retrospective says “Musik På Tværs” began in 2024 and was a success, bringing members from across the house together to play music. The 2026 programme invited members to join weekly sessions, with a coordinator assembling an ensemble based on members' instruments and experience. A 5 Sept. 2025 Fredags TAMTAM schedule included a concert by Musik På Tværs.
- **Recording status:** no released track, audio/video recording, member band name or archive was located. The evidence proves rehearsals and performances, not surviving recordings.
- **Next:** search the house's photo/video archive, social media and TAMTAM event documentation for concert recordings; ask whether the group has a name, repertoire, demos or member-held recordings. Do not treat workshop attendance or public performance as a recording candidate.

B. Krog and publisher Gateway.dmf.dk and links an iTunes single and an MP3. A 2010 member-magazine report describes Natasha performing it at a campaign event and mentions other live performers. The page describes reports and interviews rather than a music programme; no resident/member-created song recording was identified.

## Radio La Colifata — Hospital José T. Borda, Buenos Aires, Argentina (1991 onward)
- **Status:** VERIFIED participant-generated audio archive / PROMISING for specific member-created jingles and radio art; CONTEXT ONLY for the widely circulated Manu Chao collaborations.
- **Organisation and recording practice:** Radio La Colifata began on 3 August 1991 as a radio column produced from the Hospital Borda by current inpatients, initially recorded in the hospital gardens and relayed through a community FM station. The organisation's official history describes patient-led expression, recorded discussions and exchange with listeners. A 2013 annual report says the live Saturday broadcast from hospital gardens was fully recorded and edited, with inpatients, external participants, community visitors and professionals contributing; keep individual item attribution distinct.
- **Viva La Colifata (2005):** the official archive describes a 20-song collection containing material generated by radio members — songs, poetry and improvisations — mixed by Manu Chao with his own musical bases. The organisation's current support site says two volumes were released in 2005 and makes the songs available for listening/download. This is a verified participant-generated recording corpus, but CONTEXT ONLY for obscurity because of the high-profile Manu Chao collaboration and wide public availability. Do not describe every instrumental backing or production element as participant-created.
- **Spot Colifatos:** the official podcast/archive page explicitly describes this collection as jingles, spots, “chichas” and radio-art material generally produced and created by Colifata members. A SoundCloud set is embedded from the official site at https://soundcloud.com/radio-la-colifata/sets/spots-colifatos . This is the strongest PROMISING item-level lead: participant-created sound and musical radio art, distinct from the famous album. Exact item titles, dates, durations, makers and whether any are full songs remain to be extracted from the SoundCloud set.
- **Rompiendo Muros / Rompiendo Muros II:** the official archive describes these as compilations of microprogrammes (short, edited radio discussions) made by La Colifata. An independent radio-archive blog documents a physical CD titled *Rompiendo Muros*, with 22 tracks of 2–4 minutes, produced/edited by La Colifata and an artisan CD package made in its microenterprise workshop. This is important radio history and an extant audio object, but the described content is mainly speech/reflection rather than music; CONTEXT ONLY unless track-level review finds original music/jingles.
- **Siempre fui loco (c.2003–05):** separate collaboration from *Viva La Colifata*. The official archive says Manu Chao assembled music by twenty Barcelona street bands with La Colifata radio audio; approximately 3,000 copies were sold by the street musicians. This is a mixed outside-band / participant-voice production, not a resident-made music album. CONTEXT ONLY; do not conflate with the 2005 *Viva La Colifata* participant-generated songs.
- **Additional solo/group performance route:** a 2005 *El País* feature documents Enrique Moressi (“Enrique Bolero”) singing boleros with other Colifata members at the hospital's Saturday live programme. This is evidence of member musical performance, but the article does not identify a specific original composition or a surviving standalone recording. Treat as a search route for live radio tapes, not a distinct candidate yet.
- **Current sound-production route:** La Colifata's 2023 article on its “Creativos Colifatos” workshop says members invent and record sonic/audiovisual advertising spots for ethical/sustainable enterprises and create audio-visual material during visits to partner producers. This is participant-generated audio production, though commercial/social advertising rather than music. Keep as a route for jingles and sonic art, not automatically in the music discography.
- **Ethics / rights:** the official organisation makes selected songs and some unpublished sound/audiovisual material available for remixing, but rights are item-specific; the 2005 album mixes member-created material with Manu Chao's musical bases. Do not assume the remix invitation covers all voices, music, performers or archive items. Preserve individual contributors' own names and consent where they have chosen public attribution; do not infer diagnosis or inpatient status for every person heard in a group broadcast.
- **Next:** enumerate the *Spot Colifatos* SoundCloud items (title, duration, upload date, credits, whether jingle/song/spoken ID); inspect the official *Viva La Colifata* tracklists and crosswalk the 20 pieces to original member contributions; locate original *Rompiendo Muros* CD/tracklist and compare 1996–97 broadcasts; seek item-level records or public recordings of Enrique Bolero and other member musicians; identify the archive custodian and rights statement for unpublished audio. No audio auditioned, archive contact or reuse request made.
- **Sources:** official history: https://lacolifata.com.ar/historia-reconocimientos/ ; official podcast/audio archive: https://lacolifata.com.ar/podcasts/ ; official English audio archive: https://lacolifata.com.ar/en/podcasts-2/ ; official support/listening page: https://www.vivalacolifata.org/ ; official *Creativos Colifatos* project: https://lacolifata.com.ar/2023/04/19/productivos-para-si-y-para-terceros-proyecto-colifato-etico-sustentable/ ; 2013 annual report: https://lacolifata.com.ar/wp-content/uploads/2020/10/Informe-Anual-2013.pdf ; *Radio en Papel* CD documentation (12 Sept. 2017): https://radioenpapel.blogspot.com/2017/09/fm-1001-radio-la-colifata-rompiendo.html ; *El País* 2005 report on Enrique Bolero and live music: https://elpais.com/diario/2005/08/14/eps/1124000821_850215.html ; *Página/12* on *Viva La Colifata* (28 July 2009): https://www.pagina12.com.ar/diario/suplementos/espectaculos/15-14715-2009-07-28.html ; *lavaca* on *Siempre fui loco*: https://lavaca.org/notas/la-colifata-radio-libre/ .


## Urakawa Bethel House / Papipupepo Orchestra — Hokkaido, Japan (2012–2013)
- **Status:** HIGH PRIORITY participant-authored album project / PARTIALLY VERIFIED issue. A 22-track CD project and recording sessions are documented, but a released copy/catalogue entry has not yet been located.
- **Environment:** Urakawa Bethel House is a community founded by and for people with lived experience of psychiatric disability, with member-led work, peer research and cultural activities. This is a community setting rather than a hospital recording.
- **Ensemble:** ぱぴぷぺぽ楽団 (*Papipupepo Gakudan*, “Papipupepo Orchestra/Band”) formed in March 2012 around music-loving Bethel members and staff to write and perform original songs. Bethel's FY2012 official report says the group created original songs for the Bethel Festival, with each team writing lyrics and music and performing its own song; a weekly music team and meeting followed, including discussion of CD and music-goods ideas.
- **CD project:** A 14 May 2013 Japanese news report says the group was preparing its first CD, with 22 songs. Recording was completed by February 2013; 56 people participated in singing/playing, including 38 Bethel members with lived experience (described as about half the service users) plus staff/supporters. Most lyrics and music were written by members themselves. Named song examples: “べてるのずんどこ節” (“Bethel Zundoko-bushi”), a song about embracing one's experience, and “住居っていいな” (“Housing is Nice”), about the value of companions/community. The report said a June 2013 release was planned and a booklet with rehearsal photographs and lyrics was being prepared; no evidence yet confirms the CD was actually issued on schedule.
- **Why it fits:** a sizeable, multi-participant body of original songs made by members of a peer community, with a primary institutional annual report corroborating the ensemble and songwriting process and a contemporaneous news account giving the 22-track recording details.
- **Next:** identify the CD title, actual release date, label/catalogue number, physical copy and complete 22-track list; search Bethel's official shop, magazine/newsletter archive, Japanese second-hand CD listings and member blogs; check whether any demos/live recordings predate the CD. Preserve Japanese orthography and translation nuance. Do not repeat the colloquial term “papipupepo” as a diagnostic label outside the group's own self-description. No audio audition or contact made.
- **Sources:** Urakawa Bethel House FY2012 official annual report (group formed, team-authored songs, weekly music meetings and CD/product planning): https://www.urakawa-bethel.or.jp/images/h24houkokusho.pdf ; contemporaneous 14 May 2013 report (22 songs, 56 participants, 38 members, most lyrics/music member-created, release planned for June): https://chachacha.rgr.jp/news/document/NewsArticle.cgi?mon=5&year=2013 ; Bethel official newsletter/profile: https://note.com/bethelnoie/n/n637ed2b3ceb1 .

## Workman Arts — Toronto, Ontario, Canada: Bruised Years Choir and Sounding Spaces
- **Status:** VERIFIED participant-group live audio / PROMISING limited-circulation recording; additional sound-art archive route.
- **Bruised Years Choir:** Workman Arts established this year-round choir in January 2016 for artists living with mental health and/or addiction issues. It began as a six-week course with seven participants and grew to engage over fifty members during a season; led by Jim LeFrancois and Rob Joy, it rehearsed contemporary popular songs in group arrangements. Workman Arts describes it as a resident ensemble.
- **Independent live recordings:** Toronto music blog **Mechanical Forest Sound** published two downloadable MP3 recordings from the choir's performance at Workman Arts Main Hall during Long Winter 7.5 on 29 March 2019: “Trouble in Mind” (composer Richard M. Jones) and “We Are the Champions” (Queen cover). The blog describes a dozen singers, guided by Jim LeFrancois on piano and Johnny Spence on guitar, and calls the choir one of Workman Arts' resident ensembles. These are documented participant-group performances, not original compositions; the blog-hosted MP3s appear to be niche event recordings rather than a commercial release. The direct file links currently return 403 to automated retrieval, but remain linked from the blog page.
- **Sounding Spaces / Lingering Echoes (2025):** Workman Arts' sound-art archive documents the *Lingering Echoes* exhibition, resulting from the “Sounding Spaces” course in its Train to Present programme. The exhibition brought together sound-centred works by Aliyah Aziz, Ghislan Sutherland-Timm, Middle, Tanya Louise Workman and Winta Hagos; named works include Ghislan Sutherland-Timm's “What Ails Me, Opens Me.mp3,” Middle's “Back A Yaad,” Tanya Louise Workman's “echo (chamber),” Winta Hagos's “Wegee Bun,” and Aliyah Aziz's “Return” (described as a sculpture with sound composition). This is a documented sound-art output set, but not all works are necessarily songs or standalone recordings. Treat as a secondary sound-art route and verify participant/member status and playback access per item.
- **Other music-related work:** Workman Arts archive documents the choir's “Greatest Hits Volume One” public showcase (27 June 2019) and its participation in Luminato's *Maada’ookii Songlines* (23 June 2019); these are events, not album titles. A 2021 virtual open house also included Bruised Years Choir performances by Julie Crann and Ethelrida Zabala-Laxa.
- **Obscurity:** CONTEXT for the choir as a known programme with many public performances; PROMISING for the specific two independently archived live tracks and any unreleased rehearsal/arrangement tapes. The sound-art works are public exhibition outputs and need individual assessment.
- **Next:** verify current accessibility and permission status of the two MP3s; identify the choir's full 2019 set and any other independent live recordings; search Workman Arts' media archive, Long Winter recordings, Rendezvous with Madness and member-artist pages for participant-created songs or original compositions; inspect *Lingering Echoes* playback/credits and distinguish artist-owned sound works from commissioned exhibition sound. No audio auditioned, contact made or rights request made.
- **Sources:** Workman Arts choir history and 2019 programme: https://workmanarts.com/wa_genre/musical/ ; Workman Arts membership criteria: https://workmanarts.com/become-member/ ; Mechanical Forest Sound, 24 May 2019 with MP3 links: https://mechanicalforestsound.blogspot.com/2019/05/ ; Workman Arts Sound Art archive / *Lingering Echoes*: https://workmanarts.com/wa_type/sound-art/ ; Workman Arts musical archive: https://workmanarts.com/wa_genre/musical/ ; Luminato / choir context: https://workmanarts.com/wa_type/performance/page/2/ .


## Creedmoor Psychiatric Center / The Living Museum — Issa Ibrahim and DSM5 (Queens, New York, USA; 2004–2012)
- **Status:** VERIFIED ward-recorded solo music and a verified hospital-recorded ensemble CD; HIGH PRIORITY item-level source audit, with obscurity assessed separately for each release.
- **Maker and recording conditions:** Issa Ibrahim's own press kit says he was committed to Creedmoor Psychiatric Center in Queens in 1990 and began self-recording in 2004. He states that he officially released CDs while still institutionalised, recording surreptitiously with a Fostex portable 16-track digital recorder/CD burner in bathrooms and bedrooms on the locked ward, playing all instruments and singing all vocals. He says he was released in 2009; later recordings were made at halfway houses and at home. These are his own first-person statements, not independently audited tape logs, but they are specific and corroborated by NPR's account of receiving a CD recorded in his hospital room.
- **Solo albums / corpus:** his official store catalogue lists *Square Roots* (2004; UPC 888174226583) and *Cosmic Knockout* (2007; UPC 888174253176), alongside later releases *Zombie Savant* (2010) and *The Asylum Years: The Best of the Rest from Latrine Studios 2004–2011* (2012; UPC 888174253190). The first two fall within the documented institutional period and are likely direct ward-recording candidates; exact album-by-album recording dates and track provenance still need verification. *The Asylum Years* covers 2004–2011 and must not be treated as entirely Creedmoor-recorded: it spans the 2009 discharge and subsequent halfway-house/home period. Amazon Music lists 25 tracks and 75 minutes for the 2012 compilation, beginning “Gidget Meets the Secret Agent Man,” “Afro Pick,” “State of the Onion,” “Lazy, Dumb and Dangerous,” and “Smoke Stacks.” NPR's *State of the Re:Union* episode reports that Ibrahim gave producer Laura Starecheski a CD of original songs recorded in his room on the ward in 2004; the transcript reproduces a short excerpt from one song but the exact disc/album is not identified.
- **DSM5 — The Living Museum Band:** the official music page says DSM5 formed in January 2000 within The Living Museum at Creedmoor, and was a joint venture of outpatients, volunteers and hospital staff, including diagnosed and undiagnosed musicians. Issa Ibrahim is identified as frontman and principal songwriter. The page states that *Insanity Never Sounded So Good!* was recorded at Creedmoor in summer 2011 by engineer Ben Harson, with additional production by Oliver Hoffmann, from original recordings by Ibrahim; the CD was released in spring 2012. The artist's official product page says it contains ten songs written and performed by musicians connected to the mental-health-care system. Named tracks: “Finally Gone,” “Shock Value,” “Someone To Save Me,” “Somewhere/Nowhere,” “Shining Down On Me,” “D.W.B.,” “Christmas in Creedmoor,” “I'm a Living Museum,” “Been So Long,” and “DSM5.” CD UPC 884501690515. This is a verified original ensemble recording made on hospital grounds, but do not describe all musicians as inpatients.
- **Related film/audio:** Ibrahim's *Patient's Rites* is a musical documentary made from songs he wrote and recorded during his Creedmoor years, intercut with dialogue on stigma; it is not itself an original album. His later *The Cosmic Knockout* (2024) also incorporates songs originally recorded in Creedmoor bathrooms and bedrooms, but the film is a later reconstruction/compilation of earlier works. Keep film soundtracks and original masters separate.
- **Fountain House connection:** Ibrahim is now a member-artist represented by Fountain House Gallery, but his Creedmoor recordings predate that association and must be attributed to him / The Living Museum / Creedmoor, not to Fountain House. Fountain House's Media and Tech Center appears to have supported music production, audio editing and podcasts (a member's first-person employment profile says so); official search surfaced the spoken-word *Voices Within* podcast, not a member-made music catalogue. Treat this as a future archive route, not evidence of additional music releases.
- **Obscurity assessment:** Ibrahim has become publicly visible through his memoir, films, NPR coverage, and recent albums, so he is not an unknown person. Nevertheless, the early ward-recorded DIY CDs and DSM5's limited-edition 2012 CD are relatively niche, directly relevant artefacts. Prioritise the exact 2004/2007 solo masters, original ward CDs and the 2011 DSM5 source recordings rather than his 2025–26 commercial releases.
- **Next:** obtain full tracklists/liner notes for *Square Roots* and *Cosmic Knockout*; crosswalk the 25 *Asylum Years* tracks to original recording dates and settings; locate the original CD Ibrahim gave to NPR producer Laura Starecheski in 2004; identify DSM5 members and instrument credits from the CD insert without inferring inpatient status; trace original 2011 multitracks/session tapes and any rehearsal/live recordings at the Living Museum; clarify rights and whether artist has approved online circulation. Do not assume a public limited-edition CD purchase listing means permission to repost audio. No audio auditioned or contact made.
- **Sources:** Issa Ibrahim first-person press kit (ward recording method and 2009 release): https://www.issaibrahim.net/epk ; official music page (DSM5 history, 2011 Creedmoor recording, *Insanity Never Sounded So Good!*): https://www.issaibrahim.net/music ; official DSM5 CD page (10 tracks, UPC and recording credits): https://www.issaibrahim.net/store/p/dsm5-living-museum-band-music-cd-insanity-never-sounded-so-good-2012-limited-quantities-collectors-item ; official catalogue/store (2004/2007/2010/2012 releases and UPCs): https://www.issaibrahim.net/store ; Amazon Music *The Asylum Years* 25-track listing: https://music.amazon.co.jp/albums/B00F4LP8HA ; NPR / PRX *State of the Re:Union* transcript (2004 ward CD): https://cdn.nycitynewsservice.com/blogs.dir/422/files/2016/02/TheHospitalAlwaysWins_SOTRU__Starecheski_TRANSCRIPT.pdf ; NY1 report on *Patient's Rites*: https://ny1.com/nyc/queens/news/2015/09/24/former-creedmoor-patient-shares-his-mental-health-journey-through-film ; official Fountain House Gallery artist profile: https://fountainhousegallery.org/artist/issa-ibrahim ; Fountain House *Voices Within* podcast (spoken-word archive, not music): https://www.fountainhouse.org/news/the-voices-within-podcast-episode-9-emergency-stories-from-a-mental-health-response-system-in-crisis .


## Creative Growth Art Center / William Scott — *Peace Makers in the Summer Time* (Oakland, California, USA; 2022 sessions / 2025 release)
- **Status:** VERIFIED participant-artist collaborative album / PROMISING limited-circulation release.
- **Artist/environment:** William Scott has worked at Creative Growth since 1992; the centre's official biography describes him as a self-taught artist whose work imagines alternative realities and positive social transformation. Creative Growth is a progressive art studio for artists with developmental disabilities. This album was Scott's first foray into music.
- **Recording:** *Peace Makers in the Summer Time* by William Scott and BJ Armour was released 20 June 2025 as digital album and limited-edition 12-inch vinyl. It was made in collaboration with facilitator/producer Hop Peternell, with sessions at Creative Growth in Oakland and Studio Route 29 in Frenchtown, New Jersey. The Creative Growth announcement says the album was premiered at NADA 2025 and proceeds would support participating artists and future music projects for musicians with intellectual and developmental disabilities.
- **Tracklist / split provenance:** Side A, William Scott (four tracks): “Peace Makers in the Summer Time” (1:20), “People Are Coming Back To Life” (5:57), “Santa Cruz Beach Boardwalk” (5:04), “Whole Wide World” (2:36). Scott's vocals are improvised; he gave direction for desired sound and sometimes suggested instrumental melodies. Songs were co-composed and produced by William Scott and Hop Peternell; Peternell played guitar, synth and drum machine, with additional drum programming by Liam Peternell. Side B, BJ Armour (four tracks): “Fall is Coming,” “Swimming in the Spa,” “Qweirder,” and “If You Love Me Let Me Leave.” These are co-composed and produced by BJ Armour and Peternell; Armour's vocal takes are improvised, with further layering/arranging. Do not attribute the full album to Creative Growth alone: recording locations span both studios, and the two artists' sides should remain distinct.
- **2026 follow-up:** Creative Growth's *Stereo Dynamic* exhibition (18 Sep.–21 Nov. 2026) celebrates music-inspired art and live performance. The opening event listed William Scott performing his original song “Peacemakers in the Summertime”; the closing event announced Suver & The Free Key Choir. This is a performance lead, not a separate recording.
- **Obscurity:** PROMISING rather than high priority. The 2025 release was promoted at NADA and is sold digitally/on vinyl, but it is a small, collaborative record with a niche audience and documents an unusual participant-led creative process. Focus on Scott's side and any earlier demos, not on treating the entire project as an institutional production.
- **Next:** check whether William Scott's 2026 live performance was recorded; identify exact recording venues per track/session and whether all four Side A tracks were made at Creative Growth; seek demos/rehearsal recordings predating the 2025 album; trace other Creative Growth artist music/video projects and the Free Key Choir; preserve full composer/producer/performer credits. No audio auditioned or contact made.

## The Dragon Café / Mental Fight Club — The Dragon Café Singers, *In This Circle Is My Heart* (Southwark, London, UK; 2016–2025 songs / July 2025 album)
- **Status:** VERIFIED collaborative community-song album / PROMISING participant-led song corpus. The album is publicly available but little-known beyond arts/mental-health community networks.
- **Environment:** The Dragon Café is a creative community space run by Mental Fight Club, with free wellbeing and arts activities open to all. It is not a closed psychiatric institution and its patrons are not all necessarily mental-health service users; Mental Fight Club's stated purpose is social inclusion for people affected by mental ill-health. Its weekly singing group and songwriting ensemble are led by community musician Vivien Ellis.
- **Album:** *In This Circle Is My Heart* was released in July 2025 by The Dragon Café Singers, containing 12 original songs collaboratively written between 2016 and 2025. The choir recorded at 9M Studios in Southwark. Vivien Ellis's first-person blog provides the song histories and identifies direct patron contributions.
- **Track-level provenance / notable songs:**
  - “Roots” (written for Shakespeare's Globe Concert for Winter, Dec. 2019); Dragon Café patron/song-maker Daniel O'Byrne helped shape it; soloist Helen McDonald.
  - “Here and Now” (2022), written for the Café's tenth anniversary; Latin-inspired and accompanied by a dance created by dance leaders Ariella and Renzo.
  - “Southwark Rebel Women” (written and recorded virtually during 2021 lockdown); celebrates six women, including Dragon Café founder Sarah Wheeler; patron Caroline Moore sings a tribute to unrecorded voices.
  - “Ballad of The Dragon Café” (2024), part of a trilogy for the Café's tenth anniversary; Café patrons Sionie and Daniel helped write it; the group distilled a 100-verse draft.
  - “Drive the Cold Winter Away” (2017), new lyrics to an Elizabethan carol tune, written for the Globe's Concert for Winter.
  - “Music to Hear” (2018), a musical setting of Shakespeare's Sonnet 8 for Globe Sonnet Sunday.
  - “When Icicles Hang By The Wall” (2016), new lyrics to a song quoted in *Love's Labour's Lost*, performed at the Globe's Concert for Winter.
  - “Finding Our Way” (2016), the first song with lyrics and music collaboratively written with Café patron Daniel O'Byrne.
  - “These Things” (group song celebrating shared experiences).
  - “Birds of a Feather” (words adapted from a nineteenth-century broadside ballad printed in Union Street, Borough; new tune by the group).
  - “Friendship Celebration” (song of friendship and mutual support).
  - “At The Dragon Café,” written and performed by patron Daniel O'Byrne.
- **Other patron-created song/performance:** Mental Fight Club's annual report for 2023–24 says the group continued singing, performing and songwriting; it describes a self-written musical performance “Windrush: The Story of Cordelia” by a long-time attendee, about a Jamaican woman arriving on the Empire Windrush in 1948. This appears to be a separate solo performance, not confirmed as part of the 2025 album. Follow up for an audio recording and performer consent.
- **Related recordings:** “Our Journey” (finalised after a project begun in 2019 and adapted during pandemic restrictions) is a creative collaboration between Café patrons and artists, combining patrons' artwork, animation and a song written/performed by The Dragon Café Singers led by Vivien Ellis; premiered at a live Dragon Café event on 4 April (year to verify). “Southwark Rebel Women” is also listed as a standalone song on the Mental Fight Club recordings page, written and recorded during lockdown in 2021.
- **Obscurity:** PROMISING. The 2025 album has a DistroKid/Spotify distribution link and public performance dates, but the underlying patron-collaborative song histories and individual recordings are poorly indexed in music databases. The strongest compendium value is the individual patron-authored/co-authored tracks, not presenting a wellbeing choir as outsider music.
- **Cautions / next:** identify the 12th track if the artist page's text list is incomplete (the page enumerates 12 entries including “At The Dragon Café”); verify the album's exact distributor metadata and complete tracklist; establish which singers/songwriters are patrons, facilitators or invited professional performers without assuming all are patients; identify Daniel O'Byrne's and Sionie's own preferred credits; locate “Windrush: The Story of Cordelia” recording and “Our Journey” audio master; ask the project/participants about permission before reposting. No audio auditioned or contact made.
- **Sources:** Vivien Ellis first-person album history, track-by-track notes and 12-song count (24 Sept. 2025): https://www.vivienellis.co.uk/new-songs-from-the-dragon-cafe/ ; album distribution link: https://distrokid.com/hyperfollow/thedragoncaf/in-this-circle-is-my-heart---songs-from-the-dragon-caf ; Mental Fight Club Recordings page: https://www.mentalfightclub.art/recordings ; Mental Fight Club “Our Journey” project: https://www.mentalfightclub.art/our-creative-programme/our-journey ; Mental Fight Club annual report (2023–24; Dragon Café Singers and Windrush performance): https://register-of-charities.charitycommission.gov.uk/en/charity-search ; City Lit 2025 speaker bio / album reference: https://www.citylit.ac.uk/speakers ; Bloomsbury Festival 2026 choir performance: https://bloomsburyfestival.org.uk/whats-on/the-dragon-cafe-singers-from-isolation-to-connection .

### Studio Route 29 / Beauty Music — BJ Armour, *mooooons/aspirations* (Frenchtown, New Jersey, USA; 29 November 2023)
- **Status:** VERIFIED artist-led EP / PROMISING small-label release.
- **Studio and label:** Studio Route 29 is a progressive art studio centering artists with intellectual and developmental disabilities. Its official projects page describes Beauty Music as a music studio practice, collaborative recording project and record label that centres the musical practices of people with intellectual/developmental disabilities, supporting new music through facilitation, collaboration, instruments and technology.
- **Recording:** *mooooons/aspirations* by BJ Armour was released on Beauty Music on 29 November 2023 as a six-track digital EP. Tracklist: “Welcome” (0:59); “Don't Let Them See You Sweat” (3:28); “Me and Mrs Catwoman Made a Song Called The Kitten Desire” (3:22); “Everybody Do Your Thing, I Dont Care” (3:33); “I Love BJ Three Different Ways” (3:30); “Story About My TJ” (3:13). Individual Bandcamp track pages credit lyrics and vocals to BJ Armour and production to Hop Peternell and BJ Armour. This is a verified artist-led composition/recording with a facilitator-producer, not a workshop cover or outside artist's performance.
- **Artist:** Studio Route 29's official biography describes BJ Armour Jr. as an artist, musician and performer; his work is represented by the studio and Beauty Music. His music is available on streaming platforms. No personal medical history is inferred or required for this entry.
- **Further releases and performances:** Beauty Music's current Bandcamp discography contains two albums: *mooooons/aspirations* (2023) and *Peace Makers in the Summer Time* (2025; separately catalogued above). The latter includes four BJ Armour tracks on Side B, co-composed/produced with Peternell. A 2023 local newspaper report describes Armour performing to three pre-recorded original songs he wrote with audio assistance from Peternell, but the exact performance tracks are not yet crosswalked to the EP. A 2025 album-release performance at White Columns is documented.
- **Beauty Radio:** Studio Route 29 operates an internet radio stream. Its official description says it primarily broadcasts music listened to in the studio, selected by a rotating group of artists and staff, with future plans for interviews, weather reports, puppet monologues and broadcasts of other progressive art studios' dance parties, stories and audio work. This is a discovery conduit, not proof that the stream is a catalogue of participant-made songs.
- **Other performance route:** *Greenwood: The Musical* (24 April 2026) was written/directed by Ira Karp, a performer with Down Syndrome who has created original works, often with Bread and Puppet Theater. Studio Route 29 hosted the musical, described as a “musical soap opera” with songs and dance inspired by nursery rhymes, folk traditions and pop music. No surviving audio/video recording identified yet.
- **Obscurity:** PROMISING. The EP is publicly streamable/downloadable through a small specialist label and not a hidden tape, but it is underrepresented in general music discovery and offers direct artist-led songwriting in a progressive studio environment.
- **Next:** identify where and when each of the six tracks was recorded; inspect credits/liner notes for any other collaborators; trace earlier demos, rehearsal recordings and the 2023 live pre-recorded songs; check whether Beauty Radio archives past shows; find any recording of *Greenwood: The Musical*; preserve Armour's own credits and distinguish his original lyrics/vocals from Peternell's production. No audio auditioned or contact made.
- **Sources:** Studio Route 29 projects / Beauty Music / Beauty Radio: https://studioroute29.org/projects ; Studio Route 29 artist biography: https://studioroute29.org/artists/bj-armour ; Beauty Music EP (tracklist and credits): https://beautymusic.bandcamp.com/album/mooooons-aspirations ; “Story About My TJ” credits: https://beautymusic.bandcamp.com/track/story-about-my-tj ; 2023 *Wiley Report* performance account: https://thewileyreport.blogspot.com/2023/11/ ; Studio Route 29 events archive: https://studioroute29.org/events ; “Greenwood: The Musical”: https://studioroute29.org/events/greenwood-the-musical ; 2025 album-release event: https://studioroute29.org/events/bj-armour-album-release-performance .


## 2026-11-15 — Peer-run music spaces: Thorvaldsen / Rooster Music and Maskine Maskine archive audit

### Netværkstedet Thorvaldsen / Rooster Music — Frederiksberg, Denmark
- **Status:** STRONG VERIFIED participant-led music-production environment; specific release catalogue still unresolved. HIGH-PRIORITY archive route.
- **Institutional history:** A Copenhagen municipal document states that Netværkstedet Thorvaldsen emerged in 1996 from the user-run associations RadioTi and Galebevægelsen. The initiative and subsequent building work were carried out by users themselves. Its stated target group includes people who have experienced, currently experience, or risk developing mental-health conditions, including people also experiencing social hardship. This is an important peer-run environment, not a psychiatric hospital.
- **Music production:** The same municipal documentation lists Rooster Music, RadioTi, music instruction and studio-technique courses among current projects. It explicitly describes Rooster Music as a modern recording studio and music publisher where members can record and release music in collaboration with professional producers, with artistic material and process taking precedence over therapeutic framing. It also describes annual concerts and tours by “Thorvaldsens talrige talenter” to activity, social and residential settings.
- **Current official site:** Thorvaldsen describes itself as 100% user-run, with no therapeutic or pedagogical staff; members themselves direct the activities. Its contact page lists a dedicated Rooster Music telephone number. This supports the user-led nature of the environment, but no actual member recording/release catalogue has yet been identified.
- **Related broadcast archive:** RadioTi is an associated community-radio project. Kanal 1's Galebevægelsen archive lists music-related programmes and performers connected with the wider Thorvaldsen/Galebevægelsen network, but participant identity and authorship need item-level confirmation; do not merge these automatically into Rooster Music.
- **Obscurity:** PROMISING/HIGH PRIORITY discovery route. The production infrastructure and user-authored releases are explicitly documented, but the extant audio corpus, titles, dates, performers and release formats remain unknown.
- **Next:** search historic Rooster Music catalogues, the site's news/publication archive, member magazines, local library/municipal records, RadioTi schedules and Kanal 1 audiovisual listings; request an inventory from the organisation only if/when authorised. Distinguish member compositions from producer-led collaborations and outside performances. No contact, audio audition or rights request made.
- **Sources:** Copenhagen municipal document describing Rooster Music and member recording/release: https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/778bca12-35f7-444c-b45f-ae8468218d17/6f6045da-10b6-492a-88e6-4f54f852d2cb-bilag-2.pdf ; municipal history of the user-run centre: https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/0739445f-0cb1-4dd4-9f20-9a4e4135262a/7d03cd97-68d5-4ccf-8d52-de486af8c9da-bilag-6.pdf ; official user-run description: https://www.thorvaldsen.dk/copy-of-radioti ; contact / Rooster Music phone listing: https://www.thorvaldsen.dk/kontakt ; Kanal 1 archive: https://kanal-1.dk/galebevaegelsen/ .

### Maskine Maskine Amager / MMusik — Amager, Copenhagen, Denmark (archive refinement)
- **Status:** VERIFIED ongoing venue-run concert-video archive; performer/member provenance remains unresolved. CONTEXT / high-value archive route, not yet a participant-made recording candidate.
- **Archive scope:** The official resources page explicitly separates MMANYT (newsletter, 2019–22), MMAtalks (recorded talks from 2022 onward), MMAfilm (the centre's own video-production unit) and MMusik (concert recordings). The MMusik list includes El Ray (4 Sep. 2020, official opening); Nicolai Agerholm performing Tudeprinsen songs (6 Jun. 2023); Tudeprinsen and Love Against The Machine Allstars (2 Feb. 2024); Farmand and A + band (2 Jul. 2024); and a 2025 event. The catalogue establishes dates and named acts but does not establish that these acts are member-created or member-performed.
- **Methodological distinction:** A performance recorded at a user-run venue is not automatically music made by its members. The video archive should be treated as a venue collection until artist biographies, band membership and authorship are established. MMANYT may preserve announcements and member stories that help resolve provenance; MMAfilm may have shorter clips not listed under MMusik.
- **Next:** map each act against the centre's membership/participant records where publicly available; inspect the MMANYT seasons for band origins and event credits; determine whether the 2026 *Virelai* film's musicians are members, invited artists or a mixed ensemble. Do not infer from venue or film title alone. No audio audition or contact made.
- **Sources:** official archive/resources: https://mmamager.dk/resurser ; official about page: https://mmamager.dk/om ; *Virelai* event: https://www.sst.dk/en-af-os/arrangementer/virelai-koncertfilm .

**Urakawa Bethel / Papipupepepo Orchestra:** Japanese-language searches for the exact ensemble name and two reported song titles located the same May 2013 article and the official FY2012 report, but no subsequent release announcement, catalogue number, label listing, physical copy or streaming release. The 2013 article says recording work was underway/completed with 56 participants (38 Bethel members plus staff/supporters), 22 songs planned, and most lyrics/music authored by members; the official report confirms original team-written and composed songs, public performances and a weekly music team. The official page explicitly describes the set as jingles, spots, “chichas” and radio-art created by La Colifata members; the embedded player points to https://soundcloud.com/radio-la-colifata/sets/spots-colifatos . The widget page returned no item-level metadata, so titles, dates, durations, creators and which items qualify as music remain unresolved. ### Netværksstedet Thorvaldsen / Rooster Music — archival depth and possible label-catalogue hits (2026-11-15 follow-up)
- **Major upgrade to archive priority:** a Copenhagen municipal evaluation (prepared for the 2021 budget process; describes the service around 2019–20) gives unusually detailed operational evidence. Thorvaldsen had c.120 members; its professional studio enabled music performance and production, supported by three employees sharing 1.5 full-time posts. Members booked sessions; approximately 15 production sessions/forløb took place per week during nine studio hours on weekdays, with a long waiting list. Members also formed groups in a heavily used rehearsal room. A handful of members produced RadioTi programmes spanning reportage, music and debate, usually broadcast in evenings. This documents a sizeable, sustained participant music-production ecosystem and radio output, not merely an aspiration or occasional workshop. **Status: HIGH PRIORITY / documented corpus likely exists; item-level recordings still not identified.** Source: Copenhagen Municipality, *Notat om bevillinger der udløber i 2020 med fortsat genfinansieringsbehov*, pp. 16–18 (PDF printed pp. 17–19): https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/9f0a4187-0249-41c3-9030-be87548163a2/7b989b43-bb70-4609-a131-4dabf22ecdac-bilag-6.pdf .
- **Earlier institutional history:** 2006 municipal evaluation says music project and RadioTi were among activities attracting most visitors. It identifies Thorvaldsen as a user-run activity centre, founded 1996, and records a 2006 request for funding. Source: https://www.kk.dk/dagsordener-og-referater/Socialudvalget/m%C3%B8de-17052006/dagsorden/punkt-22 .
- **RadioTi timeline:** LAP memorial published in 2021 says RadioTi went on air in August 1993, initially in premises on Frederiksberg, and that the Rooster Musik studio was built soon after the 1996 move into adjoining premises. It describes three professional producers working in the studio. This is a first-person memorial account, not a full technical/archive inventory. Source: https://www.lap.dk/wp-content/uploads/2021/07/LAP_2_2021_web.pdf .
- **RadioTi grant description:** a municipal grant table describes RadioTi as user-run and operated by psychiatric service users and volunteers; it grew from three weekly broadcast hours in 1993 to 33 hours/week at the time of the application. This suggests an important broadcast archive and long-form music programming. Source: https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/9f41d307-07ae-4fbc-b26f-94b866a426bd/7e4cba10-0209-44b8-9aed-4ba0a6038d5b-bilag-1.pdf .
- **Unresolved catalogue collision:** Presto Music currently lists two digital releases under the label name “Rooster Music”: Niels Andersen, *En Blandet Landhandel* (27 Aug. 2014; catalogue no. 484829; 49 min.; 14 tracks); and The Copenhagen Jazzharmonics, self-titled (1 May 2012; catalogue no. 484831; 14 min.). Amazon's listing for “Jazzy Love” by The Copenhagen Jazzharmonics credits ℗© 2011 tunecore / Rooster Music. These are concrete label-distributed audio objects, but no evidence yet links this Rooster Music imprint to Netværksstedet Thorvaldsen. Do not attribute either release to Thorvaldsen or classify the performers as members until the label owner/producer/metadata chain is established. Treat as **unresolved possible catalogue connection / false-positive risk**, not confirmed candidate. Sources: https://www.prestomusic.com/jazz/labels/5225/browse ; https://www.prestomusic.com/jazz/products/8722242--en-blandet-landhandel ; https://music.amazon.it/albums/B015LY9XB6 .
- **Next archive targets:** (1) seek RadioTi programme schedules, station recordings, playlists and surviving tapes; (2) inspect municipal grant applications/evaluations and old Thorvaldsen newsletters for member/artist names, producer credits and releases; (3) investigate the two Rooster Music digital catalogue entries by label owner, ISRC/UPC, distributor and producer, keeping separate from Thorvaldsen until corroborated; (4) search 1993–2006 local-radio archives, Radio 10/Radioator, old frequencies and station documentation; (5) find *Thorvaldsens talrige talenter* concert programmes, tapes and recordings. No contact made, no audio auditioned, no rights cleared.
