### Dragon Café Singers — 2026 follow-up performance
- A Bloomsbury Festival listing advertises the choir's 10 Oct. 2026 performance, featuring tracks from *In This Circle Is My Heart* and a new song titled “Isolation.” This confirms continuing activity and a new original song beyond the 2025 album; it does not yet prove that “Isolation” has been recorded/released. Source: https://bloomsburyfestival.org.uk/whats-on/the-dragon-cafe-singers-from-isolation-to-connection .



### RadioTi / Thorvaldsen — broadcast-archive recovery and Radioator distinction (2026-11-15)
- **RadioTi identification:** the station's current official website identifies it as a non-commercial local radio station broadcasting on 92.9 MHz FM in Greater Copenhagen and online. It says it began in 1993, was created by and for people with psychosocial disabilities (while welcoming others), and offers radio participation, technical work, multimedia, music production and electronic media. Its recent programme types include listener poems, songs and stories, radio drama, sound montages and portraits/interviews. This establishes a direct route to listener-created songs and sound works, but no dated historical audio item or track-level archive was found in this pass. Source: https://radioti.dk/ .
- **Programme-grid clues:** the current schedule includes “Det handler om musikken,” a monthly programme presenting Danish underground musicians; “Sunlight,” featuring music from India from oldies to Punjabi folk, fusion and remixes; and “Radio Uden Smag” / “Radio Guldkorn,” described as music, poetry and guest contributions. These are discovery/broadcast programmes, not evidence that the music was created by RadioTi members. The schedule is current and should not be projected backwards onto the 1990s–2000s. Source: https://radioti.dk/programoversigt/ .
- **Historical frequency / station identity:** an independent frequency directory lists RadioTi at 92.9 FM (Frederiksberg); another directory lists the same. This corroborates a useful historic search identifier, but the directory data are secondary and do not establish dates of transmission or complete frequency history. Sources: https://mobile.sender-tabelle.de/result.php?inputLand=DNK&mode=f ; https://dansk-tv.dk/dkradio/lokalrd2.htm .
- **Municipal description:** Frederiksberg's association guide describes RadioTi as a non-commercial station for people with psychosocial difficulties and others, and explicitly lists music production and electronic communication as possible activities. It gives the 92.9 MHz frequency and online presence. A Copenhagen municipal grant spreadsheet describes RadioTi as 100% volunteer/user-run, with 35 broadcast hours weekly at the time of that application (2023–24). These sources strengthen the scale and member-operated status, but do not demonstrate that all broadcasts or music were participant-created. Sources: https://frivillighed.frederiksberg.dk/media/gnfjxwyr/foreningsoversigt-18.pdf ; https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/1d79c092-9311-45b7-8ce4-61c7cb35a0e4/9f766cd5-f5a2-42ec-bf4f-8a7763662e5e-bilag-1_0.pdf .
- **Radioator — related but distinct small-web archive:** Radioator's own site says it broadcast from RadioTi at 92.9 MHz and 88.9 cable. A profile/interview describes its presenters as a separate youth-radio programme, initially broadcasting on Sokkelund Radio and later moving to RadioTi, where it had a five-hour Saturday slot. The article explicitly says RadioTi originally served people wishing to make programmes about psychiatry, but had opened its doors to others from outside. Therefore Radioator is not automatically a peer-run RadioTi production or a member-created music archive. Its website nevertheless preserves 422 audio files, including 23 jingles and 33 “Musikupdate” clips, alongside interviews, sketches and other radio segments. The “Musikupdate” descriptions identify a DJ presenting forthcoming commercial releases; these are spoken music-news segments, not original music. A jingle page confirms downloadable short audio (one example is 11 seconds). Treat Radioator as an adjacent independent-radio preservation/conduit and a useful source for station history, not a qualifying participant-music candidate on current evidence. Its site states that text, images and sound may not be reproduced without permission. Sources: https://radioator.dk/ ; https://radioator.dk/artikel.php ; https://radioator.dk/lyd.php ; https://radioator.dk/lyd.php?side=J ; https://radioator.dk/lyd.php?side=M .
- **Archival repositories:** the Royal Danish Library says its radio/TV collection includes broadcasts from DR, TV2 and selected periods from other Danish channels; pre-2006 recordings may be on reel-to-reel tape or VHS, and finding a specific programme in the non-digitised holdings requires its broadcast date and time. Its DR-arkiv is for DR broadcasts and is not the RadioTi collection. The library's guides therefore suggest a research route through channel-coverage lists and programme schedules, but no RadioTi accession or holdings claim has yet been found. Sources: https://www.kb.dk/find-materiale/radiotv ; https://www.kb.dk/find-materiale/samlinger/radio-og-tv-samlingen ; https://www.kb.dk/en/find-materials/guides/how-find-danish-radio-and-tv-programmes .
- **Next exact work:** (1) inspect old RadioTi programme grids, station schedules and archived website snapshots for named member-produced programmes and dates; (2) search municipal grant appendices and local-radio directories for RadioTi's broadcast hours, address, frequencies and programme names by year; (3) identify the non-DR channel coverage periods held by the Royal Danish Library and determine whether RadioTi was collected; (4) look for RadioTi tapes in Thorvaldsen / Galebevægelsen / LAP / volunteer collections and local-radio association archives; (5) distinguish member songs and original jingles from licensed music, outside guests and syndicated programmes. No contact made, no recordings auditioned and no reuse permission obtained.


### RadioTi / Rooster Music — primary municipal cross-link and programme-level archive clues (2026-11-15 follow-up)
- **Important correction to the label-collision assessment:** the generic Rooster Music label catalogue remains unlinked to Thorvaldsen, but the *Thorvaldsen Rooster Music studio itself is no longer a merely name-matched possibility*. A Copenhagen municipal grant application record for 2013 lists Lokalradioen Radioti as applicant, 40 participants and 25 volunteers, and explicitly says it had close cooperation with Thorvaldsen and Rooster Music. This directly documents the organisational relationship between the station and studio; it does not identify any particular commercial label release or prove that the two Presto catalogue items belong to the studio. Source: https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/5e774b7c-79d1-4b97-930a-48ff56cdcff8/693a4c11-bde8-414b-96c9-272aea108a39-bilag-2.pdf (printed/searchable lines 962–963; entry 172).
- **Additional first-person founder evidence:** a 4 Aug. 2015 CV by Erik Olsen describes him as co-founder of RadioTi (1992) and Netværkstedet Thorvaldsen (1995), and states that Rooster Music is a music studio where psychiatric service users produce their own music. This is a first-person biographical statement, not an item-level catalogue. It also creates a date discrepancy: the CV says RadioTi co-founded 1992, while RadioTi's own current site and LAP memorial say it began/broadcast in 1993. Preserve both as different formulations (founding vs first broadcast) rather than silently harmonising them. Source: https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/226901a3-c283-48fa-af6e-09397392fe36/9a2201ee-fdc8-46b0-9f1b-36db5851b284-bilag-2.pdf (PDF p. 1; dated 4 Aug. 2015).
- **2019–20 municipal operational detail (additional confirmation):** a Copenhagen municipal evaluation states that the studio supported around 15 production sessions per week over nine weekday hours, had three employees sharing 1.5 FTE, and had a long waiting list; member groups also used the rehearsal room. A handful of members produced RadioTi reportage, music and debate programmes. Source: https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/9f0a4187-0249-41c3-9030-be87548163a2/7b989b43-bb70-4609-a131-4dabf22ecdac-bilag-6.pdf (printed pp. 17–19).
- **2020 municipal application:** a Copenhagen §18 grant overview describes RadioTi as 100% volunteer-run by people with psychosocial difficulties, gives c.40 participants and c.20 volunteers per month, says the project had existed for 27 years, and frames its work as creative community participation. This corroborates the long duration and user-led structure, but is not an audio inventory. Source: https://www.kk.dk/sites/default/files/agenda/f1c9fa66-f579-48dc-bd1c-56a3c859d14b/865cc914-84a0-41be-b382-bbdc4899a3a1-bilag-6.pdf (entry 186).
- **Programme-title discovery list (secondary aggregator, not an official schedule):** Radio.co.dk lists Radio Kiks, Debat med Ivan, RadioNatCachen, Monstermix, Amalies Time, Radio Dunst, Sunlight, Samfundsdebat, Geocacher Radio med Gade og hans gæster, Mandags Salonen, Onkel Oles musikportrætter and Det Lesbiske Litteraturhjørne, plus presenter names. Treat these as search terms only; dates, continuity and participant authorship are unverified. Source: https://radio.co.dk/radioti/ .
- **A useful small-web boundary / dated broadcast lead:** the independent band Venice's 2009 WordPress blog announces a Radio Kiks broadcast on Friday 12 June 2009, 20:00–22:00, on 92.9 FM / 88.9 cable, with an interview and radio premieres of songs from its album *Sober*, possibly including a live version. This is a guest-band promotional broadcast, not evidence of member-made RadioTi music. It provides a precise programme date/time for station-history cross-checking. Source: https://venicesober.wordpress.com/ (posts “Venice i radionen – coming up!” and “Venice i æteren af Morten Kistrup,” May/June 2009).
- **National archive sampling nuance:** the Royal Danish Library says it has collected local-radio and local-TV broadcasts in calendar week 46 each year since 1988, in addition to DR/TV2 and selected other channels. Its current detailed radio-channel coverage table does not list RadioTi. Thus there is a possible annual sample route, but no verified RadioTi capture or accession. The June 2009 Radio Kiks slot is outside week 46, so that particular programme is not covered by the stated local-radio sampling window; do not imply otherwise. Sources: https://www.kb.dk/find-materiale/samlinger/radio-og-tv-samlingen ; channel coverage: https://www.kb.dk/find-materiale/samlinger/radio-og-tv-samlingen/radiokanaler .
- **False-positive check:** a 2017 Danish local-radio application describes “MonsterMix” as electronic club music and a proposed five-hour weekly co-production with “Radio Ti” on Frederiksberg 92.9 MHz, but the applicant is Radio Helsingør and the text is conditional (“if this station also obtains programme permission”). It is not proof of a RadioTi programme or a member-created release. Keep out of the candidate corpus unless a later schedule confirms transmission. Source: https://slks.dk/fileadmin/user_upload/0_SLKS/Dokumenter/Medier/Radio/Ikkekommerciel_lokalradio/Ansoegninger_og_programbeskrivelser_-_2.pdf .
- **Next:** pursue historical RadioTi schedules / Radio Kiks programme dates and the station's own annual accounts/newsletters; check whether the library's week-46 local-radio tapes contain RadioTi in any year; investigate the 2013 municipal file's referenced cooperation with Rooster Music and trace actual Rooster studio output through artist/producer metadata. Keep the two Presto releases unresolved until their imprint is linked by an identifiable producer, address, ISRC or catalogue record. No contact, audio audition or rights clearance made.


### Maskine Maskine Amager / MMusik — individual video records and artist-provenance triage (2026-11-15 follow-up)
- **Archive object status:** the official MMusik section links directly to individually dated MMAfilm concert-video records, not just a general playlist. Individual pages preserve event date, title, venue context and embedded YouTube item. This verifies an extant venue-produced audiovisual corpus. It still does not prove that the performers are members or that the music was composed by them.
- **Potential member/participant artist lead — Tudeprinsen / Fiskprinsen:** official MMusik pages document (a) Nicolai Agerholm performing Tudeprinsen songs at the 6 June 2023 summer party (recorded by Claus Poulsen); (b) Nicolaj Agerholm performing a Tudeprinsen cover at Kulturnatten on 13 Oct. 2023; and (c) Tudeprinsen performing at the centre's fifth-anniversary festival on 2 Feb. 2024. A 2025 Folkemødet event listing names “Fiskprinsen (formerly Tudeprinsen), Maskine Maskine Amager,” suggesting a direct artist/centre association. The event-listing source is secondary; membership, creator identity, songwriting credits and whether “Tudeprinsen” is an individual pseudonym or project remain unresolved. Treat as **HIGH-PRIORITY provenance lead**, not a confirmed participant-made recording. Do not attempt to identify a pseudonymous person beyond public self-attribution. Sources: https://mmamager.dk/archives/930 ; https://mmamager.dk/archives/928 ; https://mmamager.dk/archives/924 ; event lead https://www.paqle.dk/p/per-%C3%B8rum-j%C3%B8rgensen/2451763 (2 May 2025 DDEA/Folkemødet item).
- **Other individual MMusik records:** Love Against The Machine Allstars (2 Feb. 2024 anniversary festival); A + band performing “My Heart Will Go On” (2 July 2024 summer party); Farmand (DK) (2 July 2024 summer party); MMA's 31 Jan. 2025 changeover/farewell celebration; and El Ray at the 4 Sep. 2020 official opening. Official item pages: https://mmamager.dk/archives/926 ; https://mmamager.dk/archives/920 ; https://mmamager.dk/archives/922 ; https://mmamager.dk/archives/906 ; resources page https://mmamager.dk/resurser .
- **Likely visiting/established act boundaries:** El Ray is a Copenhagen surf-rock band with an established public discography dating back to 2010; Farmand is publicly listed as a Copenhagen punk band with external festival/venue appearances. No evidence in this pass links either act to MMA membership. Keep both as visiting-performance recordings/context, not participant-created music. Sources: https://elray.bandcamp.com/album/chasing-ray ; https://www.sunburn.surf/bands/el-ray/ ; https://concertstoattend.com/concerts/byhaven-farmand-pumpehuset-2026-07-14 .
- **Unknowns / ethics:** “A + band” is the venue's own abbreviated credit; do not try to infer or expose the performer behind “A”. The presence of a cover performance (Celine Dion's “My Heart Will Go On”) is not evidence of original composition. “Love Against The Machine Allstars” remains unclassified without performer/author credits. No video was auditioned in this pass; embedded YouTube fetches were throttled, so only the official page metadata and titles are documented.
- **MMANYT as a parallel provenance source:** the centre says its fortnightly newsletter ran from January 2019 into 2022, was compiled into four seasons, and covered events and people at/around the centre. Season 1 (2019) and Season 2 (2020) were also published as books by MMApress; Season 2 includes a gallery of members' work. These issues may identify Tudeprinsen, band members and member-made cultural projects, but the newsletter itself was written by Søren Dixen and also covered material beyond MMA; it is not itself proof of music authorship. Source: https://mmamager.dk/resurser ; bibliographic record for *MMANYT* Sæson 1: https://ugefortegnelser.dbc.dk/DBFU2020-49-bog.pdf .
- **Next:** obtain/inspect the MMANYT seasons or search their full-text contents for Tudeprinsen/Fiskprinsen, Nicolai/Nicolaj Agerholm, Love Against The Machine, and the 2024–25 performers; establish which acts are MMA members and which are guests; identify whether Tudeprinsen/Fiskprinsen has original releases, home recordings or other live tapes beyond the venue videos. Keep anonymous “A” anonymous. No contact, audio audition or rights clearance made.



### Maskine Maskine / MMANYT — bounded full-text search and Tudeprinsen/Fiskprinsen external booking (2026-11-15)
- **MMANYT corpus description confirmed from official source:** it began in January 2019, was issued fortnightly as PDFs, and ran through three complete seasons (2019–21) plus part of Season 4 (2022). The first two seasons were also issued as books by MMApress; Season 2 included a gallery of members' works. The official resource page states that all released issues can be read by clicking the images, but the text-extraction view exposes only the general season descriptions, not the underlying issue contents. A web-index search for Tudeprinsen, Fiskprinsen, Nicolai/Nicolaj Agerholm and Love Against The Machine did not surface full-text matches. Therefore this is a **bounded discovery attempt, not a completed content audit**. Access to the published volumes / individual issue PDFs remains necessary for a true name search. Source: https://mmamager.dk/resurser (MMANYT section); national bibliography gives Season 1 178 pages, 2019, ISBN 978-87-93859-00-5: https://ugefortegnelser.dbc.dk/DBFU2020-49-bog.pdf .
- **Independent public booking:** a 17 Apr. 2025 RUST event listing bills “FARMAND + support: Fiskprinsen + Dj Aske Andersen aka. nyt gulv.” This independently confirms Fiskprinsen as a booked live act outside MMA, but does not prove any studio release or the precise relationship to the centre. A separate DDEA/Folkemødet listing describes “Fiskprinsen (formerly Tudeprinsen), Maskine Maskine Amager.” These sources strengthen the public alias/act trail while leaving membership and authorship questions unresolved. Sources: https://allevents.in/copenhagen/farmand-support-fiskprinsen-dj-aske-andersen-aka-nyt-gulv-rust/200028014331625 ; https://www.paqle.dk/p/per-%C3%B8rum-j%C3%B8rgensen/2451763 (DDEA item, 2 May 2025).
- **Assessment:** Tudeprinsen/Fiskprinsen has multiple venue-produced live recordings and public external bookings; this is not an unknown tape discovery. It remains a provenance lead for possible member-created music, but priority for obscurity is now lower until original compositions, releases or private tapes are found. Do not infer personal identity from aggregator cross-links. Keep the official MMA archive video as venue-held live documentation, not automatically an artist-controlled master.
- **Next:** if obtaining the four MMANYT volumes/issues is practical, inspect the actual contents for artist/member status and recording leads; otherwise close this as a limited null result and move to Dragon Café and further peer-run studios. No audio/video auditioned and no contact made.


### Dragon Café Singers — earlier album and song chronology verified (2026-11-15 follow-up)
- **First album:** Mental Fight Club's annual report for the year ended 30 April 2020 states that The Dragon Café Singers had recorded their first album, *Finding Our Way*, comprising 10 professionally produced songs written and performed by the singers. The report does not give a release date, tracklist, label, catalogue number or recording venue. Do not conflate this first album with the 2025 12-track album, which includes a track also titled “Finding Our Way.” Source: Charity Commission annual report 2019/20, pp. 2–3: https://register-of-charities.charitycommission.gov.uk/charity-search?_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_objectiveId=A10832716&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_mvcRenderCommandName=%2Ffull-print&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_organisationNumber=5052431&p_p_cacheability=cacheLevelPage&p_p_id=uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet&p_p_lifecycle=2&p_p_mode=view&p_p_resource_id=%2Faccounts-resource&p_p_state=maximized .
- **2025 recording sessions:** singing leader Vivien Ellis says the 12-song *In This Circle Is My Heart* was recorded at 9M Studios in Southwark; the album's 12 original songs were collaboratively written between 2016 and 2025. Her track notes establish chronology and collaborative credits: “Finding Our Way” was the first jointly written lyrics/music with café patron Daniel O'Byrne, performed at Shakespeare's Globe Concert for Winter in 2016; “Drive the Cold Winter Away” was written for the 2017 Globe Concert for Winter using a traditional Elizabethan tune; “When Icicles Hang By The Wall” was written/performed at the 2016 Globe concert; “Music to Hear” was a setting of Shakespeare's Sonnet 8 for the Globe's 2018 Sonnet Sunday; “Roots” was written for the 2019 Concert for Winter with Daniel O'Byrne shaping the song and Helen McDonald (Future Groove) as soloist; “Here and Now” was written in 2022 for the Dragon Café's 10th anniversary; “Ballad of The Dragon Café” was written in 2024, with patrons Sionie and Daniel helping to write; and “Southwark Rebel Women” was written/recorded virtually in lockdown in 2021. Source: Vivien Ellis, “New Songs From The Dragon Café,” 24 Sept. 2025: https://www.vivienellis.co.uk/new-songs-from-the-dragon-cafe/ .
- **“Southwark Rebel Women” recording:** Mental Fight Club's 2021 annual review confirms the choir and Vivien Ellis wrote and recorded it in March 2021 with Team London Bridge. Resonance FM's programme notes say songwriter Daniel O'Byrne collaborated with the choir and Ellis; guest soloists included Victoria Couper, Clemmie Franks, Frankie Armstrong, Sally Davies and Gina Foster; Glenn Keiles recorded, arranged, produced and mixed the song. The 2025 album lists it at 7:02. Sources: https://www.mentalfightclub.art/review-of-2021 ; https://resonancefm.com/schedule/2021-03-08 ; https://music.amazon.com.au/albums/B0FGGL6X3F .
- **2023–24 participant narrative song:** Mental Fight Club's annual report for year ended 30 April 2024 describes a second song in a trilogy celebrating the café's 10th anniversary, an epic story of over 100 verses in two acts told from participants' points of view. Vivien Ellis later identifies the 2024 song as “Ballad of The Dragon Café,” says patrons Sionie and Daniel helped write it, and says 100 verses were distilled into the released track. This is valuable participant-authored narrative music, not a clinical or diagnostic artifact. Sources: https://register-of-charities.charitycommission.gov.uk/en/charity-search?_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_objectiveId=A16642357&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_mvcRenderCommandName=%2Ffull-print&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_organisationNumber=5052431&p_p_cacheability=cacheLevelPage&p_p_id=uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet&p_p_lifecycle=2&p_p_mode=view&p_p_resource_id=%2Faccounts-resource&p_p_state=maximized ; https://www.vivienellis.co.uk/new-songs-from-the-dragon-cafe/ .
- **Other solo performance:** the 2024 annual report notes a long-time attendee's self-written musical performance *Windrush: The Story of Cordelia*, about a Jamaican woman arriving on the Empire Windrush in 1948 to find her brother, a former RAF serviceman. No recording is identified in the report; keep as a contextual performance lead pending audio evidence and performer permission.
- **Status refinement:** the Dragon Café corpus is more substantial than a single 2025 release: a 10-song first album was already recorded by April 2020, and the group has a documented original-song repertoire from 2016 onward. Both albums and tracks are public-facing, so not obscure releases; the individual songwriting histories, early album metadata and participant contribution detail remain worth documenting. The 2025 album is recorded at 9M Studios, but no specific audio has been auditioned. Access/streaming does not imply permission to republish. Sources above.


### Dragon Café Singers — first CD narrowed to 2018; surviving track-level stream and 2021 radio broadcast (2026-11-15)
- **Date of first CD recording:** Vivien Ellis's first blog post, dated 27 Dec. 2019, says “last year we recorded a CD of our music”; this places the first *Finding Our Way* CD recording in 2018, consistent with the Charity Commission report for year ended 30 April 2020, which says the first album had been recorded. The 2019 post does not name the CD's release date or tracklist. Treat 2018 as a strongly supported recording year, not an exact session date. Source: https://www.vivienellis.co.uk/category/news/ (27 Dec. 2019 entry); report: https://register-of-charities.charitycommission.gov.uk/charity-search?_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_objectiveId=A10832716&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_mvcRenderCommandName=%2Ffull-print&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_organisationNumber=5052431&p_p_cacheability=cacheLevelPage&p_p_id=uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet&p_p_lifecycle=2&p_p_mode=view&p_p_resource_id=%2Faccounts-resource&p_p_state=maximized .
- **Track-level public stream:** the same 2019 post identifies “Drive the Cold Winter Away” as the first track and links to a SoundCloud stream (`https://soundcloud.com/vivien-keiles/drive-the-cold-winter-away`). Ellis says the song was written for the Globe Concert for Winter in 2017, using the melody of a sixteenth-century ballad with new lyrics about love, food and community and a samba rhythm. This is the clearest accessible item from the early CD, but the SoundCloud page could not be inspected due access restrictions; no listening claim. It is not established whether the same master/version appears on the 2025 album.
- **Radio broadcast of another original:** Mental Fight Club's 2020–21 annual report records a Great Get Together / Southwark Community Festival radio broadcast that included the Dragon Café Singers' “Finding Our Way”; it separately records “Southwark Rebel Women” songwriting and performance on Resonance Radio on 8 March 2021. The annual report does not give the Great Get Together broadcast date, station or programme, so this is a broadcast trace but not yet a locatable archive item. Source: https://register-of-charities.charitycommission.gov.uk/charity-search?_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_objectiveId=A11588171&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_mvcRenderCommandName=%2Ffull-print&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_organisationNumber=5052431&p_p_cacheability=cacheLevelPage&p_p_id=uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet&p_p_lifecycle=2&p_p_mode=view&p_p_resource_id=%2Faccounts-resource&p_p_state=maximized .
- **Assessment:** the first album's 2018 recording year and at least one individual song link are now supported; tracklist and release metadata remain open. The group's original-song archive is unusually well documented through its leader's own notes and charity annual reports, but the commercial/public release profile makes it context rather than obscure release material. Follow-up: locate first CD artwork/tracklist, check whether the SoundCloud item is the first CD master or later upload, and identify the 2021 Great Get Together broadcast station/date. No audio auditioned.


### Park Royal Mental Health Centre / Pine Ward — *Pine* (Finding Rhythms, 2025)
- **Status:** VERIFIED participant-created album / PROMISING. Strong recent inpatient recording candidate; not peer-run in the clubhouse sense, but fits the wider environment-first criterion of music made by residents within an institutional setting. Do not describe it as a therapy album or assume all contributors were inpatients beyond the institutional report.
- **Environment / process:** Pine Ward at Park Royal Mental Health Centre, London, England. Central and North West London NHS Foundation Trust reports that around 15 service users took part in a flexible drop-in music project run with Finding Rhythms during autumn 2024, concluding before Christmas. Participants wrote and composed original songs, worked with professional musicians/producers in a makeshift recording studio on the ward, and all participants feature on the resulting album. A special Christmas track also includes staff contributions. The Trust says every lyric, beat and vocal was created from scratch, sometimes using sounds from the room; the project ended with a listening party on the ward. NHS article dated 12 June 2025: https://www.cnwl.nhs.uk/news/music-project-pine-ward-helps-patients-find-their-voice .
- **Release / audio:** *Pine*, by Finding Rhythms & Park Royal Mental Health Centre, digital album released 17 April 2025 on Bandcamp; 19 tracks, available for streaming/download. Bandcamp explicitly states all tracks were composed and recorded at Park Royal Mental Health Centre by participants on Finding Rhythms' 157th project. Project musicians: Fred Harper and Cleo Savva; mixing/mastering Fred Harper; artwork Liam Horne. This confirms place, authorship framework and production credits but does not give individual participant credits or identify who played/sang each part. Source: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/pine .
- **Tracklist:** “In My Zone” (3:03); “Front Door Key” (1:43); “Grande Voyage” (4:20); “Freedom” (1:36); “Hamlet” (1:29); “It All Happens For Love” (2:21); “Who You Love” (2:40); “Music Of The Future” (2:30); “Come To The Party” (2:49); “The Same Truth” (1:18); “World Peace” (3:48); “Reversing The Pyramid” (2:45); “Win Or Lose” (3:36); “Mitt Liv” (2:00); “Light In The Dark” (3:12); “Without A Doubt (I Can't Wait)” (2:52); “Twigs” (1:08); “Park Royal Christmas” (4:00); “Seasons” (3:34). Bandcamp metadata above.
- **Obscurity / ethics:** PROMISING, not a forgotten historical object: public Bandcamp release and NHS publicity make it discoverable, but it is a recent, unusually well-documented institutional resident-made recording. Preserve contributor anonymity; do not attempt to infer names from ward/staff publicity. Bandcamp lists a “some rights reserved” licence; inspect its exact terms before reuse, and do not assume it covers all contributors' performances/voices or third-party samples. Streaming/download availability is not blanket consent to republish.

### La Colifata — additional official catalogue clarification (2026-11-15)
- Official La Colifata podcast page distinguishes several separate audio corpora: *Rompiendo Muros* (first compilation of 2–4 minute radio microprogrammes edited/produced by the organisation, from 1996–97); *Rompiendo Muros II* (second compilation of microprogrammes); *Viva La Colifata* (20 items of member-generated songs, poetry and improvisations mixed by Manu Chao with his own musical bases); and *Spot Colifatos* (member-created jingles, spots, “chichas” and radio-art). The latter is the strongest discrete short-form music/sound-art archive; its SoundCloud embed is linked from the official page, but no item-level metadata was visible in this pass. Source: https://lacolifata.com.ar/podcasts/ .
- **Important false-positive correction:** the same official page explains that *Siempre fui loco* is a different compilation: Manu Chao assembled music by 20 Barcelona street bands with La Colifata radio microprogramme audio; around 3,000 copies were sold by the street musicians. Do not describe that release as an album of songs made by hospital residents. The official *Viva La Colifata* page instead describes the member-generated audio/music collaboration. Source: https://lacolifata.com.ar/podcasts/ .
- **Date discrepancy:** La Colifata's current *Viva La Colifata* site says the two volumes were edited in 2005; contemporary 2009 press coverage says the album was launched in July 2009 and that recording/workshops began in 2007. Preserve the discrepancy rather than silently selecting one date. Sources: https://www.vivalacolifata.org/ ; https://www.elterritorio.com.ar/noticias/2009/07/30/199152-manu-chao-lanzo-viva-la-colifata ; https://www.theguardian.com/music/2009/aug/28/manu-chao-psychiatric-patients .
- **Editorial status:** La Colifata is a prominent, internationally documented project, so its better-known Manu Chao collaboration is CONTEXT ONLY for obscurity. The member-created radio-art/jingles and microprogramme archives remain PROMISING item-level discovery targets. Distinguish member-created speech/music from visiting musicians' backing tracks and commercial compilation material. No audio auditioned in this pass.



### Finding Rhythms — cross-project participant-recording catalogue (2023–2026; institutional and community environments)
- **Archive significance:** Finding Rhythms' Bandcamp catalogue is an unusually rich, searchable corpus of participant-composed/recorded music across prisons, acute mental-health wards, approved premises, community groups and post-release studio work. Its 2025 annual report says it worked with 199 people in 2025; delivered 25 group projects (14 custodial, 11 community) involving 151 participants; and produced 22 original-music albums. Its Peckham studio additionally hosted 48 participants for 351 one-to-one sessions. This is a discovery map, not one environment or one homogeneous collection. Sources: https://www.finding-rhythms.co.uk/news/making-music-building-futures-our-2025-annual-report ; Bandcamp catalogue https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/ .
- **Harrow / Northwick Park mental-health projects:** a peer-reviewed 2026 study and Finding Rhythms' summary identify three projects from 2023–24, involving 27 Black men experiencing repeated contact with mental-health services, resulting in three original albums shared with their communities: *Hotter Than Water*, *Every Effort Counts* and *Creative Energy*. Do not collapse participants into a diagnosis or infer that every contributor was an inpatient; the work was collaborative with NHS psychologists and professional musicians. Sources: https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/capr.70139 ; https://www.finding-rhythms.co.uk/news/finding-rhythms-research-highlights-the-impact-of-music-projects-for-black-men-experiencing-mental-health-difficulties .
  - *Hotter Than Water* — Finding Rhythms and Northwick Park Hospital, 15 tracks; released 14 Dec. 2023. Bandcamp says tracks were recorded with Finding Rhythms musicians at Northwick Park on project 134. Project musicians Aron Kyne and Marcelle; mix/master Aron Kyne. Tracklist: “Our Story,” “All I Need,” “Cool Cool Cat (Interlude),” “Hotter Than Water,” “Bonnie and Clyde,” “Bread Without Butter,” “Light In The Dark,” “Did I Sleep Last Night,” “I'll Be Back (Interlude),” “Crossroads,” “Drugs I'm Abusing,” “Ain't Living For Yesterday,” “Heaven Scent,” “Hey Ho!,” “Our Story (Outro).” Source: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/hotter-than-water .
  - *Every Effort Counts* — Finding Rhythms and Northwick Park Hospital, nine tracks; released 10 May 2024. Bandcamp says composed and recorded at Northwick Park by participants on project 143; music practitioners Marcelle and Aron Kyne; mix/master Aron Kyne. Tracklist: “Thru the Valley,” “Shady,” “Running,” “Bounty,” “Our Future,” “Salvation,” “Drop in the Ocean,” “Small Blue Dot,” “Every Tear Dries.” Free download. Source: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/every-effort-counts .
  - *Creative Energy* — Finding Rhythms and Northwick Park Hospital, ten tracks; released 15 Oct. 2024. Bandcamp says recorded by participants at Northwick Park on project 153; project musicians Marcelle and Aron Kyne; mix/master Aron Kyne. Tracklist: “Touchdown,” “Still Here,” “Strategise,” “More Blessings,” “Radiant,” “Stuck in the Game,” “Celestial Endeavour,” “Hoshy's Vision,” “Foundation Freestyle,” “Riots.” Source: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/creative-energy .
- **Other adjacent community/custodial album leads verified in Bandcamp catalogue:** *Timeless* and *Timeless II* (Finding Rhythms & YMCA Landaid House; 2024 and 2025; participant-recorded at the residential project); *W11* (Rugby Portobello Trust, 2024; 15 participant-recorded tracks); *Listen To Our Stories* (HMP Bristol, 2025; participants composed and recorded all 11 tracks); *Pine* (Park Royal Mental Health Centre, 2025; detailed above); and 2026 releases from Westbourne House Approved Premises, HMP Woodhill, HMP High Down and other custodial settings. These are publicly released, so generally CONTEXT / PROMISING rather than obscure releases. Exact titles and dates should be confirmed per Bandcamp page before separate candidate entries. Sources: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/timeless ; https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/timeless-ii ; https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/w11 ; https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/listen-to-our-stories ; https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/ .
- **Editorial assessment:** treat Finding Rhythms as a research conduit and collection-level archive, not as one outsider artist. The organisation's catalogue includes prisons and community projects with varying degrees of participant composition, performance, recording and professional input. Use each Bandcamp album's own credits to distinguish “composed by participants,” “recorded by participants,” and “recorded with Finding Rhythms musicians.” A publicly accessible download and “some rights reserved” licence do not automatically establish individual consent or rights for every participant voice/performance. Do not republish without checking exact licence and rights-holder terms.



### Finding Rhythms — additional residential / custodial album records (follow-up, 2026-11-15)
- **YMCA LandAid House, London — *Timeless* and *Timeless II*.** LandAid House is a YMCA accommodation project opened in May 2021 for young people experiencing homelessness, with 146 ensuite rooms/bedspaces and support toward independence; it is not a psychiatric institution or clubhouse. YMCA's own page: https://www.ymcalondoncan.org/our-services/accommodation/landaid-house/ . Bandcamp says all tracks on *Timeless* (11 tracks, released 19 Nov. 2024) and *Timeless II* (12 tracks, released 10 Sept. 2025) were recorded by participants at YMCA LandAid House on Finding Rhythms projects 152 and 165 respectively. Project musicians Tom Goss (with Aron Kyne on *Timeless*); Tom Goss on *Timeless II*. Sources: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/timeless ; https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/timeless-ii . These are participant-recorded releases, but the Bandcamp credits do not state that every track was composed by participants. PROMISING contextual recordings; not obscure releases.
- **HMP Bristol — *Listen To Our Stories*.** 11-track digital album released 17 Apr. 2025. Bandcamp explicitly says all tracks were composed and recorded at HMP Bristol by participants on Finding Rhythms' 164th project, delivered with Change Grow Live. Project musicians Aron Kyne and Thabo Mkwananzi; mix/master Aron Kyne. Source: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/listen-to-our-stories . This is a VERIFIED participant-composed and recorded prison album; public Bandcamp availability makes it a documented modern example rather than an obscure master.
- **Westbourne House Approved Premises, Forest Gate, London — *Our Brighter Future*.** 11-track digital album released 7 Oct. 2026. Bandcamp says all tracks were composed and recorded at Westbourne House AP by participants on Finding Rhythms' 207th project; musician/producer Nathan Smoker. Finding Rhythms' project account says four participants took part across six days / 24 hours of activity in July–August 2026 and created the 11-track original album. Westbourne House is an Approved Premises: a temporary supervised residential placement in the community for people on probation after release from prison, not a prison or hospital. Sources: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/our-brighter-future ; https://www.finding-rhythms.co.uk/news/our-brighter-future-finding-connection-through-music-at-westbourne-house ; HM Inspectorate of Probation background: https://hmiprobation.justiceinspectorates.gov.uk/news/westbourne-house-approved-premises-in-london-rated-good-following-inspection/ . This is a recent, small-cohort participant-composed recording in a transitional probation environment; PROMISING but already publicly released.
- **Rugby Portobello Trust — *W11*.** 15-track digital album released 10 Dec. 2024; Bandcamp says all tracks were recorded by participants at the Trust on Finding Rhythms' 144th project. Project musicians Dre Hall, Dwayne Kilvington and Cleo Savva; mixing/mastering Dre Hall and Dwayne Kilvington. Source: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/w11 . Participant recording is verified; participant composition is not explicitly claimed by the album page. Keep as adjacent community/youth-service lead, not a psychiatric or custodial environment.




## 36. Chilli Studios — Newcastle upon Tyne, England; member recording studio, documentary soundtrack and Chilli Radio

- **Status:** PROMISING environment / partial recording evidence; no discrete, fully catalogued member EP or radio track yet identified. Strong primary route because this is a member-oriented creative studio with music facilities, podcasts and documented member recording.
- **Environment:** Chilli Studios (Newcastle and Gateshead Arts Studios Ltd) is a charity founded in 2004, serving members who experience or are at risk of mental-health problems and other forms of social exclusion. Its current site describes music among its creative activities, a community studio and member participation. The official Projects and Resources page explicitly says **Chilli Radio** hosts podcasts containing readings, insights and music from members. Sources: https://www.chillistudios.co.uk/ ; https://www.chillistudios.co.uk/projects-and-resources ; https://www.chillistudios.co.uk/about .
- **Member-made documentary soundtrack:** *Apples Is Apples* (2014), a 69-minute feature documentary directed by Andrew Wilson, was a year-long collaborative project involving Chilli Studios members, staff and the artist/director. Wilson's project page describes the film as having a compelling original soundtrack recorded by members themselves; the Vimeo trailer description independently says the soundtrack was recorded by studio members. World premiere: Star and Shadow Cinema, Newcastle, 18 June 2014. This is verified member-created audio within a film, but no separate soundtrack release or tracklist has been located. Sources: https://www.iamandrewwilson.co.uk/apples-is-apples ; https://vimeo.com/420370579 .
- **Individual member recording practice:** Andrew Fletcher's Northumbria University doctoral thesis reports that one studio member recorded and released her own EP about experiences of depression and alienation; another used the facilities to record songs on an ongoing basis; and a further participant was developing an album project with other members. It also discusses a member using Chilli's studio extensively for recordings and public performances. The thesis anonymises these individuals; preserve that anonymity and do not cross-match them to named artists or public biographies. Source: https://nrl.northumbria.ac.uk/36253/1/fletcher.andrew_phd.pdf .
- **Chilli Radio:** the current official site confirms a member music/readings podcast strand, but the public Projects and Resources page does not expose a working archive URL, item titles, dates, durations or contributors. Search-engine retrieval did not surface an itemised Chilli Radio feed in this pass. Treat this as an unresolved archive route, not a verified tracklist.
- **Found Sounds ’26 — new collaborative cassette lead:** AFSC (Audio Friendly Sound Collective) describes a 2026 project with Chilli Studios (lead partner), Sound Rooms (Gateshead), Ouseburn Radio (Newcastle) and SVP. It is intended for people with lived experience of severe mental illness, with field recording, up to six one-to-one beginner electronic-production sessions, and finished original pieces. The project page says the music will be broadcast on Ouseburn Radio and issued on a short-run tape, with performance optional. It lists open days on 25 Feb., 4 Mar. and 11 Mar. 2026 and says recruitment was underway. As of this research pass, no completed tape, broadcast or finished track was located; the page may be a stale recruitment notice. Do not state that a tape has been issued. Source: https://afsoundcollective.org/foundsounds26/ .
- **Obscurity / next steps:** Chilli Studios is a useful environment and archive lead; the 2014 film soundtrack, anonymised member EP and Chilli Radio archive warrant follow-up. Found Sounds '26 could yield a genuinely small-run tape, but only after publication/broadcast is confirmed. Seek official podcast feeds and member-authorised catalogue metadata; do not infer identities from the thesis. No audio auditioned, no contact made, no permissions obtained.




### London Pathways Partnership — post-release forensic mental-health hubs / participant albums (2025–2026)
- **Status:** VERIFIED participant-composed and recorded album pair / PROMISING. This is a distinct transition environment: community hubs serving people leaving prison through a specialist forensic mental-health partnership, not a prison wing or a general psychiatric inpatient unit.
- **Setting and process:** Finding Rhythms reports two projects with London Pathways Partnership (LPP), which provides specialist forensic mental-health services to people leaving prison. One took place at the London Bridge hub in autumn 2025 and the second at Friends House, Euston, in Jan.–Feb. 2026. Ten participants took part across 12 sessions (six per project), led by professional musician Nathan Smoker. London Bridge: four participants, six sessions / 18 contact hours, one 11-track album and a listening event for 25 guests. Euston: six participants, six sessions / 18 contact hours, one 14-track album and a listening event for 25 guests. Participants collaborated on music, artwork concepts and creative direction. Source: https://www.finding-rhythms.co.uk/news/finding-purpose-through-music-london-pathways-partnership-in-london-bridge-and-euston (8 May 2026).
- **London Bridge album — *Nothing's Undone*:** released 2 Apr. 2026; 11 tracks; Bandcamp says all tracks were composed and recorded at London Pathways Partnership by participants on Finding Rhythms' 186th project. Project musician Nathan Smoker; mixing/mastering Nathan Smoker; artwork Liam Horne. Tracklist: “Just Like That” (1:57), “Say What You Want” (3:49), “Last Night” (1:55), “Keep Your Head Up” (2:27), “Out For Blood” (2:07), “No Commitment” (2:36), “Hills” (2:00), “Valentine” (3:04), “Nothing's Undone” (2:22), “Phone Life” (2:54), “Finding Rhythms” (2:09). Source: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/nothings-undone .
- **Euston album — *Higher Power*:** released 23 Apr. 2026; 14 tracks; Bandcamp says all tracks were composed and recorded at London Pathways Partnership by participants on Finding Rhythms' 188th project. Project musician and mix/master Nathan Smoker; artwork Andrea Phillips. Tracklist: “Shallow Streets” (2:06), “Everyone, Everywhere” (2:39), “Half Way” (1:54), “Pottery” (3:11), “Straight & Narrow” (2:21), “Not Looking Back” (2:32), “In It Together” (1:00), “Controlled” (2:28), “Skeleton People” (2:22), “Going Forward” (3:57), “Yesterday” (3:30), “Becomes Real” (3:08), “The Essence Of Melanin Mystical Journey” (4:36), “Tight & Focused” (2:29). Source: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/higher-power .
- **LPP context:** LPP is a consortium of five NHS trusts co-delivering the Offender Personality Disorder pathway with probation and prison partners. Its community service includes hubs and support for people on probation, and the music programme is explicitly intended to support people transitioning from prison to community. Sources: Finding Rhythms project account above; NHS / LPP role overview https://www.cnwl.nhs.uk/health-and-justice/our-service-lines ; job profile describing LPP as five-trust partnership and its community hubs https://docs.trac.jobs/docs-vacancy/7924214/10202186/SW%20ICPS%20B4%20Team%20Admin%20JD.pdf .
- **Assessment:** These two albums are unusually well documented participant-authored works in the often-overlooked post-release period. Public Bandcamp release means not hidden releases, but the specific setting, small cohorts, track credits and transition context have clear discovery value. Preserve anonymity; no participant names supplied. No audio auditioned, no contact made, no reuse permission obtained.

Treat these as title-level leads until each album page is opened and its precise participant authorship/setting checked. Catalogue: https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/ . Avoid implying all projects are inpatient or that the artist names are the participants; the project musicians listed are professional facilitators/producers.

### Chilli Studios, Newcastle — studio infrastructure confirmed; participant recording catalogue not yet identified
- Chilli Studios (Newcastle and Gateshead Arts Studio Ltd; charity 1116957) is a member-centred creative space founded in 2004. Its current site describes music alongside visual arts and multimedia; project page lists **Chilli Radio**, a podcast stream with readings, insights and music from members, and a **Heads & Tales** mental-health heritage archive led and shaped by participants, volunteers and staff. Sources: https://www.chillistudios.co.uk/about ; https://www.chillistudios.co.uk/projects-and-resources .
- A 2018 PhD thesis by Andrew Fletcher describes a small music studio with instruments and recording equipment, professional musicians offering support, two guided sessions weekly focused on songwriting or jamming, member-led activities, and personal songs/ideas that could be developed into semi-professional recordings. This is useful independent historical description, not proof of a particular surviving release. Source: https://nrl.northumbria.ac.uk/36253/1/fletcher.andrew_phd.pdf .
- Charity Commission 2020/21 report documents music recording and technical support in 898 one-to-one support sessions; online events included live music, and a SoundCloud podcast received 1,008 listens. Source: https://register-of-charities.charitycommission.gov.uk/en/charity-search?_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_objectiveId=A11492216&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_mvcRenderCommandName=%2Ffull-print&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_organisationNumber=4020661 .
- **Found Sounds '26** is a current collaborative project led by Chilli Studios with AF Sound Collective, Sound Rooms Gateshead, Ouseburn Radio and SVP Newcastle. It invites participants with experience of severe mental illness to field-record local sounds, learn electronic production, and create complete original music. The project page says finished music is to be broadcast on Ouseburn Radio and issued as a short-run cassette, with optional performance; it advertises recruitment and 2026 open days. This is a concrete forthcoming output pipeline, but the page alone does not prove the tape has been released or provide titles/participants. Source: https://afsoundcollective.org/foundsounds26/ .
- **Important false-positive:** Simon Taylor's *Survival E.P* credits acoustic bass recorded at Chilli Studios by Ziad Jabero, but the Bandcamp page says all music/lyrics were written, arranged, performed, recorded and mixed by Taylor. This is a solo singer-songwriter's own release with one session contribution at the venue, not evidence of a Chilli member-created album. Source: https://simontaylor11.bandcamp.com/album/survival-e-p .
- **Assessment:** PROMISING recording environment and archive route; no discrete Chilli member-authored album or item-level Chilli Radio track has yet been verified. Next: inspect Chilli Radio's SoundCloud/podcast items, identify whether Found Sounds '26 has delivered the cassette/broadcast, and look for member-authored works in Heads & Tales. Do not conflate professional guest artists, staff-supported sessions, visiting musicians or outside artists who merely recorded at the venue with member-created music.



### Chilli Studios — first explicit album trace in 2015–16 annual report (new archival lead, 2026-11-15)
- Chilli Studios' own annual report for the year ended 31 March 2016 explicitly lists **“1 studio album”** among the organisation's achievements, alongside “Chilli Radio: numerous members podcasts.” The report does not name the album, format, participants, tracklist, producer or release date; it does not establish whether the album was member-composed, a compilation, or a staff/guest project. This is a much stronger archive lead than the generic existence of a studio: a discrete album existed by the 2015–16 reporting year and member podcasts were already being produced. Source: Charity Commission report, lines 339–379: https://register-of-charities.charitycommission.gov.uk/sector-data/top-10-charities?_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_objectiveId=A8148248&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_mvcRenderCommandName=%2Ffull-print&_uk_gov_ccew_onereg_charitydetails_web_portlet_CharityDetailsPortlet_priv_r_p_organisationNumber=4020661 .
- The same report records 172 regular individual member participations and 4,269 workshop participations during the year, and says members could work independently in the studio or join weekly structured workshops, including music. This contextualises the album but does not prove its participants or authorship. Source: same report, lines 449–515.
- **Next archival target:** retrieve Chilli Studios Annual Report 2015–16 and the contemporaneous Chilli Seed programme material from Issuu; search for album title/format, music session leaders, artist credits, member consent/rights, and Chilli Radio podcast names. Ask only through public-facing archive/contact channels if documentary search fails. Do not assume the album is the later *Survival E.P* or any other outside artist's work.
- **Assessment:** Chilli Studios moves from general PROMISING studio infrastructure to a **documented but unidentified album** (HIGH PRIORITY provenance chase). The album's existence is verified; member authorship and content are not.
