Project musician / mix and master: Tom Wright-Goss. Source https://findingrhythms.bandcamp.com/album/a-p-living-2 .
- **Editorial note:** The project numbers are not necessarily chronological in release order: project 183 (*Timeless III*) released after project 187 (*A.P Living 2*), and project 188 (*Higher Power*) released after project 186 (*Nothing's Undone*). Preserve the page's own project number and release date independently. These are public, relatively discoverable Bandcamp releases, useful as evidence of varied participant recording environments rather than obscure recordings.


### La Colifata — *Rompiendo Muros* physical CD and item-level catalogue boundary (2026-11-15)
- A 12 Sept. 2017 collector/archive blog post documents a physical CD titled **Rompiendo Muros**, describes it as a 22-track compilation of “Micros Colifatos,” each approximately 2–4 minutes, edited and produced by La Colifata Salud Mental y Comunicación. The post includes photographs/descriptions of the front, inside and handmade cardboard packaging; the insert identifies a “taller de microemprendimiento colifato.” This is useful independent physical-object evidence, but not an item-level tracklist or a primary label catalogue. Source https://radioenpapel.blogspot.com/2017/09/fm-1001-radio-la-colifata-rompiendo.html .
- La Colifata's official podcast catalogue independently identifies **Rompiendo Muros: Recopilación 1996–1997** as the first compilation of its microprogrammes and **Rompiendo Muros II** as the second. The official description says the microprogrammes were recorded from discussions in the Borda Hospital gardens, edited into 2–4-minute pieces and retransmitted by radio stations. The content is principally spoken radio production on health, religion, politics and other subjects; do not classify the entire compilation as music. Source https://lacolifata.com.ar/podcasts/ .
- The same official page defines **Spot Colifatos** as jingles, spots, “chichas” and radio-art pieces produced and created by Colifata members. It provides a SoundCloud embed/set link, but the player returned no item-level metadata through the accessible page. The SoundCloud destination itself was not accessible to the browser, so track names, dates, creators, durations and music-vs-speech distinctions remain unknown. Official source https://lacolifata.com.ar/podcasts/ ; embed destination https://soundcloud.com/radio-la-colifata/sets/spots-colifatos .
- The official page describes **Viva La Colifata** as 20 pieces of songs, poetry and improvisations generated by radio members and mixed by Manu Chao with his own musical bases. This is a hybrid member-voice / guest-backing production, not an unqualified collection of member-only compositions. Official page https://lacolifata.com.ar/podcasts/ . A separate official campaign page describes two 2005 volumes and allows free listening/download, but the precise relationship between “20 songs” and the two-volume packaging should be checked against the actual discs before asserting a per-volume track count: https://www.vivalacolifata.org/en/index.html .
- **Assessment:** *Rompiendo Muros* is a verified physical radio-production object, but not necessarily a music recording; *Spot Colifatos* is a stronger music/sound-art candidate archive, pending item-level metadata. No audio was auditioned, and no rights for reuse were established.


### Chilli Studios / Found Sounds '26 — archive retrieval outcome (2026-11-15)
- The 2015–16 Chilli Studios annual report and Chilli Seed programme are bibliographically identified in a 2022 academic article, which cites the original Issuu URLs: https://issuu.com/chillistudios/docs/cs_annual_report_1516 and https://issuu.com/chillistudios/docs/the_seed_programme_explained . Both Issuu URLs were inaccessible in this research pass. The Charity Commission copy of the annual report remains the accessible primary-source evidence for “1 studio album” and “numerous members podcasts”; album identity/authorship still unknown. The 2016 Chilli Seed programme is a business start-up model, not evidence of a music release.
- Chilli Studios' current official projects page still describes Chilli Radio as podcasts containing readings, insights and music from members, but its “Find out more” link resolves to an image asset rather than a playable archive/catalogue. No dated podcast item, track title, creator or audio catalogue was verified. Source https://www.chillistudios.co.uk/projects-and-resources .
- Found Sounds '26 project page still describes the intended output as participant-created music mixed/mastered for Ouseburn Radio broadcast and a short-run cassette, and lists recruitment/open days in February–March 2026. As of this research pass, no completed cassette, programme date, broadcast archive or release announcement was found. Treat the cassette/broadcast as **planned, not verified delivered**. Source https://afsoundcollective.org/foundsounds26/ .
- Do not conflate independent singer-songwriter Simon Taylor's releases with Chilli member recordings. His credits explicitly identify him as writer, performer and recorder; Ziad Jabero/Chilli Studios provided production, mixing or isolated session contributions. Examples: https://simontaylor11.bandcamp.com/album/survival-e-p ; https://simontaylor11.bandcamp.com/album/karma-e-p ; https://simontaylor11.bandcamp.com/track/postcard-from-paradise .
- **Assessment:** Chilli's unidentified 2015–16 studio album remains a HIGH PRIORITY provenance chase; Chilli Radio is a documented but currently unindexed member-audio source; Found Sounds '26 remains a planned-output lead pending a release/broadcast record. No audio auditioned, no contact made, no restricted archive accessed, and no reuse permission obtained.



### Outsider Music / Outsider Music Live — London peer-created songs and member releases (new Phase 13 lead, 2026-11-15)
- **Organisation / environment:** Outsider Music CIC, a London-based music-therapy and creative-music organisation working with NHS and community partners. Its work includes songwriting, studio recording, film and live performance. The organisation's website explicitly distinguishes project music created by participants from a separate, still-planned talent-development project for an EP/album. Source https://www.outsidermusic.org.uk/music ; organisational context https://www.outsidermusic.org.uk/ .
- **Outsider Music Live** is described by the organisation as a self-organised peer group formed by people who had participated in earlier Outsider Music projects and wanted to continue making music beyond funded sessions or formal care. The group meets weekly at Higher Potential in Brent studios and has a monthly residency at The Antwerp Arms, Tottenham. The page links member biographies and individual Bandcamp pages. Source https://www.outsidermusic.org.uk/live .
- **Mary G — *The Wandering Star***: nine-track digital album, released 3 Aug. 2023. Tracklist: “Wandering Star,” “I'm a survivor,” “Lonely Girl,” “Shades of grey,” “It all falls on deaf ears,” “Mother Nature,” “Now,” “Resilience,” “Hallelujah.” Album credit thanks Jon Hall for production/support and specifically says this enabled Mary to write and perform her own songs. This is unusually clear primary artist testimony of member-authored, member-performed work with facilitator support. Bandcamp lists all rights reserved. Source https://maryg1.bandcamp.com/album/the-wandering-star .
- **Trav Man — *Rise Together***: nine-track digital album, released 14 Aug. 2023. Tracklist: “Spaced Out,” “All You Need Is Love - Feat. Headway North London,” “Put Your Love In My Life,” “Rise Up,” “The Pleasures of SLI,” “World Pull Together,” “A Brother's Love,” “All Lives Matter,” “Black Lives Matter - Reprise.” Credits thank Jon Hall as producer, Lisa, Mary, Lin and Cathleen as backing singers, Toby, Paul and Nick as musicians, and Dwayne at Higher Potential Studios. This is a mixed collaborative production, not simply a solo inmate/member tape; identify participant vs professional contributions track by track if ever featured. Bandcamp lists all rights reserved. Source https://travlinman.bandcamp.com/album/rise-together .
- **Victory Vibes / Victoria Lamb — *Hello***: 17-track album, released 3 July 2023, available digitally and on CD. Artist's Bandcamp description says the songs were written and recorded between 2014 and 2023, entirely by the artist using GarageBand; the Outsider Music Live profile links to the album and identifies the artist as a member. Tracklist and durations are on the Bandcamp page. This is an individual member's own home-recorded work, not an Outsider Music studio project; preserve that distinction. Bandcamp licence all rights reserved. Source https://victoryvibes2.bandcamp.com/album/hello ; group profile https://www.outsidermusic.org.uk/live .
- **Lisa / lisaneos**: Outsider Music Live profile describes her own songwriting and singing and links https://lisaneos.bandcamp.com/ ; the Bandcamp domain redirected to an inaccessible signup page during this pass. No release title/metadata verified.
- The organisation's general music page lists six original-song examples — “Banger,” “Lonely World,” “Cry,” “Baltimore,” “Dominic,” and “Before the skies turn black” — as songs written by individuals themselves or created during music programmes. Page's embedded players did not expose artist credits or recording dates, so retain as an unattributed song list, not as six verified album tracks. Source https://www.outsidermusic.org.uk/music .
- **Assessment:** HIGH PRIORITY as a well-documented peer-led production environment and source of identifiable original recordings; individual releases are publicly discoverable and therefore not highly obscure in themselves. The most compelling record is Mary G's explicit statement that she wrote and performed her own album, alongside Outsider Music Live's self-organised peer-group model. Do not publish sensitive biography details from member pages beyond what is needed for provenance; individual members chose to publish some personal context, but the compendium need not reproduce it. Check exact consent/rights before republication: the Bandcamp releases say all rights reserved. No audio auditioned.
- **Research distinction:** the group name “Outsider Music Live” refers to the peer collective; “Outsider Music CIC” is the facilitating organisation; “Outsider Music Originals” is a separately linked project/site. Do not conflate all participants or projects.

### Om Music Sanctuary — Edinburgh peer-led recording studio (new environment lead, 2026-11-15)
- Founded by Ralph Turner in 2016, Om Music Sanctuary describes itself as a community project for people experiencing isolation due to mental-health or other challenges. It offers songwriting, open jams, instrument tuition, electronic music production and bookable recording sessions where members can record their own music with volunteer help. Following the COVID closure, it reopened in early 2022 at Palmerston Place Church with a teaching facility and recording studio. Official sources: https://ommusicsanctuary.com/ ; https://ommusicsanctuary.com/about-the-project .
- The current official team page documents volunteers who support member recording and original composition: some run collaborative songwriting and recording, some produce member tracks, and others supervise jamming/recording. Source https://ommusicsanctuary.com/our-team .
- An Edinburgh Council partnership paper (17 March 2020, agenda item 7.12) describes the project as peer-inspired, peer-led, peer-managed and peer-supportive; documents its earlier studio at the founder's home, then dedicated space at Stafford Centre, rehearsal and recording facilities, free lessons and a peer open-mic event. Source https://www.edinburgh.gov.uk/downloads/file/27061/2020-papers-17-march-2020 .
- A 2020–21 annual report says the charity's purpose included providing recording and performance opportunities, but pandemic closure and staffing/volunteer strain led to transfer of property and resources to Support in Mind Scotland. This is a historical interruption, not evidence the current project ended; current website says reopened in 2022 and has regular sessions/events. Source https://oscr-docs.s3.eu-west-2.amazonaws.com/signed-om-annual-accounts-2020-21-redacted-sc047500-1-bc1fd58a-1608-ed11-82e5-000d3a875ce3.pdf .
- Current 2026 programme includes an Electric Café, studio band and monthly Black Medicine Show open mic for original music. No member-made release, identifiable member track or public recording catalogue was found in this pass. Sources https://ommusicsanctuary.com/whats-on ; https://ommusicsanctuary.com/ .
- **Assessment:** PROMISING / archive hunt only. The infrastructure and recording activity are verified, but no specific member-made recording is yet identified. Do not count visiting open-mic performers or the volunteer studio band's public performances as member-composed music without item-level credits. No audio auditioned and no contact made.


### Waupun / Upheaval — expanded source audit and exact media leads (2026-11-15)

The existing Upheaval entry is now supported by a more exact audiovisual and discographic trail:

- **1975 archive video:** the Wisconsin Historical Society's *People's Video - Upheaval* is a half-inch open-reel video, filmed by People's Video at the then Wisconsin State Prison in Waupun in 1975. It documents the prison soul band Upheaval rehearsing and performing “Love Station”; AMIA's 2024 Archival Screening Night programme says the item was digitised in 2022 by UW-Madison Department of Communication Arts. Max Wilson's 2022 investigation embeds two WHS Aviary players (media IDs 155112 and 155113). First embed: https://whs.aviaryplatform.com/embed/media/155112?embed=true&media_player=true ; second: https://whs.aviaryplatform.com/embed/media/155113?embed=true&media_player=true . The web reader hit an Aviary redirect loop; these are verified embed URLs from the article, but direct browser playability still needs checking. The article associates “Broken Down Porch,” “Ready Train,” “Happy Hooker” and “Love Station” with the footage; exact reel segmentation remains unverified.
- **Original single:** *Paradise Lost* / *Now That You're Gone*, recorded 26 Aug 1974 according to Wilson, is the band's only original vinyl release. Official Now-Again Bandcamp offers “Paradise Lost” as a streaming/digital-download track on *Tickets For Doomsday*: https://nowagainrecords.bandcamp.com/track/paradise-lost . Symphonical Records' official compilation *The Key To Our Love Vol. 3* includes “Now That You've Gone” (5:45): https://symphonicalrecords.bandcamp.com/album/the-key-to-our-love-vol-3 . Preserve title spelling variants in the source record.
- **Previously unreleased Waupun recordings:** Symphonical's 2016 single *Come On with the Come On* / *T.L.C.* contains two recordings made inside Waupun, catalogued as SYM001 / SR001. Newtone's product page identifies the tracks (3:18 and 3:05), says they were previously unreleased and that surviving masters were cassette copies: https://www.newtone-records.com/product/9053925 . Qobuz provides a second digital listing and track credits: https://www.qobuz.com/nl-nl/album/come-on-with-the-come-on-tlc-upheaval/n0w3b9oyu4c0a . Newtone says recordings were made in 1979; Wilson's 2022 article caption says circa 1974. Preserve the date discrepancy. Official Symphonical SoundCloud catalogue: https://soundcloud.com/symphonical-records/tracks ; individual track URLs not independently confirmed in this pass.
- **Additional reported but unverified corpus:** Joe Hayes reportedly had a CD containing “Vampire Lady” and other Upheaval tracks. LaVelle Rudd reportedly left reel-to-reel and music tapes to his girlfriend; whereabouts unknown. Wilson says member-circulated cassettes were degraded. These are provenance/search leads only, not verified surviving masters.
- **Other Waupun groups:** QCR, Yusef & The Fellows, King Thomas and the Blue Flames, The Spiritual Five, and The Sudanese remain leads mentioned in the 2022 oral-history article; no specific surviving audio identified in this pass.

**Assessment:** Known 45 and 2016/2021 reissues are CONTEXT ONLY because specialist collectors have documented them. The 1975 WHS rehearsal film and unissued repertoire remain HIGH PRIORITY as less-circulated primary documentation, but neither should be represented as undiscovered. Keep the video separate from an audio-only release discography. Respect archival rights and any access conditions.

Sources: Max Wilson, “Lost Soul Behind Bars,” https://quillette.com/2022/07/28/lost-soul-behind-bars/ ; AMIA 2024 programme, item 16, https://amiaconference.net/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/ASN-2024.pdf ; official Now-Again Bandcamp, https://nowagainrecords.bandcamp.com/track/paradise-lost ; official Symphonical Bandcamp, https://symphonicalrecords.bandcamp.com/album/the-key-to-our-love-vol-3 ; Newtone catalogue, https://www.newtone-records.com/product/9053925 ; Qobuz, https://www.qobuz.com/nl-nl/album/come-on-with-the-come-on-tlc-upheaval/n0w3b9oyu4c0a .


## 36. Soledad CTF / Jack Bowers cassette recordings (1983–1996) — item-level audit

<badge>HIGH PRIORITY — archival access lead</badge>

**Primary source:** Benjamin J. Harbert collection of music in prisons, American Folklife Center, Library of Congress, finding aid PDF (2025), call no. AFC 2024/014, Series 7, Subseries 1 “Jack Bowers collection (Soledad Prison),” printed pp. 143–144 / PDF lines 5751–5795: https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/gdc/gdcfindingaidpdfs/af024007/af024007.pdf . The finding aid identifies these as materials collected by Jack Bowers at Soledad Prison, also known as the Correctional Training Facility (CTF), California. All five groupings share digital collection ID `afc2024014_dc001`; this is a collection-package identifier, not a public track download URL.

### Catalogued sound groupings

1. **Rusty Smith (1983)** — 10 AIFF sound files. Tracklist:
   1. All I Wanna Do Is Make Good Music
   2. Three Squares
   3. Western Sunsets
   4. Can't Take the Beer Out of Country Fried Music
   5. Gonna Open Your Door (Say Adios For the Last Time)
   6. All Make Believe
   7. The Speckled Bird
   8. The Cowboy Song
   9. God Needed an Angel
   10. Never Could Dance (But I Can Sure Rock n' Roll)

   **Important cross-reference:** a searchable transcription of US Copyright Office records identifies a 1984 cassette, *Pearls Before Swine*, published by Joint Venture, Santa Cruz, and credits Rusty Smith and the Saddletramps; the sound recording entry says George Lee Davidson authored, performed and recorded the material. The related musical-work record describes *Pearls Before Swine* as an unpublished 1983 cassette of lyrics and music by Davidson. Sources: https://www.copyrightencyclopedia.com/all-i-wanna-do-is-make-love-to-you-the-receiving-end-more/ and https://www.copyrightencyclopedia.com/two-family-organizational-patterns-and-their-involvement-in/ . This is a potentially important identity/edition match, **not yet proven to be the same 1983 CTF audio files**. Do not infer or publish an artist's legal identity from this cross-reference without matching the cassette liner and primary LOC materials. It does establish that at least a closely related Rusty Smith/Saddletramps cassette circulated commercially in 1984, so the work may be less obscure than the CTF collection record alone suggests.

2. **Sacred Ryche (1986)** — 7 AIFF sound files. Tracklist:
   1. Heavy Metal Atmosphere
   2. Lover's Delight
   3. Guitar Solo
   4. Patiently Devoted to Love
   5. Seasons of the Witch
   6. Why Don't We Say Goodbye?
   7. Sound of a Broken Heart

   No public commercial release or reliable outside discography located in this pass. Likely strongest obscurity candidate of the named groups, but the finding aid alone does not establish which tracks are originals, covers, or individual member authorship.

3. **Correctional Training Facility Recording Project, July 1992** — 13 AIFF sound files. Tracklist:
   1. Youngster, Youngster
   2. Guiding Light
   3. Hasta Que Cierre el Salon
   4. Down By the Riverside
   5. Thinkin' uv U
   6. I Am an Eagle
   7. Day & Night
   8. Jessica
   9. Middle Class Brainwashed
   10. The Memorie of Your Smile
   11. My Feet Don't Touch the Ground
   12. Drivin' Your Heart to Ruin
   13. Bonus Jam

   This is a project/compilation rather than a named band release. Need identify performers, authorship, recording process and whether any tracks are participant compositions versus standards/covers.

4. **Anthology Correctional Training Facility, 1996** — 9 AIFF sound files. Tracklist:
   1. LA Samba
   2. Recuerdos de la Alhambra
   3. Rejas de Acero
   4. Dreams of Arrowhead
   5. Not Love
   6. Invictus
   7. Blurs
   8. Affirmation
   9. Cosa de la Vida

   Also an institutional compilation. “Recuerdos de la Alhambra” is a known classical guitar composition; do not treat all nine titles as original participant compositions. Need track-by-track performer and authorship mapping.

5. **Oscar's Tune** — 2 AIFF sound files: “Oscar's Tune” and “Oscar's Tune (extra) - God Bless the Child.” The finding aid does not give a date or identify Oscar, the performer, or the facility more precisely than its placement within the Jack Bowers/Soledad subseries. Treat as a high-value but low-confidence lead; do not attribute to a named person or state a precise recording year.

**Artwork and possible provenance key:** the same subseries includes five JPG photographs of cassette liners for the 1983–1996 recordings. These may identify performers, bands, dates, studio or track credits. Prioritise inspection of all five images when the AFC makes them available; liner data could resolve the Rusty Smith/Saddletramps connection and identify Sacred Ryche/CTF project contributors.

**Access and rights:** the overall collection is open to research, but the finding aid directs researchers to request materials through the AFC Folklife Reading Room: https://hdl.loc.gov/loc.afc/folklife.contact . The finding aid says the collection has 660 digital sound files overall; it does not expose these Soledad AIFF files as public audio downloads. Collection digital ID `afc2024014_dc001` is not a working audio URL. Harbert's collection rights are governed by copyright for his lifetime, after which he dedicates his rights to the public domain; researchers may reuse his rights under CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 while the collection remains under copyright, but third-party rights may apply. See the finding aid pp. 2, 143–144. No audio auditioned and no files retrieved.

**Assessment:** HIGH PRIORITY as an unusually well-described, under-circulated prison recording corpus with original cassette artwork and exact tracklists. Prioritise Sacred Ryche and Oscar's Tune for obscurity, then the 1992 and 1996 compilations for track-level authorship work. Rusty Smith is provenance-strong but has a potentially related 1984 cassette publication trail; investigate before assigning the highest obscurity rating. This is not evidence that all material was composed by incarcerated people; the item-level performers/authors remain to be identified.


## 37. Kalaupapa settlement church choirs — 1952 cassette now item-described

<badge>HIGH PRIORITY — publicly playable archival excerpts</badge>

**Primary community/church source:** Hawai‘i Conference United Church of Christ, “Kalaupapa Sunday,” LISTEN section: https://www.hcucc.org/kalaupapa-sunday . The page hosts 13 playable excerpts labelled Audio A1–A13 for Kana‘ana Hou Boys Chorus / Kana‘ana Hou Choir, and Audio B1–B8 for St. Francis of Assisi Church Choir, all from a single 60-minute cassette recorded in 1952. The page credits the recordings as courtesy of Nancy Chang Talino.

**Crucial two-page description PDF:** https://www.hcucc.org/_files/ugd/1c51b0_974ec3094c53448d93230effc8ebec64.pdf . It says the cassette was recorded by Steve Dawson and Robert Kihe, both patients. It does **not** say that every singer was a patient; preserve the distinction between recordists and choir membership.

**Side 1 (Protestant church; 11 songs):**
- First six by Kana‘ana Hou Boys Chorus; last five by Kana‘ana Hou Choir.
- “Your Mother is Praying for You”
- “Oh, Mary Don't You Weep”
- “Peter, Peter, On the Sea, Sea, Sea”
- “Sweet Are the Promises” — Ernest Kalā solo at beginning
- “Moku Kakele o i ona” — title/spelling explicitly uncertain in the HCUCC description; Ernest Kalā sings lines solo
- “Ho‘onani ia ke Akua ma nā lani ki‘eki‘e loa”
- “E Nā Kini o ka ‘Āina” — explicitly credited as written by Ernest Kalā; the description notes its adoption as the Kalaupapa Lions Club's unofficial theme song
- “Ke Kali Nei Au” — duet by Ernest Kalā and his sister
- Two songs with unknown titles
- “Hau‘oli Nā Lāhui Āpau” — closing number for the church choir

The PDF names the boys' chorus and director Ernest Kalā and lists the Kana‘ana Hou Church Choir personnel. Preserve Hawaiian diacritics where possible; the original document has inconsistent orthography and one title marked uncertain.

**Side 2 (Catholic church):** St. Francis of Assisi Church Choir, 8 excerpts (Audio B1–B8), but the written description says the titles and singers are not yet identified and asks for help from someone in the Catholic Church. Do not invent titles or personnel.

**Assessment:** This is substantially stronger than the earlier vague “patient choir tapes” lead: a stable church-hosted listening page, a complete side-level track count, partial tracklist, named recordists, named composer for “E Nā Kini o ka ‘Āina,” and clear distinction between Protestant and Catholic choir sources. Strong candidate for HIGH PRIORITY because of direct playable excerpts and strong community provenance, while respecting that not all singers are confirmed patients. The source page is an excerpt/player page, not a verified downloadable full cassette. The source PDF says Nancy Talino supplied the recording and did not know how she originally obtained it. Do not claim chain-of-custody beyond that.

## 38. Carville / National Hansen's Disease Center patient cantatas — precise cassette inventory

**Primary archival source:** Archives of the Daughters of Charity, Province of St. Louise, *Carville, LA – Hansen's Disease Center* finding aid (2023): https://docarchivesblog.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/02/11-2-carville-la-national-hansens-disease-center-finding-aid.pdf . The Audio-Visual series, RG 11-2-8-4, identifies:
- Box 48, folder 7: “Carville Easter cantata performed by patients, narrated by Buddy Glacis, organ played by Sr. Laura Stricker” — cassette; date not specified in the inventory.
- Box 48, folder 8: “Christmas cantata” — cassette; no date or performer attribution in the inventory.
- Box 48, folder 9: “Christmas cantata by Sr. Laura Stricker & patients” — cassette; inventory date 1 Jan 1981.
- Box 48, folder 6: “Carville organ music” — cassette; likely contextual music, not automatically a patient-created recording.

**Access and use:** the finding aid says researchers must use digital alternatives to audiovisual materials where they exist. Some restrictions apply, and publication of any archival material (including audio) requires permission from the Daughters of Charity Communications Director. Contact the archives for access and reproduction forms. The inventory is a catalogue description, not a playable audio link.

**Assessment:** PROMISING / HIGH PRIORITY archival chase. The Easter cantata is especially clear evidence of patient performance, with named narrator and organist. The 1981 Christmas cantata is also explicitly performed by patients with Sr. Laura Stricker. Do not conflate patient performance with patient composition; authorship is not specified. The generic Christmas cantata and organ cassette remain lower-confidence until their contents are described.


## Pros and Cons Program — 2026 source-route and track-level audit supplement

This supplements entry 3 (Grand Valley) and the earlier Pros and Cons references; it does not create new candidate numbers. Primary source pages were opened and individual media/download links followed.

### Grand Valley — *Undisclosed Location* (2017–2018; 10 tracks)
Official page: https://prosandconsprogram.com/undisclosed-location/
- The official description explicitly states all ten songs are original music, written, sung and recorded anonymously by incarcerated women at Grand Valley Institution for Women in 2017–18. Producer Aimee Copping; mix Hugh Christopher Brown. This is unusually strong participant-authorship evidence.
- Official downloadable archive: https://prosandconsprogram.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/Undisclosed-Location.zip
- Individually linked audio files are M4A:
  - “Demons” https://prosandconsprogram.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/01-Demons.m4a
  - “Falling” https://prosandconsprogram.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/02-Falling.m4a
  - “Lost” https://prosandconsprogram.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/03-Lost.m4a
  - “Running Back To You” https://prosandconsprogram.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/04-Running-Back-To-You.m4a
  - “Secret Survival” https://prosandconsprogram.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/05-Secret-Survival.m4a
  - “Victoria” https://prosandconsprogram.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/06-Victoria.m4a
  - “Shining Fool” https://prosandconsprogram.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/07-Shining-Fool.m4a

  - “Broken Pieces” https://prosandconsprogram.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/10-Broken-Pieces.m4a
- Direct URLs were obtained by following the official page's track links; web preview could not play the audio MIME type, which does not mean the files are unavailable. The ZIP link was likewise verified as a linked download target, not downloaded here.
- Keep all participant names anonymous; do not attempt to infer identities. The official site says the music is freely available anonymously, but this does not itself settle all third-party reuse rights.

### Pittsburgh Institution — *Postcards from the County* (2014; 12 tracks)
Official page: https://prosandconsprogram.com/postcards/
- The project page states the music was produced entirely inside Pittsburgh Institution. Inmates wrote and arranged songs in the prison chapel, converted to a recording studio; every note was sung or played in jail. Guest musicians also joined sessions. The page explicitly says tracks marked “P.I.” are original inmate works by participants who chose anonymity; the text extraction of the page does not preserve which individual track titles carry that marker, so do not infer a track-by-track authorship assignment from the extracted list.

- Facility naming: official project page says Pittsburgh Institution; other official program material describes this as Joyceville Correctional Centre. Preserve the original period/source name “Pittsburgh Institution” in the candidate record and note the later/current Joyceville naming; do not treat it as a second unrelated prison.
- The official page says every note was sung or played inside jail, but guest participants included Sarah Harmer, Kate Fenner, Luther Wright, Sarah McDermott and Pete Bowers. Do not imply every contributor was incarcerated. The archive page says P.I. marks identify original inmate works; track markers should be checked visually on the page or in the downloaded liner/archive before assigning individual tracks.

### *Private Town* (Grand Valley, released 15 May 2020)
Official page: https://prosandconsprogram.com/private-town/
- The page confirms it is the second album produced at Grand Valley after *Undisclosed Location*, and explicitly says songs were written and performed by incarcerated women. Producer Aimee Copping; mix Hugh Christopher Brown and Jake Bury.
- The official page's visible text extraction does not expose its embedded player track titles, track count, or individual media URLs. Do not reuse the older Bandcamp route as though it were a direct official media link. An official 2020 announcement confirms the album release and describes the participant songwriting process: https://prosandconsprogram.com/do-you-want-to-hear-what-hope-and-transformation-sound-like/
- Next step: inspect the official page's embedded player or video and obtain a stable official tracklist/audio source; no direct track URL verified in this pass.

### *Paint in the Forest* (Joyceville Institution, 2017–2019; 12 selected tracks)
Official page: https://prosandconsprogram.com/paint-in-the-forest/
- The official page confirms the location and recording period. The May 2020 official announcement states 12 songs were selected from over 100 tracks recorded in Joyceville's on-site studio; “Lord Prepare Me” is a rendition of a traditional Methodist hymn. Do not claim all 12 are original participant compositions without track-level evidence.
- Track-level titles and source pages need a separate audit; the official page's text extraction in this pass does not expose its embedded player track list or direct audio links.
- Official 2020 announcement: https://prosandconsprogram.com/do-you-want-to-hear-what-hope-and-transformation-sound-like/

**Assessment update:** *Undisclosed Location* now has the clearest direct official audio and ZIP route plus explicit originality/authorship statement; retain HIGH PRIORITY / provenance strong. *Postcards from the County* is a rich mixed-authorship institutional compilation; PROMISING / CONTEXT rather than uniformly inmate-created. *Private Town* has explicit inmate songwriting/performance but incomplete item-level track mapping. *Paint in the Forest* has strong in-prison recording provenance but needs track-level authorship and audio mapping. The official pages describe free and/or donation-based access; no audio or ZIP was downloaded or auditioned in this research pass.


## Waupun State Prison / Upheaval — new recording and reissue audit (2026-11-15)

Supplement to the existing Upheaval entry (see candidate 32); research is based on Max Wilson's 2022 oral-history article and label/retailer pages. Do not repeat sensitive criminal-history details in the compendium.

### Newly identified surviving recordings and titles
- The article reports that Joe Hayes held a CD's worth of Upheaval recordings, including “Come On with the Come On” and “Vampire Lady.” The latter is not found with a public authorised audio/download route in this pass; treat as a source lead, not a verified download.
- The 1975 Wisconsin Historical Society half-inch video footage is described as about one hour. Songs specifically discussed in the article include “Love Station,” “Broken Down Porch” (written by James Harris), “Ready Train,” and “Happy Hooker.” Article embeds point to WHS Aviary media IDs 155112 and 155113. Both embeds redirect-looped / failed in the web viewer; no public downloadable file URL verified. The article does not provide a complete segment-by-segment cue sheet.
- The article says LaVelle Rudd held reel-to-reel and cassette tapes, including live cassette recordings, but after circulation the tapes were in poor condition; the later whereabouts of his materials are unverified. Do not infer current custody or access.
- It also documents that “Come On with the Come On” / “T.L.C.” was reissued on Symphonical Records in 2016 from the surviving cassette masters, separate from the earlier scarce 1974 45.

### Authorised reissue/download routes — suitable for Grok transfer queue
1. **Upheaval — “Come on with the Come on” / “TLC” (2016, Symphonical Records, SYM001).** Newtone Records Digital product page: https://www.newtone-records.com/product/9053925 . The page offers a digital purchase (420 JPY) and lists both tracks, durations 3:18 and 3:05, 16-bit/44.1 kHz formats (WAV/FLAC/AIFF/ALAC/MP3). It explicitly describes these as previously unreleased live recordings made inside Waupun Correctional Institution, with cassette masters as the only surviving source. This is a paid retailer download route, not a free direct file URL. **GROK DOWNLOAD REQUEST: acquire the two-track digital release from this retailer if purchase is authorised; retain the supplied original files and metadata.**
2. **Upheaval — “Paradise Lost” (2021/2023 digital reissue).** Official Now-Again Records Bandcamp track page: https://nowagainrecords.bandcamp.com/track/paradise-lost . It offers streaming and a paid digital download in MP3/FLAC and more, 16-bit/44.1 kHz; listed at $1 USD or more. The track appears on *Tickets For Doomsday: Heavy Psychedelic Funk & Soul Ballads and Dirges 1970–1975* (released 26 Jan 2023). **GROK DOWNLOAD REQUEST: acquire the official Bandcamp digital track (or full compilation only if desired) via authorised purchase.** Note the source is a later commercial reissue; not an obscure/unissued master.
- These two digital reissues are the only Upheaval audio files for which an authorised commercial download route was identified in this pass. Do not try to rip the WHS video or unlicensed copies of Hayes/Rudd tapes.

### Assessment
- The two-track Symphonical 45 is especially relevant to the compendium: it preserves two previously unreleased prison-recorded performances, and its source cassette masters are explicitly described.
- “Vampire Lady,” “Broken Down Porch,” “Ready Train,” and “Happy Hooker” are worthwhile follow-up targets but no authorised downloadable audio was found. Keep them as unresolved leads.
- *Paradise Lost* is already reissued and relatively well documented among rare-soul collectors; retain as contextual / CONTEXT ONLY rather than a high-priority obscurity find.
- Primary/near-primary sources: Max Wilson, “Lost Soul Behind Bars,” 28 July 2022, https://quillette.com/2022/07/28/lost-soul-behind-bars/ ; Newtone Records Digital SYM001 page above; Now-Again Records Bandcamp page above. The article cites Joe Hayes and historical source materials; its account is valuable but remains secondary reporting/oral-history rather than an archive catalogue.


## Langdon Hospital / Labyrinthe Des Esprits — *The Cosmic Hunt* (Dawlish, Devon, UK; released 18 March 2025)

**Status: VERIFIED RECORDING / PROMISING, but low obscurity due to recent coverage and commercial release.**

- Primary institutional source: Devon Partnership NHS Trust announcement, https://www.dpt.nhs.uk/news/langdon-patients-release-therapeutic-ambient-album-831/ . It identifies *Labyrinthe Des Esprits: The Cosmic Hunt* as an album created at Langdon Hospital, a medium-secure forensic service in Dawlish. Six patients recorded, edited and produced it under Musical Technical Instructor Matt Smith. Field recordings were made during Ministry of Justice-approved Section 17 leave at locations including Fernworthy Reservoir and Buckfast Abbey; guest ambient musicians Nick Lesja, Anthony Tang and Ash Wednesday also contributed. A Trust employee, Peter, narrates.
- Official artist/label Bandcamp: https://labyrinthedesesprits.bandcamp.com/album/the-cosmic-hunt . Released 18 March 2025; 24-bit/44.1kHz digital download offered for £7 or more; limited cassette + digital edition listed at £10. Bandcamp credits six patient contributors by initials only: JPW, CN, NT, JLG, DS, AC. Licence is “all rights reserved.”
- Tracklist (Bandcamp): Ceremonies (4:48); Bug Candy Toot (3:46); Temporal Disjunctions (4:29); Henceforth (1:33); Heden Ritualer (3:36); Pater Noster (1:44); Cheesy Norbert (3:53); Beef Casual (4:37); Woodwose Dawn (6:34); Shug Monkey (2:33); The Limpet (6:57); Lords of Misrule (6:56); Widecombe 1638 (4:43); The Cosmic Hunt (2:23); Luxury of Ignorance (3:53); Splitting the Flock (3:27); Ambitious Ghost (3:17); Burst of Blue Flame (3:39); The Stones of Froggymead (3:57); Night Time Buzzard Trail (3:18); Judith Bastard (2:41); Die Haarigen Hande (2:12); March of the Wee Folk (5:51); plus long-form “The Cosmic Hunt, Side A” (43:16) and “The Cosmic Hunt, Side B” (45:00).
- The Bandcamp description says collaborative ambient album made by patients in an NHS medium-secure forensic hospital; natural field recordings and experimental textures using cassette loops, modular instruments, Norns Shield and Akai MPC Live. Do not infer that each listed composition is wholly patient-authored: collective credits include outside ambient artists, and the institutional source explicitly confirms guest contributions.
- Patient initials are published by the project; preserve that limited attribution and do not attempt deanonymisation. No diagnostic or offence details belong in the entry.
- Obscurity assessment: the institutional origin is unusual and directly evidenced, but the album is a recent public release, received a full-page review in The Wire and BBC coverage; it is not an obscure/unknown private recording. Its value is as a transparent contemporary example of participant-created music in a secure psychiatric environment, not as a high-obscurity discovery.
- **GROK DOWNLOAD REQUEST added to TRACK_MAPPING.md:** official artist Bandcamp digital album at £7+; high-quality MP3/FLAC and other formats, 24-bit/44.1kHz. Purchase/download only through the official page. All rights reserved; purchase does not grant republication or redistribution permission.
- Sources: NHS Trust announcement above; official Bandcamp album page above; BBC News report (3 Sep 2025), https://www.bbc.com/news/articles/clydyxl1w65o (search-indexed copy may be available where BBC page is blocked).


### Waupun World — item-level archival target refined (2026-11-15)
- Max Wilson's Upheaval oral-history article quotes Eddie “Baby Q” Quinn's essay defining soul music, published in the **January 1971 issue of Waupun World**; the same article identifies Quinn's “Music Scene” columns and names the prison groups QCR, Yusef & The Fellows, King Thomas and the Blue Flames, The Spiritual Five and The Sudanese. These are band-history leads only; no separate surviving recordings for those other groups are established here. Source: https://quillette.com/2022/07/28/lost-soul-behind-bars/ .
- Wisconsin Historical Society's prison newspaper collection PDF gives unusually specific holdings for *Waupun World*: 20 Feb, 20 Apr and 20 Nov 1970; **20 Jan–20 Nov 1971**; 1 Sep 1972; 15 Oct and 20 Dec 1973; 20 Jan–20 Nov 1974; 20 Jan–20 June and 20 Nov 1975; July 1980–Jan 1983. Source: https://cdn.wisconsinhistory.org/cdm/Prison%20Newspaper%20CollectionSep2017.pdf .
- Immediate exact archival request: January 1971 issue for Quinn's article; then 1974–75 issues for Upheaval band notices, performances and recording references. This is a better-targeted request than a broad “Waupun newspapers” enquiry. The 2024 Waupun Public Library digitisation announcement lists local newspapers and *Prison Press*, but does not list *Waupun World*: https://waupunpioneer.com/2024/05/21/waupun-public-librarys-collection-of-historical-waupun-newspapers-digitized/ .


## Iowa State Penitentiary, Fort Madison — inmate radio production and The Madisonians (1956–1959)

**Status: VERIFIED MUSIC / RECORDING INFRASTRUCTURE; extant sound objects not yet located. HIGH-PRIORITY ARCHIVE SEARCH, not a Grok download request yet.**

- A contemporary trade-magazine article, Dutch Vanderneese, “Prison Tapes—Unique Public Relations,” *Tape Recording*, Aug. 1959, pp. 28–30, is digitised at WorldRadioHistory: https://www.worldradiohistory.com/Archive-All-Audio/Archive-Tape-Recording/50s/Tape-Recording-1959-08.pdf . It documents the Iowa State Penitentiary's weekly half-hour radio programme **“Prison on the Hill”**, first broadcast 11 March 1956, initially on KXGI and later carried by 21 stations in Iowa and Illinois. The show was produced, directed, engineered and operated entirely by inmate talent; recordings were made throughout the prison using portable Ampex/Pentron equipment. Content included music, vocals, quartets and interviews. The article says no guard or official attended recording sessions.
- The same article identifies **The Madisonians**, an institutional orchestra directed by incarcerated trumpeter/bandleader Bob Frost. Its engagements were “at home”; Frost is pictured conducting and operating recording equipment. Bobby Mohr, a comedian/night-club entertainer before incarceration, is named as co-producer/performer. Duke Birr is identified as engineer in the studio photograph. These are contemporary credited names; avoid further biography without corroboration.
- The *Daily Iowan*, 23 May 1957, reports four Fort Madison inmates interviewed Iowa Governor Herschel Loveless and recorded the interview for “Prison on the Hill”; it says the programme was broadcast weekly by 20 Iowa stations. https://dailyiowan.lib.uiowa.edu/DI/1957/di1957-05-23.pdf .
- A 1938 *Daily Iowan* article confirms an inmate orchestra played at meal times, but that is earlier than the radio recordings and should be treated as a separate pre-radio music activity unless continuity is established: https://dailyiowan.lib.uiowa.edu/DI/1938/di1938-01-23.pdf .
- **Potentially surviving source objects:** original weekly programme tapes and/or station airchecks may have been retained by KXGI, WBAP? (not the station for this Iowa programme), KMA, prison administration, Iowa state archives, local Fort Madison collections, or private participants. These are search hypotheses, not known holdings. KMA's 1957 and 1958 station guides list “Prison on the Hill” in its Sunday schedule, confirming rebroadcast/syndication but not preservation: https://www.worldradiohistory.com/Archive-Station-Albums/KMA/KMA-Guide-1957-04.pdf ; https://www.worldradiohistory.com/Archive-Station-Albums/KMA/KMA-Guide-1958-03.pdf .
- No extant programme audio or direct download URL identified in this pass. Do not create a Grok download request until an authorised extant recording is found. Prioritise searching Iowa State Archives / Iowa Department of Corrections records, KXGI station history/collections, KMA broadcast archives, and Fort Madison local historical society. Search terms: “Prison on the Hill”, “Bob Frost”, “Bobby Mohr”, “Duke Birr”, “The Madisonians”, “John E. Bennett”, “J. Roby Hilpert”.
- Rights/ethics: programme was made under prison administration and used for public relations; inmate production does not by itself establish freedom from coercion or publication consent. Assess each surviving broadcast, performer, interview and station master separately.
- **Assessment:** unusually well documented inmate-operated recording and radio infrastructure with named music group and technical roles. It is a strong research lead, but the key audio survival criterion remains unfulfilled until a specific tape/aircheck is located.


## Iowa State Penitentiary — WOI-TV *In Our Care*, Episode 10 (1952): surviving film with prisoner song

- **Extant audiovisual item verified:** WOI-TV's *In Our Care*, Episode 10, “State Penitentiary at Fort Madison,” broadcast 11 July 1952. Iowa State University's WOI-TV 16mm film inventory assigns it **K-155**, runtime **29:00**, and credits producer Ray Stewart, director LaMar Smith, writer Hazel Allen, production research Ferne Bonomi and technical director Charles Hawley. The catalogue summary identifies the subject as the State Penitentiary at Fort Madison. Inventory PDF: https://specialcollections.lib.iastate.edu/files/documents/2023-04/WOI-TV-Films.pdf (printed PDF page 145; web text lines 6459–6467).
- **Music evidence:** the Johnson County Justice Center's page for the episode explicitly says it “also features a song conducted and performed by the prisoners.” This is a source description, not an independent audition or identification of the song, performers or composition. Page: https://jcjusticecenter.com/2015/01/11/in-our-care-fort-madison-penitentiary-documentary/ .
- **Public viewing route:** Iowa State University Special Collections and University Archives' YouTube channel has published at least Part 1 (https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ef6OTf8kTc8) and Part 3 (https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=taqZqdE74Jw). The Justice Center page embeds Parts 1–3. This establishes a public streaming route to the film, not a separate downloadable music master or an authorised audio extraction route.
- **Production-paper trail:** Ray Stewart Papers, RS 5/6/22, Box 1, Folder 5, “In Our Care” Fort Madison Penitentiary (1952), is listed in the Iowa State University finding aid. The collection is open for research; publication rights require consultation with the Head of Special Collections. Finding aid: https://findingaids.lib.iastate.edu/spcl/arch/rgrp/5-6-22.html (container list lines 106–112; rights lines 41–47).
- **Collection-level context:** WOI-TV film inventory K-155 and online university-hosted segments are direct evidence that the documentary survives. Iowa State's audiovisual access policy states that audio and moving-image materials are digitised for access; selected items are made freely available online. https://specialcollections.lib.iastate.edu/collections/audiovisual-collections .
- **Assessment:** upgrade from an unverified mention to a **verified extant audiovisual document containing a prisoner-performed song**, but keep the song itself as an unidentified excerpt within a documentary—not a standalone recording or a verified resident-authored composition. Do not add a Grok download request for the film or extract its audio absent an authorised file/access route and a clear project decision about documentary excerpts. Next: inspect the three public segments to locate and identify the song; consult Box 1/Folder 5 for scripts, shot lists or music cue information; ask SCUA whether K-155 has a preservation copy and whether any reuse permissions or source audio elements exist.
- **Related but separate:** the 1956–59 *Prison on the Hill* radio programme and The Madisonians remain a distinct archive-survival search. Do not assume the 1952 film song is by The Madisonians or that it is the same orchestra.
- Sources: WOI-TV Films inventory https://specialcollections.lib.iastate.edu/files/documents/2023-04/WOI-TV-Films.pdf ; Ray Stewart Papers finding aid https://findingaids.lib.iastate.edu/spcl/arch/rgrp/5-6-22.html ; Justice Center episode description https://jcjusticecenter.com/2015/01/11/in-our-care-fort-madison-penitentiary-documentary/ ; university-hosted video Part 1 https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ef6OTf8kTc8 ; Part 3 https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=taqZqdE74Jw .


### Fort Madison supplementary print-source route — *The Presidio* (1939–1968)

- Fort Madison Public Library's local-history holdings list includes **The Presidio, 1939–1968**, under prison history. This is a potential source for identifying the Madisonians, programme contributors, song titles, and the *Prison on the Hill* production context; issue-level completeness and access have not been established. Holdings guide: https://www.fortmadison-ia.com/DocumentCenter/View/33 .
- The April 1956 issue of *The Penal Press* carries a contemporary notice/review of *The Presidio*, describing it as an inmate-edited Fort Madison publication and naming inmate writers Hendrie, Runyon and Gagnan. Digitised issue: https://penalpress.com/wp-content/uploads/KPT_Apr56.pdf . This confirms circulation within the prison-publication exchange network, not the contents of any particular *Presidio* issue.
- A 1958 *Freeman* article explicitly describes *The Presidio* as prepared and published monthly by the men at the penitentiary and discusses its November 1956 issue; https://cdn.mises.org/The%20Freeman%201958%2C%20Vol%20IV_2.pdf . The 1956 issue's contents described there are literary/editorial, not music-related.
- The 1959 *Tape Recording* article says *The Presidio* circulated to more than 5,000 subscribers outside the prison and was associated with *Prison on the Hill*. Treat the magazine as an inmate-produced print/documentary source, not audio evidence.
- **Next use:** seek 1956–59 issues through Fort Madison Public Library and the State Historical Society of Iowa; inspect only for explicit programme/music references, contributor names and cross-references. Do not infer that the magazine contains song lyrics or audio.

**Primary source pages opened:**  
- *Private Town*: https://prosandconsprogram.bandcamp.com/album/private-town  
- *Paint in the Forest*: https://prosandconsprogram.bandcamp.com/album/paint-in-the-forest

Coming Through (4:41); 8. See You Smile (2:51); 9. A Show of Hands (2:41); 10. Heartless Tempo (3:27); 11. Double Life Day (4:17).

**Joyceville — *Paint in the Forest***  
- Bandcamp exposes a 12-track list with durations and lyrics. Release date 15 May 2020; recorded at Joyceville Minimum Institution between 2017 and 2019. The page offers MP3, FLAC and other formats, 24-bit/44.1 kHz, through Bandcamp checkout. Licence shown as all rights reserved.

Fly With Me (3:48); 9. Psalm 23 and Me (5:01); 10. Daddy's Little Girl (3:32); 11. Disbarred (3:50); 12. Shark Bait (2:26).

Official project copy says the 12 tracks were selected from over 100 recordings made in the on-site studio; “Lord Prepare Me” is a traditional Methodist hymn rendition. Do not claim all tracks are original compositions by participants.
- **Editorial assessment:** both releases meet the participant-created prison-music criterion. They are publicly available 2020 albums, so PROMISING / CONTEXT is more defensible than describing them as obscure archival finds. Keep Grand Valley and Joyceville as separate institutions.
- Source evidence: Bandcamp *Private Town* tracklist/credits/format https://prosandconsprogram.bandcamp.com/album/private-town ; Bandcamp *Paint in the Forest* tracklist/format https://prosandconsprogram.bandcamp.com/album/paint-in-the-forest ; official Pros and Cons project overview https://prosandconsprogram.com/ .


## Global discovery correction and Latin American lead — 2026-11-15

### Research-balance directive

The project must not default to North American and western European archives, institutions, languages or press coverage. Treat geographic balance as a substantive search requirement. For each new research batch, deliberately seek locally grounded evidence from underrepresented regions, including Africa, Asia, Latin America and the Caribbean, the Pacific, the Middle East, and eastern/southeastern Europe. Prioritise local-language catalogues, national and regional archives, community museums, universities, oral-history projects, independent labels and testimony from participants/families. Do not treat English-language discoverability as a proxy for significance or obscurity. Preserve original-language titles and spellings alongside translations. This does not mean imposing quotas or weakening evidence standards.

### Chile — *Cantos Cautivos* / Chacabuco prisoner camp (1973–74)

- **Archive:** *Cantos Cautivos* (Captive Songs), a bilingual digital archive documenting music created, performed and listened to in political detention in Chile under the Pinochet dictatorship, 1973–1990. Archive portal: https://www.cantoscautivos.org/ ; project background: https://www.manchester.ac.uk/about/news/pinochets-soundtrack-to-torture-available-to-the-public/ ; scholarly overview: https://www.cantoscautivos.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/08/LMJ_a_00949-Chornik.pdf .
- **Specific extant recording lead — “El Suertúo”:** a cueca composed by Víctor Canto and Luis Cifuentes at Chacabuco between November 1973 and February 1974. It was clandestinely recorded by Alberto Corvalán Castillo using a tape recorder supplied by a camp captain. The recording is identified as part of *Cantos Cautivos*. Contemporary Chilean reporting describes the song and the recording history: https://radio.uchile.cl/2015/01/11/cantos-cautivos-la-musica-que-sono-en-los-centros-de-detencion-de-la-dictadura/ ; https://www.biobiochile.cl/noticias/2014/12/31/cantos-cautivos-canciones-al-interior-de-los-centros-de-torturas-de-pinochet.shtml .
- **Performance context:** Los de Chacabuco, a group created and directed by Ángel Parra, performed the song in the camp's weekly concert. University of Chile reporting describes the ensemble as having eleven other members. Do not collapse the song's composers, clandestine recordist, ensemble director and performers into a single authorship credit without item-level archive metadata.
- **Other leads in the archive:** the peer-reviewed project account reports several recordings made by musicians while detained at Chacabuco. A University of Chile music-education thesis table points to additional audio/score items, including a 1973 instrumental work from Cárcel de La Serena and several Chacabuco/Ritoque songs from 1973–75. Treat the thesis table as a discovery index and cross-check each item against the archive: https://openbiblio.museodelamemoria.cl/media/digitales/TESIS_EDUCACION%20MUSICAL.pdf .
- **Scale and update:** University of Chile's 2026 account says that by mid-2025 the archive had collected 168 testimonies, including 40 musical works created in prison and 12 works whose lyrics were written or altered in captivity over pre-existing music: https://uchile.cl/noticias/238457/u-de-chile-integra-archivo-cantos-cautivos-a-su-acervo-patrimonial . UNESCO published a pedagogical resource based on the archive in September 2026: https://www.unesco.org/en/articles/cartilla-pedagogica-archivo-digital-cantos-cautivos-musica-resiliencia-y-memoria .
- **Assessment:** HIGH PRIORITY archive audit for “El Suertúo” and other Chacabuco recordings, because the source is a contemporaneous clandestine recording with specific provenance, not merely a later reconstruction. Do not add a Grok download request until the archive's exact entry, playable media/download route, file identity and rights/access conditions are confirmed. The archive contains testimony and recordings of music heard under coercion as well as participant-initiated music; classify each entry carefully and do not equate all music in detention with prisoner-created work.
- **Rights/ethics:** the recording's clandestine origin and political violence context require careful provenance and consent/reuse assessment. Public archive access is not blanket permission to redistribute. Respect contributors' chosen names and any anonymity requests.
- **Research next:** enumerate Chacabuco audio entries; capture archive item titles, dates, makers, recordists, performers, recording medium and exact player/download links; identify which are original in-camp recordings versus later performances/reconstructions; then audit La Serena and Ritoque candidates from the thesis table against primary archive records.

### Malawi — Zomba Prison Project (2015–16), contextual comparator

- Official artist Bandcamp for *I Have No Everything Here* (released 27 January 2015): https://zombaprisonproject.bandcamp.com/album/i-have-no-everything-here . It has 20 tracks, streaming and paid digital download (MP3/FLAC and other formats, 16-bit/44.1 kHz), and credits the Zomba Prison Project. Tracklist and release details are captured in the official page.
- *I Will Not Stop Singing* (released 8 September 2016) tracklist and production context: https://www.sixdegreesrecords.com/zomba-prison-project-i-will-not-stop-singing/ . The project records music by prisoners and officers at Zomba Maximum Security Prison, Malawi.
- **Assessment:** geographically important and participant-led, but both releases are widely distributed commercial albums and have already received substantial international coverage. Keep as CONTEXT / comparative infrastructure rather than presenting them as obscure discoveries. The presence of officers alongside prisoners must be reflected in any track-level attribution. Do not add a download request absent a clear project need.

### Austrian Phonogrammarchiv — multilingual POW recording source infrastructure (1915–18)

- Austrian Academy of Sciences Phonogrammarchiv published a commented source edition of approximately 250 language and music recordings of First World War prisoners of war, including recordings from camps at Eger, Reichenberg and Theresienstadt (former Tsarist army prisoners) and Italian prisoners at Mauthausen and Marchtrenk. The series includes transcriptions, translations, protocols and scholarly commentary: https://www.oeaw.ac.at/en/phonogrammarchiv/publications/audio-and-video-publications/the-complete-historical-collections-1899-1950/ .
- The archive's series pages distinguish the Italian recordings from the multilingual recordings of Armenian, Jewish, Latvian, Lithuanian, Finno-Ugric, Russian, Ukrainian, Turk-Tatar, Georgian, Avar, Ossetian and Svanetian prisoners. The records are a multilingual source collection, not a single uniform musical tradition.
- **Assessment:** high-value comparative source infrastructure. Next task is item-level audit for recordings with music rather than speech, especially less-documented language communities; distinguish the POWs' own musical expression from the colonial/imperial researcher's recording agenda. The source edition is a scholarly publication; verify purchase/access terms before any acquisition request.



### Chile item-level correction/addendum — Museo de la Memoria Archivo Radial (2026-11-15)

- **Important provenance refinement:** the clandestine Chacabuco performance and the later broadcast are distinct layers. The Museo de la Memoria y los Derechos Humanos' Archivo Radial catalogues an item titled **“Centros de Detención”** under Radio Moscú / *Escucha Chile*, place Chacabuco, duration **23:36**. Its catalogue description says it documents an event at the Chacabuco nitrate works/prison camp marking the release of a group of prisoners. It lists performing groups **Punto 30, Jamarí, Hugo Peñaloza, Mario Céspedes and Coro Chacabuco**, and songs including “Libre,” “Oficina Chacabuco,” “Volver,” “Himno a la Alegría,” “Tonada del Viejo Amor,” “Amalia Rosa,” “Caliche” and “El Puntudo.” It notes defects and cuts caused by the technical/environmental conditions of recording. Public catalogue/search result with embedded audio player: https://archivoradial.museodelamemoria.cl/buscador_resultado.php?filtro=11&registros=0&tipo=1 .
- A music-education thesis citing the same Museo archive says the 23:36 “Centros de Detención” recording contains “El Suertúo” from **18:30 to 20:50**; it separately shows a 2:03 item titled “Grabación clandestina en Oficina Salitrera ‘Chacabuco’, convertido en centro de detención,” also filed under Radio Moscú / *Escucha Chile*. Thesis PDF: https://openbiblio.museodelamemoria.cl/media/digitales/TESIS_EDUCACION%20MUSICAL.pdf . This is a locator/timecode from a secondary teaching document, not independently checked by audition; use as a timecode lead.
- **Authorship correction:** a later itemised source table identifies “El Suertúo” and “El Puntúo” as lyrics by Víctor Canto and Luis Cifuentes and music by **Roberto Parra**; collection Fondo García Salas, Gerardo, Museo de la Memoria, conservation code **067-001-002**. Do not simplify this to Canto/Cifuentes as sole composers. Source: https://manager.mmdh.cl/uploads/L_Ibro_tesis_20212022_EDUCACION_MUSICAL_Vok_WEB_da5720b67c.pdf . The archive's testimony for Luis Cifuentes also says “St John’s Passion” is the third track on the cassette recorded at Chacabuco by Los de Chacabuco; search index result: https://www.cantoscautivos.org/en/search.php?curr=11&fdsp=2&ldsp=11&query=La+guerra+de+los+Vargas&size=916 .
- **Interpretation:** “El Suertúo” is documented as a camp composition and performance, with a clandestine camp recording and a separate Radio Moscú broadcast/archive record. The 23:36 broadcast item is a mixed event recording containing several acts/songs; do not describe the whole programme as a standalone original composition. The archive's 2:03 clandestine item is a separate catalogue object and must not be conflated with the 23:36 programme.
- **Access:** the Museo Archivo Radial result exposes an in-page audio player and item metadata. This verifies a public listening route, not a direct file-download link or reuse licence. No Grok download request added. Seek the individual “Ver detalle” item page and repository access/rights terms before considering any acquisition.
- **Additional candidate:** “Pasión según San Juan” / *St John’s Passion* by Ángel Parra is identified by the Cantos Cautivos index as the third track on the Chacabuco cassette recorded by Los de Chacabuco, with testimony by Luis Cifuentes. This is a strong secondary archival item lead; determine whether the archive offers the cassette/audio and whether it is a contemporary recording or later transfer/re-recording before adding an acquisition request.


## Further non-Western research — Nigeria and Japan (2026-11-15)

### Nigeria — Uzuakoli / Ikoli Harcourt Whyte: stronger recording-infrastructure evidence

- LEPRA's institutional history states that Uzuakoli opened in August 1932 with 201 residents, and that a choir formed under the leadership of composer Ikoli Harcourt Whyte. His doctor, J.A.K. Brown, recorded traditional songs from villages during visits, played the recordings back to Whyte, and encouraged him to create new works from local musical materials. https://www.lepra.org.uk/news/article/the-impact-of-discriminatory-laws-segregation/
- LEPRA's biography adds that Methodist doctor Frank Davey collaborated with Whyte; Davey recorded traditional music from outlying patients and brought it back to Whyte. Whyte's own hymns, mainly in Igbo, remain on sale in Nigeria. https://www.lepra.org.uk/centenary/people/details/ikoli-harcourt-whyte
- John Manton's *Uzuakoli in Music and Medicine* radio documentary (Modulations, 12 June 2015) is a concrete audio source that assembles original vinyl recordings of Harcourt Whyte's choir in remastered form, contemporary arrangements by biographer A. Kanu Achinivu, oral testimony and field recordings of sung/spoken passages. https://africanbiosciences.wordpress.com/publications/radio-uzuakoli-in-music-and-medicine/ . This is a later documentary mix, not itself an original patient-era master; its description confirms that original vinyl exists.
- The International Leprosy Association's archive page says Whyte was choir conductor at Uzuakoli and links to the broadcast and related archive material: https://leprosyhistory.org/reading-material/harcourt-whyte-broadcast/ . The 2022 WHO Goodwill Ambassador's Leprosy Bulletin describes a project to publish 50 selected songs in dual notation with Igbo lyrics translated into English: https://www.shf.or.jp/en/wp-content/uploads/sites/2/2022/08/LB110.pdf .
- **Assessment:** upgrade the Uzuakoli lead from general choir/vinyl possibility to confirmed surviving original vinyl recordings and documented field-recording history. Still need exact discographic identification of the patient-era choir sides, label/catalogue numbers, original recording dates, performers and access route. Separate Whyte's own compositions from the traditional source songs recorded by Davey and from later Achinivu arrangements. No Grok request until a specific original pressing or authorised digital source is identified.
- Additional archival lead: Northwestern's Vernon McKay papers include photographs of a patient dancing to music by a band of youngsters at Uzuakoli (ca. 1935–1977); visual context only, not audio evidence. https://findingaids.library.northwestern.edu/subjects/840 . TLM Canada archive notes audio tapes made in Purulia, Chandraghona, Bhutan, Irian Jaya, Papua New Guinea and Thailand, suggesting a broad missionary/health-recording collection worth item-level search, but not necessarily music or patient-created material: https://leprosyhistory.org/archive/archive714/ .

### Japan — Nagashima Aiseien / Aoi-tori Gakudan (Bluebird Band), 1953 onward

- The resident-founded harmonica ensemble **Aoi-tori Gakudan** (Bluebird Band) began in 1953 at Nagashima Aiseien National Sanatorium. It was led by resident musician/composer Koichi Kondo (1926–2009), who read Braille scores with his tongue and composed music. The band included harmonica, drums, guitar and triangle. The Japan Times/Chugoku Shimbun report provides a detailed oral-history account: https://www.japantimes.co.jp/news/2024/03/04/japan/society/harmonica-band-by-hansens-disease-patients/ .
- The National Hansen's Disease Museum held a 2018 exhibition on music activities in sanatoria and a 2020 museum talk specifically on music at the sanatoria, with an account of the Blue Bird Orchestra; the museum says a recording of the talk is available on YouTube. https://www.nhdm.jp/en/events/list/2241/ ; https://www.nhdm.jp/en/events/list/2533/ .
- A Japanese-language Kyoto University paper examines patient-led cultural and musical activities at Nagashima Aiseien, specifically the Aoi-tori ensemble: https://cir.nii.ac.jp/crid/1050564285755765504?lang=en .
- **Assessment:** PROMISING archive hunt, not yet an extant historical recording. The ensemble and its resident-led musical activity are well documented, but no contemporary band recording or exact catalogue item has yet been verified. Seek the National Hansen's Disease Museum, Nagashima Aiseien historical materials, former members/families and local broadcasters; search Japanese terms 楽団「あおいとり」, 青い鳥楽団, 長島愛生園, 近藤幸一, ハーモニカ. Do not substitute 2019 revival project Aoi Tori no Harmonica or later tribute performances for the 1950s–70s ensemble.


## Uzuakoli / Ikoli Harcourt Whyte — discographic correction and source-bibliography trail (2026-11-15)

- **Correction to the earlier working description:** do not imply that Ikoli Harcourt Whyte himself made an album or that the surviving vinyl necessarily dates from his lifetime. Music In Africa's historical account says Whyte did not record an album; the Choir of the Leprosy Colony, Uzuakoli recorded his compositions after his death in 1977. It also distinguishes a later 1999 recording by the St. Louis Missouri African Choir. This is secondary music-history reporting, but it is a material chronological qualification: https://musicinafrica.net/fr/node/13683 .
- John Manton's 2015 Cambridge/Modulations documentary confirms that original vinyl recordings of Harcourt Whyte's choir were used as remastered source audio. The programme is a mixed documentary/feature/sound-art composition incorporating those vinyl recordings, later arrangements by biographer Achinivu Kanu Achinivu, oral histories and field recordings. It is not itself an original pressing or a clean archival transfer: https://africanbiosciences.wordpress.com/publications/radio-uzuakoli-in-music-and-medicine/ .
- The International Leprosy Association's reading page highlights two choir-performed compositions as particularly significant: “Atulegwu” (also rendered “Atula Egwu” in some secondary sources) and “Umu gi emebiwo uwa gi.” These are useful title variants/search terms, not yet securely mapped to a particular side, matrix, catalogue number or recording date: https://leprosyhistory.org/reading-material/harcourt-whyte-broadcast/ ; https://leprosyhistory.org/reading-material/leprosy-and-cultural-production/ .
- A 1979 two-volume monograph by Achinivu Kanu Achinivu, *Ikoli Harcourt Whyte, the Man and His Music: A Case of Musical Acculturation in Nigeria*, includes musical notation and texts in volume 2. A catalogue record in the International Library of African Music / Africa Multiple Interactive Research Atlas identifies the bibliographic object as DRE eaa-99-0243, identifier 1270; it is a book/source edition, not the original vinyl. Record/access page: https://amira.africamultiple.uni-bayreuth.de/s/amira/item/29819 . The catalogue gives a public metadata record and contact route, not the original audio's custodian.
- **Current status:** original choir-vinyl audio existence is supported; exact pressing, label, catalogue number, recording date, track sequence and authorised acquisition route remain UNRESOLVED. Keep four layers distinct: Whyte's compositions; the patient choir's posthumous recordings; Davey/Brown field recordings of village music; and Achinivu's later arrangements/documentary excerpts. No Grok request yet.

## Japan — Nagashima Aiseien / Aoi-tori Gakudan: period documentation and audio distinction (2026-11-15)

- Japanese-language research gives a more precise institutional record of the ensemble: **青い鳥楽団** / **楽団青い鳥** formed in November 1953 as a harmonica band of visually impaired residents at Nagashima Aiseien; Koichi Kondo (近藤宏一, 1926–2009) was bandmaster. The group began with 12 members and performed harmonica, guitar and hand-made drums; repertoire expanded from children's songs and “Gavotte” to popular songs, marches, waltzes and chanson-derived material. Source: National Diet Library-hosted study, https://dl.ndl.go.jp/view/prepareDownload?contentNo=1&itemId=info%3Andljp%2Fpid%2F8800148 ; Japanese institutional/historical discussion: https://www.andrew.ac.jp/soken/pdf_5-1/kiyou44-3.pdf .
- The National Hansen's Disease Museum's 2007 exhibition page identifies a photograph captioned **“Aoi-tori Gakudan performance (May 1974, Nagashima Aiseien)”** and documents an event where Kondo was invited to perform harmonica and discuss his memories. This confirms period performance documentation and a later solo appearance, but the page does not expose a historic sound recording: https://www.nhdm.jp/events/list/2327/ .
- The museum's image list identifies two additional photographs by Zhao Gen-zai: B053 “本番を待つ” (waiting to perform), Nagashima Aiseien, 1970; and B054 “演奏” (performance), Nagashima Aiseien, 1970. The catalogue describes the group as a resident-founded visually impaired harmonica band established in 1953. Photo metadata and image list: https://www.nhdm.jp/hansen/wp-content/uploads/2023/pdf/list_b.pdf . These are photographic evidence only, not audio.
- A 2023 video by Ken Miyazaki includes/reuses footage filmed in December 1982 of Kondo singing “人間回復の橋” (Bridge of Human Recovery), lyrics by Shosuke Isobe (磯部昭介), with other members of the residents' Blind Association. The maker says he interviewed Kondo at the time. Since other Japanese sources say the band ceased activity in 1978, classify this as a later resident performance/archival film excerpt, **not automatically a Bluebird Band performance or a surviving 1953–78 band recording**: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d2F1LSyWpwc .
- A 2019 Asahi report documents a memorial concert by contemporary professional musicians Abe Umitaro, Toyama Takeo and Ito Tomma, recreating songs associated with the former band; this is a tribute, not the historical ensemble's recording: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=syjLdq4Ybsw .
- **Current status:** strong, locally documented history and 1970/1974 photographs; no period Aoi-tori audio master, disc, tape or broadcast item identified yet. Do not treat tribute performances or the 1982 Blind Association song as the historical band's recording. Next: identify original photographer/film-maker holdings, NHDM/Nagashima Aiseien History Museum audio-visual inventories and any 1974 performance film or broadcast.


## Chile — MMDH Chacabuco catalogue: two distinct audio objects and item metadata (2026-11-15)

- A direct catalogue search for chacabuco in MMDH Archivo Radial returns exactly two records, both under Radio Moscú / Escucha Chile, place Chacabuco:
  1. “Centros de Detención”, 23:36, outdoor recording. Description: ceremony at Oficina Salitrera Chacabuco marking release of a group of prisoners. Catalogue lists ensembles Punto 30, Jamarí, Hugo Peñaloza, Mario Céspedes and Coro Chacabuco; songs listed include “Libre,” “Oficina Chacabuco,” “Volver,” “Himno a la Alegría,” “Tonada del Viejo Amor,” “Amalia Rosa,” “Caliche,” “El Puntudo.” Participants: political prisoners at Chacabuco. Notes recording defects/cuts due to recording conditions.
  2. “Grabación clandestina en Oficina Salitrera ‘Chacabuco’, convertido en centro de detención”, 2:03, outdoor recording. Description: clandestine recording attributed to Alberto Corvalán (son of Luis Corvalán), ceremony for prisoners being released. Catalogue names Punto 30, Jamarí, Hugo Peñaloza, Mario Céspedes, Coro Chacabuco (directed by Iván Quezada), Ángel Parra, “Canción de la Despedida,” and closing narration. Same participants/location; same warning about defects/cuts.
- Primary catalogue search/result page (both records and metadata): https://archivoradial.mmdh.cl/buscador_resultado.php?filtro=chacabuco&registros=0&tipo=3 ; current canonical redirect host is archivoradial.museodelamemoria.cl for some searches. A result's “Ver detalle” link in the text renderer resolves back to the same result page, not an independent stable item page or direct media URL.
- A thesis reproduces both players separately and says “El Suertúo” occurs at 18:30–20:50 in the 23:36 “Centros de Detención” programme. This is a secondary timecode locator, not an independently auditioned claim. It does not place the song in the separate 2:03 item. https://openbiblio.museodelamemoria.cl/media/digitales/TESIS_EDUCACION%20MUSICAL.pdf .
- A 2022 scholarly chapter describes “El Suertúo” and “El Puntúo” as cuecas on “this clandestine recording,” which may refer to the 23:36 recording or to the clandestine source underlying the broadcast. The phrasing is not sufficient to merge the two catalogue objects. It also identifies the recorded performance as collective Chacabuco musical life and reproduces Cifuentes's testimony. https://portal.amelica.org/ameli/journal/645/6454649008/html/ .
- Song manuscript appendix lists “El Suertudo” and “El Puntudo” (spelling variants), Fondo Archivo de Fondos y Colecciones, MMDH code 00000067-000001-000002, 1973–74, source “Manuscrito Tomo II, Gerardo García Salas,” and links the clandestine Radio Moscú recording. It credits Luis Cifuentes in the “Autor” field; separate testimony says the cueca was composed by Cifuentes and Víctor Canto. Earlier table-based credit for the music as Roberto Parra conflicts with this appendix/press accounts, so preserve distinct lyric/composition/music credit claims and mark the attribution conflict rather than silently harmonising. https://manager.mmdh.cl/uploads/L_Ibro_tesis_20212022_EDUCACION_MUSICAL_Vok_WEB_da5720b67c.pdf .
- **Decision:** exact catalogue descriptions now distinguish the 23:36 programme and 2:03 clandestine item. “El Suertúo” timecode belongs, according to the thesis, to the 23:36 player. The 2:03 catalogue item explicitly includes “Canción de la Despedida.” No direct downloadable file, independent item detail, or reuse terms verified; no Grok request.

## TLM Canada / Effect:Hope audiovisual collection — inventory refinement (2026-11-15)

- International Leprosy Association's archive entry for TLM Canada (now Effect:Hope) confirms an extensive audiovisual collection and specifically says audio tapes were made on Purulia, Chandraghona, Bhutan, Irian Jaya, Papua New Guinea and Thailand. It does not provide item titles, dates, recording subjects, formats, creators, catalogue numbers or rights/access terms. Therefore these are archive audit targets only, not confirmed music recordings. https://leprosyhistory.org/archive/archive714/ .
- The same inventory lists 11 largely 16mm film reels on TLM Canada leprosy work; numerous videotapes by TLM Canada and some by American Leprosy Mission; slide collections for Bhutan, Thailand, Indonesia, New Guinea, Nepal, Irian Jaya, India and Africa; and a 1978 Box 14 group of 80 slides with cassette tape and pamphlet notes titled “More Than a Prophet.” The cassette's content is unspecified; do not assume music or treat the title as a recording title.
- Contact/access lead in the archive catalogue: Effect:Hope, Markham, Ontario, Canada; info@effecthope.org; +1 888 537 7679. The catalogue entry is the source description, not a current confirmation that every item remains physically available or that copying is permitted.
