- **District Jail, Panipat, Haryana — Kashish (18 Jul 2021):** a song written and sung by inmate Kashish for Nelson Mandela Day, with the hook “तिनका जेल रेडियो ये लेकर आया है…”. Official upload: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=j3I_urizKGU .
- **District Jail, Rohtak, Haryana — Jitendra, “Corona” (21 Jun 2021):** a Haryanvi ragini by inmate Jitendra, described as a prayer for a prosperous and jubilant nation after COVID; official upload: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AVvYWcoZHzI .
- **Central Jail, Ambala, Haryana — Sheru COVID-awareness song (22 May 2021):** composed by inmate Sheru, in Punjabi, with public-health lyrics; released jointly by Central Jail Ambala and Tinka Tinka Foundation. Official upload: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aObMkGTcNQc .
- **Central Jail, Bilaspur, Chhattisgarh — Shyam Bai (17 Apr 2017):** Tinka's first-person project history says the woman folk singer, incarcerated in 2013, composed a melody using small stones for a poem sent to her by Vartika Nanda; she performed it and the song was released while she was incarcerated. Shyam Bai was released in Sep 2023. The article does not give a track title or a direct audio URL; recording venue/chain and current rights remain to verify. Keep her pre-prison two albums separate from this in-prison-era composition. Source: https://tinkatinka.org/shyam-bais-musical-rendition-from-bilaspur-jail/ .
- **Context / not prioritised as obscure:** *Tinka Tinka Agra* (2017) was a publicly documented prison-theme song prepared over eight months by Agra Central Jail inmates, with 16 selected for the video; the official page lists 17 names, so don't normalise the count. Dinesh Kumar Gaur is identified as lead singer and the video was shot in the jail temple. Official video https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bAUTdwr42TY ; page https://tinkatinka.org/tinka-tinka-agra/ . *Tinka Tinka Tihar* is a similarly visible predecessor. These demonstrate the broader programme but are not the strongest obscurity cases.
- **Rights/access:** Tinka Tinka Foundation's official site expressly reserves copyright and prohibits copying, redistribution or use without written permission. The listed YouTube URLs are public listening/viewing routes only. No official digital download/purchase route verified; no Grok download request. Do not rip videos.
- **Next exact steps:** verify item-level titles, track duration, recording/master credits and exact production locations from the linked official episodes; distinguish original compositions from outside-written lyrics or re-arrangements; identify whether Shyam Bai's 2017 master is still held by Tinka Foundation and whether it can be streamed through an authorised route; request reuse conditions only if the project later wants to publish excerpts. Keep each prison, singer, language and date distinct.


## 2026-11-16 — Tinka Tinka item-level credit audit

- Rechecked the Foundation's official “Jail Radio: Jind” page against its “Music in Jail” index and the episode 71–80 index. The official Jind page identifies Episode 81, “आए अवध में राम” / “Prayers from Jind Jail,” as a song written and sung by Jai Bhagwan, with four incarcerated instrumentalists: Ramroop (dholak), Shashi (harmonium), Sunil (matka) and Govind (matka). Official item URL: https://tinkatinka.org/jail-radio-jind/ ; official index: https://tinkatinka.org/jail-music-2/ .
- Keep Episode 81 distinct from Jai Bhagwan's earlier original song released 25 Dec 2023 (“गमे ज़िंदगी में मुस्कुराना सीख ले…”). The same Jind page says the earlier composition was shared with Dehradun, where Dr Suchit Narang, Arun and Rohit made a new composition for Episode 77. Do not call Episode 77 the same recording or assume it is a straight cover without audio comparison.
- The Foundation index lists Episode 81 with a 21 Jan 2024 date and Episode 77 with a 25 Dec 2023 release date. It also lists Episode 90, “World Music Day / Musical creations through jail radio,” dated 21 Jun 2024, and states that more than 105 podcast episodes had been released by Jan 2025. These establish a larger official podcast corpus, but Episode 90's index citation alone does not identify additional discrete songs or performers; do not inflate IM-037's item count from episode count.
- The Foundation describes Jai Bhagwan as an undertrial inmate and says the Jind song was released through Tinka Jail Radio. Preserve the published credit, avoid additional identity research, and treat official YouTube links as listening/viewing routes only. The site reserves rights; no download/reuse permission is implied.
- No new evidence for Shyam Bai's 2017 Bilaspur master, custody, track title or direct authorised audio URL was found in this pass. Her item remains a separate lead, not a newly verified downloadable recording.
- No archive contact, audio download, or listening session was made. No candidate status change. Next: ask Tinka Tinka for an item-level catalogue/tracklist and authorised listening details for Episode 81 and Episode 77, plus the current custodian and authorised stream for Shyam Bai's 2017 recording.


## Brazil — community mental-health music beyond the prison corpus (2026-11-16)

This is a deliberate expansion away from the prison/camp-heavy recent shortlist. These are music-making groups rooted in community mental-health services, not prison recordings. Do not frame the music as “symptoms set to music”: the sources document artists making songs across ordinary social, political and personal subjects.

### Mágicos do Som — CAPS Usina de Sonhos, Volta Redonda, Rio de Janeiro, Brazil (1996 onward)

- **Status:** VERIFIED recording corpus; **PROMISING** for obscurity / recovery priority. A municipal health-department announcement documents the launch of the group's second CD on 13 November 2013, titled *Saúde Musical*, containing ten songs, with samba predominant. It says the compositions were all written by the group and concerned everyday experiences, autonomy and social participation. A later municipal account confirms that the group released two CDs and that the latest was the 2013 album.
- **Environment and authorship:** The group formed in March 1996 through music-therapy work at CAPS Usina de Sonhos, a community mental-health centre in Volta Redonda. The 2013 municipal announcement says the seven-person group was made up of users of the municipal mental-health programme and names the members and coordinator; preserve those names only as published group credits, without inferring diagnoses or private histories.
- **Documented track titles from the 2013 announcement:** “Cantar no Mágicos do Som”; “A música é terapia”; “Samba para chamar atenção”; “Na hora da virada”; “Doce Sorriso”; “Eu te amo.” These are examples from a ten-song album, not a complete tracklist.
- **Important title/date discrepancy:** A 2007 UFF thesis describes an earlier first CD, also titled *Saúde Musical*, and identifies “Preconceito” as its first/group-defining composition. The 2013 municipal announcement calls its ten-song album the **second** CD and also titles it *Saúde Musical*. The 2021 municipal retrospective says there were two CDs and that the latest was released in 2013. This may be a repeated album title, a naming error, or a mismatch in the thesis/municipal accounts; do not silently merge the 1999/2000 first-disc account with the 2013 second-disc object. A physical sleeve, catalogue number or matrix is needed to resolve it.
- **Access / rights:** A historical listening lead has now been identified: Lia Rejane Mendes Barcellos's *Voices Resources* article “Carnival and Music Therapy” (6 Oct. 2002) embeds “Track 2” and identifies it as the *Saúde Musical* arrangement of “Preconceito,” sung by composer Regina Lúcia Serrão. The page states that the clip was presented with the composer's and coordinator's approval at the time. However, the linked MP3 (`https://www.voices.no/grafikk/colbar2.mp3`) currently returns 404, so it is not a working listening route and does not establish present-day download/reuse permission. The 2007 UFF dissertation's annex CD-ROM reportedly contains the *Saúde Musical* audio; no public downloadable copy of that annex has been verified. No current authorised stream, complete ten-track 2013 tracklist, physical catalogue number or current custodian has yet been verified. No audio auditioned; no contact made.
- **Why it matters for curation:** This is a non-prison, Brazilian samba-led, self-composed CD from a community mental-health programme. The documented songs include affection, everyday life, anti-prejudice and autonomy rather than a narrow institutional narrative.
- **Sources:**
  - [Secretaria Municipal de Saúde de Volta Redonda, “Mágicos do Som lança seu segundo CD”](https://www2.voltaredonda.rj.gov.br/sms/index.php/8-noticias-destaque/165-magicos-do-som-lanca-seu-segundo-cd) — 12 Nov. 2013; accessed 2026-11-16. Official announcement of the 13 Nov. launch, ten songs, group authorship, member credits and sample track titles.
  - [Prefeitura de Volta Redonda, “CAPS Usina dos Sonhos completa 26 anos”](https://www.voltaredonda.rj.gov.br/9-noticias-em-destaque/4187-caps-usina-dos-sonhos-completa-26-anos/) — 2021; accessed 2026-11-16. Confirms group formation in 1996, two CDs, and that the latest was from 2013.
  - [Raquel Siqueira da Silva, UFF thesis](https://slab.uff.br/wp-content/uploads/sites/101/2021/05/2007_d_Raquel.pdf) — 2007; accessed 2026-11-16, p. 74. Describes the first CD as *Saúde Musical*, the song “Preconceito,” early composition/recording process and the group's negotiation around municipal sponsorship. Secondary academic source; preserve its date/title conflict with the 2013 municipal release.
  - [Lia Rejane Mendes Barcellos, “Carnival and Music Therapy,” *Voices Resources*](https://www.voices.no/community/index.html?q=fortnightly-columns%252F2002-carnival-and-music-therapy) — 6 Oct. 2002; accessed 2026-11-16. Identifies embedded Track 2 as “Preconceito,” performed by Mágicos do Som, sung by composer Regina Lúcia Serrão, on *Saúde Musical*, and says the clip was presented with the composer's and coordinator's approval. The page's linked MP3 currently returns 404; treat as a documented but broken historical audio pointer, not current access or reuse permission.
  - [UFF dissertation, *Cartografias de uma experimentação musical*](https://patrimonio.uff.br/wp-content/uploads/sites/101/2021/05/2007_d_Raquel.pdf) — 2007; accessed 2026-11-16. Identifies “Preconceito” as written and sung by Regina Lúcia Serrão in 2000 and as the first CD's working song; the dissertation notes that audio is on the attached CD-ROM. The annex is not yet verified as publicly accessible.

### Harmonia Enlouquece — Centro Psiquiátrico Rio de Janeiro (CPRJ), Rio de Janeiro, Brazil (2000/2001 onward)

- **Status:** VERIFIED multi-album recording corpus; **CONTEXT ONLY** for obscurity (the group has significant public, media and streaming visibility). This is a valuable non-prison selection and a counterexample to reducing music from mental-health settings to songs about illness or treatment.
- **Formation / relationship:** The official group site says the workshop “Convivendo com a Música” began at CPRJ in 2000 and that Harmonia Enlouquece formed in 2001. It describes the group as comprising service users and health professionals, with a changing membership. Do not describe every performer as a patient or infer individual health histories.
- **Musical range:** The group's own account says most compositions concern love, human relationships, violence in peripheral neighbourhoods, ecology and everyday life. Institutional experience is one source of material, not the whole repertoire. The official site currently lists five albums and 66 recorded songs.
- **Release and track evidence:** The 2007 Regional Psychology Council of Rio de Janeiro reported that the group already had a CD. A 2011 Globo Ciência feature said two CDs had been released and a third was being recorded. The official site documents later recordings and points listeners to Spotify. Apple Music lists the fifth album, *Esse 5° É Dos Infernos* (5 July 2026), with 13 tracks, including “O Voo da Águia,” “Tanta Violencia,” “Às Vezes Eu Estou Bem,” “Preciso Cantar,” “A Cada Novo Dia,” “Na Descida Do Morro,” “Filhos Do Sol,” “Desde 1500,” “Doce Ilusão,” “Sou Soul,” “Amor Amor” and “Adora Dora.” These platform metadata and official group statements establish a broad current discography; no claim is made here about having auditioned the tracks.
- **Earlier CD detail:** The second CD *Pra distrair a lembrança do irritado* is documented as a 2008 release; a specialist Brazilian record-shop page lists 11 tracks spanning rock, samba, funk, bossa nova, xote, soul and pop. Treat that seller listing as secondary metadata and confirm against the physical sleeve before final discographic use.
- **Why it matters for curation:** This is a mixed user/professional ensemble with a broad catalogue, including ordinary love songs, social observation, ecology and neighbourhood life. It offers non-prison tracks that need not be framed around psychiatric themes.
- **Access / rights:** The group website links to its Spotify profile; Apple Music lists the 2026 album. These are authorised listening routes only to the extent provided by the platforms/rightsholders; no download or reuse permission inferred. No audio auditioned; no contact made.
- **Sources:**
  - [Harmonia Enlouquece — official site](https://harmoniaenlouquece.org.br/) — accessed 2026-11-16. First-party history, membership description, five-album/66-song count, thematic range and official listening links.
  - [Conselho Regional de Psicologia do Rio de Janeiro, “Grupo Harmonia Enlouquece encerra primeiro dia da Mostra”](https://www.crprj.org.br/noticia/view/4505) — 9 July 2007; accessed 2026-11-16. Confirms a CD existed and that the group comprised service users and professionals.
  - [Globo Ciência, “Música é facilitadora na inserção de doentes mentais na vida cotidiana”](https://redeglobo.globo.com/globociencia/noticia/2011/09/musica-e-facilitadora-na-insercao-de-doentes-mentais-na-vida-cotidiana.html) — 17 Sept. 2011; accessed 2026-11-16. Documents the workshop, composition process, two CDs, and other related groups.
  - [Apple Music artist page](https://music.apple.com/us/artist/harmonia-enlouquece/1631734647) — accessed 2026-11-16. Lists the fifth album *Esse 5° É Dos Infernos*, dated 5 July 2026, with 13 track titles.
  - [Tracks Rio, *Pra distrair a lembrança do irritado*](https://www.tracksrio.com.br/shop/harmonia-enlouquece-pra-distrair-a-lembranca-do-irritado-cd-novo-239) — accessed 2026-11-16. Secondary seller metadata for the 2008 second CD and its tracklist/genres; verify against sleeve.
  - [Universidade Federal de Santa Catarina, “Congresso Brasileiro de Saúde Mental terá shows…”](https://noticias.ufsc.br/2008/12/congresso-brasileiro-de-saude-mental-tera-shows-de-bandas-formadas-por-usuarios-de-centros-de-atencao-psicossocial/) — 2 Dec. 2008; accessed 2026-11-16. Also identifies Black Confusion as a Porto Alegre rap group formed by young participants in municipal mental-health programmes; no extant recording was verified in this pass.

### Adjacent non-prison music-group leads — not yet promoted as separate catalogue items

- **Cancioneiros do IPUB** — formed in 1996 at the Instituto de Psiquiatria da UFRJ; a songbook plus CD was issued in 1998. A 2024 Fiocruz interview with vocalist Orlando Baptista provides a current first-person research route. Need to identify the physical CD/songbook, tracklist, composers and authorised listening copy before adding a stable catalogue entry. Source: [Fiocruz, “Vocalista do grupo Cancioneiros do Ipub expõe sua vivência com a música”](https://cee.fiocruz.br/podcast/vocalista-do-grupo-cancioneiros-do-ipub-no-enloucast/) — 1 Oct. 2024; accessed 2026-11-16.
- **Black Confusion** — Porto Alegre rap group formed in 2004 after a rap workshop for young people in municipal mental-health programmes. A 2008 UFSC event notice confirms the group and describes themes including psychiatric reform, violence, oppression, exclusion, race and political participation. No particular extant recording or release was identified; retain as a context/discovery lead only. Source: [UFSC, 2 Dec. 2008](https://noticias.ufsc.br/2008/12/congresso-brasileiro-de-saude-mental-tera-shows-de-bandas-formadas-por-usuarios-de-centros-de-atencao-psicossocial/).

### Curation balance note

Prison, jail, detention and camp recordings remain important strands, but they are not the project's identity or default discovery lens. The next selection pass should deliberately include non-prison material from several different environments and musical worlds: community mental-health groups (Mágicos do Som; selected Harmonia Enlouquece tracks), resident-led sanatorium music (Takashi Tōjō / Ōshima Seishōen), intentional-community DIY recordings (ARSCH), refugee/diaspora music (Sampari and East Awin), peer-run studios/radio (Thorvaldsen/Rooster Music and Dragon Café), itinerant/community field recordings (Anders Enevig), and underground home/cassette practice. Do not replace one narrow institutional focus with another; rotate among regions, languages, genres and non-institutional as well as institutional settings.


### 2026-11-16 — Mágicos do Som: first-CD audio pointer recovered, but link is broken

- Found Lia Rejane Mendes Barcellos's *Voices Resources* article “Carnival and Music Therapy” (6 Oct. 2002). It explicitly identifies the embedded Track 2 as Mágicos do Som's arrangement of “Preconceito,” sung by composer Regina Lúcia Serrão, from the *Saúde Musical* CD, and states that the clip was presented with the composer's and coordinator's approval at the time. This is the strongest contemporaneous item-level evidence yet for the first CD and its audio.
- The article's MP3 link resolves to `https://www.voices.no/grafikk/colbar2.mp3`, which returned 404 when followed on 2026-11-16. Do not call this a working stream or say the audio was auditioned.
- The 2007 UFF dissertation by Raquel Siqueira da Silva separately identifies “Preconceito” as written and sung by Regina Lúcia Serrão in 2000, the first CD's working song, and says the audio is on the dissertation's attached CD-ROM. This supports the first-CD attribution/date while leaving the first-CD title/date discrepancy with the 2013 second-CD announcement explicit.
- The 2020 *Musicoterapia no Rio de Janeiro — Novos Rumos* book reproduces the song's full text and again credits Regina Lúcia Serrão; the 2015 article in *Psico* lists members and discusses the group, but neither supplies a current authorised audio route.
- Current assessment: first CD *Saúde Musical* and the specific song “Preconceito” are now better evidenced; a historical audio pointer exists but is dead. The 2013 second CD remains a distinct ten-song release under the same reported title. No tracklist conflation.
- Rights boundary: the 2002 page's approval applies to presentation in that article at that time; it is not a blanket modern licence. No audio download, listening, contact or rights clearance occurred.
- Next: request from the UFF repository/author or the group/coordinator an accessible research/listening copy of the dissertation annex CD-ROM and confirmation of first-CD issue details; separately seek the 2013 second-CD sleeve/full tracklist and an authorised current listening route.
- Sources: https://www.voices.no/community/index.html?q=fortnightly-columns%252F2002-carnival-and-music-therapy ; https://patrimonio.uff.br/wp-content/uploads/sites/101/2021/05/2007_d_Raquel.pdf ; https://content.amtrj.com.br/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/livro_2020-musicoterapia_no-rio-de-janeiro_novos-rumos-7.pdf ; https://revistaseletronicas.pucrs.br/revistapsico/article/download/1423/1988/0 .


### 2026-11-16 — Harmonia Enlouquece fifth-album release and title reconciliation

- The official Rio de Janeiro State Health Secretariat reported on 2026-08-15 that Harmonia Enlouquece launched its fifth album, titled *O Quinto dos Infernos*, with 13 tracks, 11 previously unreleased compositions by Hamilton de Jesus Assunção. The report identifies the group as a mixed ensemble of service users and health professionals and says the catalogue then contained 65 recorded songs.
- The group's official site currently reports five albums and 66 recorded songs, describes a mixed user/professional formation, and says most compositions concern love, human relationships, violence in peripheral neighbourhoods, ecology and everyday life. It links to Spotify.
- Apple Music and Amazon Music metadata instead list a 13-track album as *Esse 5° É Dos Infernos*, dated 5 July 2026, with copyright ℗© Hamilton Assunção. The listed tracks are “De Arrepiar Osso,” “Adora Dora,” “Amor Amor,” “Sou Soul,” “Doce Ilusão,” “Desde 1500,” “Filhos Do Sol,” “Na Descida Do Morro,” “A Cada Novo Dia,” “Preciso Cantar,” “Às Vezes Eu Estou Bem,” “Tanta Violencia” and “O Voo Da Águia.”
- These appear to be two title/date renderings for the same fifth album (13 tracks, same artist and release period), but no physical sleeve or label-side catalogue number was checked. Retain the title discrepancy explicitly rather than silently normalising it. The official site, state-health announcement and platform metadata give a strong listening/discovery route, but no track has been auditioned and no reuse rights are implied.
- Curation note: the group says most of its catalogue covers ordinary personal and social subjects, not only psychiatric experience. This supports selecting tracks on their own musical/textual merits and not describing the group solely through its institutional setting. No track-level thematic choice can be made from titles alone.
- Sources: https://harmoniaenlouquece.org.br/ ; https://www.rj.gov.br/saude/node/3948 ; https://music.apple.com/us/artist/harmonia-enlouquece/1631734647 ; https://music.amazon.ca/albums/B0HKPWSSGD .
- Next: use the official Spotify route or approved group contact for listening, then identify compositions with verified lyrical/social themes and confirm whether the release's official title is *O Quinto dos Infernos* or *Esse 5° É Dos Infernos*. Do not download or reuse without permission.

## Brazil — underground home-studio and demo-cassette networks (2026-11-16)

### Demo-Tapes Brasil / G.D.E. (Santa Maria, Rio Grande do Sul)

- Research route: Portuguese-language small-web discovery located Demo-Tapes Brasil, a collector-run archive of Brazilian underground demo cassettes. The curator says his collection began in 1987 through fanzine work, bands, postal swaps, donations and purchases; he began rescuing and digitising tapes in 2010; the blog launched in 2011. His account describes the tape-deck digitisation process and cassette degradation. A Portuguese-language academic study independently describes the archive as a substantial source of Brazilian underground demos, reporting approximately 900 tapes in the broader collection and noting its audio, artwork and production metadata. This is a valuable non-prison/non-clinical small-web route.
- G.D.E. (Grupo de Extermínio), No Art Pictures (1995): PARTIALLY VERIFIED / PROMISING. Santa Maria, RS; post-punk/industrial. The archive post credits Ricardo (guitar/vocals), Max (bass/vocals), Lincoln (drums), Galileu (keyboards/vocals) and Lule (bass/vocals). It says the audio was supplied by the band and the cover came from the collector's private collection. It lists 17 tracks and 59:34 total duration. No production detail appears on the cassette cover according to the post. “Schizophrenic Workshop” is only a track title; do not infer a diagnosis or biography from it.
- G.D.E., Synthetic Apocalypse (1996): PARTIALLY VERIFIED / PROMISING. Same city and post-punk/industrial scene. The archive post says recorded on four tracks in May 1996; credits Max (vocals/electric drill), Lulê (bass/keyboards), Lincoln (drum machine) and Ricardo (guitar/bass); audio supplied by the band, cover from the collector's collection. Nine tracks, 52:37 total. The same post points to a further 1997 Synthetic Apocalypse; do not merge versions until tracklists and artwork are compared.
- Related leads, not catalogue entries: Caustic's 1995 home-studio demo (industrial/EBM, Totem Records distribution); Caustic's Unrest (1995); Os Rochas' Brasília demo (1989); Tubaína do Demônio's four-track São Paulo demo (1993). See the dedicated dossier for tracklists and exact links.
- Why it fits: these are non-institutional underground circulation environments—home studios, four-track recording, private cassette collections, fanzines, postal swaps, small distribution and collector-led rescue—not prison or psychiatric-institution projects. Do not treat DIY as proof of outsider identity or as evidence of health status.
- Rights and verification: blog MP3 links are discovery/listening routes only; the curator's statement that audio was supplied by the band does not itself establish permission for reuse. No audio downloaded or auditioned, no physical tape inspected, no contact made and no rights cleared.
- Sources: https://demo-tapes-brasil.blogspot.com/p/nossa-historia.html ; https://demo-tapes-brasil.blogspot.com/2012/12/ ; https://ojs.sites.ufsc.br/index.php/sceh/article/download/2964/2584 ; https://www1.folha.uol.com.br/fsp/1994/9/28/ilustrada/11.html .
- Detailed note: research/brazil-demo-tapes-small-web-2026-11-16.md.

### Curation balance

Add G.D.E. as a non-institutional DIY cassette strand alongside, not instead of, commune/barn recordings, resident-led sanatorium music, community mental-health music, refugee/diaspora recordings, peer-run radio/studios and rural/itinerant music. The next pass should rotate to another region or a different musical environment rather than continuing to mine Brazilian punk/industrial demos alone.


### G.D.E. 1997 title cross-reference — unresolved

The archive's own all-posts index lists G.D.E. No Art Pictures (1995) and Synthetic Apocalypse (1996), but no separate G.D.E. 1997 post. The 1995 post's “also available: Synthetic Apocalypse (1997)” remains an unresolved cross-reference, not proof of a third tape or alternate issue. Keep the 1996 tape separate and do not create another ID until artwork/tracklist/source-tape evidence is found. Source: https://demo-tapes-brasil.blogspot.com/p/todos-os-links.html .

## Philippines — Cordillera community cassette culture and Salidummay / DKK (2026-11-16)

### IM-042 — Salidummay, Dap-ayan ti Kultura iti Kordilyera

- **Status:** PARTIALLY VERIFIED / PROMISING. An extant ten-song album and cassette release are documented, but the first cassette's date and the digital object's exact relation to the original tape remain unresolved.
- **Recording:** *Salidummay* (also spelled *Salidumay* in some sources), by Salidummay / Dap-ayan ti Kultura iti Kordilyera (DKK). A current Spotify album lists ten tracks and 32:46 total duration, with a platform date of 24 April 1987 and ℗ 2023 DKK Cultural Alliance. The 2023 *Lakbayan* exhibition catalogue also lists a 1987 DKK cassette. Ethnomusicologist Michiyo Yoneno-Reyes instead dates DKK's first ten-song cassette *Salidumay* to 1989. Do not silently resolve the 1987/1989 discrepancy or assume the platform object is a bit-identical transfer of either tape edition.
- **Environment / scene:** DKK is a cultural alliance associated with the Cordillera Peoples Alliance (CPA), whose official history describes a federation rooted mainly in Indigenous communities of the Cordillera and founded in 1984 amid opposition to the Chico dams project and commercial logging. CPA identifies cultural workers and DKK as a regional alliance of cultural organisations. Yoneno-Reyes describes the cassette's ten songs as promoting an autonomous Cordillera Region and a “unity in diversity” regionalism, in a movement shaped by opposition to mega-dam projects and logging. This is a specific community cultural/activist music setting, not an institution or psychiatric-history case.
- **Circulation / significance:** The dissertation says DKK cassette albums circulated mainly in Baguio City and the northern Luzon highlands and were among the few locally produced cassette albums in that market. It reports five cassette albums in the 1990s and five CD albums in the 2000s; the bibliography identifies separate cassettes *Dong Dong Ay. Salidummay* [1992] and *Salidumay. Elalay* [1995]. The 2023 *Lakbayan* exhibition catalogues the wider protest-music archive and describes the Salidummay-DKK albums as blending 1970s folk and Cordillera Indigenous traditions.
- **Critical context:** Do not describe salidummay as a single ancient, uniform pan-Cordillera genre. Yoneno-Reyes describes a variable family of songs/refrains and modern popular usage; a 1999 ICTM abstract cautions that treating it as a uniform regional legacy can be an “outsider's view” for some rural Igorot communities. Preserve the local and political context and do not make DKK representative of all Cordillera communities.
- **Rights / verification:** Spotify availability and an exhibition listing do not grant master, composition, artwork or commercial-use rights. No audio was downloaded or auditioned, no physical cassette inspected, no archive or collective contacted and no rights cleared.
- **Next:** inspect the original cassette/J-card/label; reconcile 1987 versus 1989; compare the ten-track platform listing with the dissertation's ten-song description; verify the distinct 1992 and 1995 tapes; seek track-level credits, language, recording location and an authorised listening/transfer route from DKK/CPA/Dang A Dang Radio.
- **Detailed dossier:** research/philippines-cordillera-salidummay-2026-11-16.md.
- **Sources:** Yoneno-Reyes dissertation: https://www.academia.edu/105350613/Yoneno_Reyes_Dissertation_Salidummay ; *Lakbayan* 2023 exhibition handout: https://www.savvy-contemporary.com/site/assets/files/9572/lakbayan_handout-2.pdf ; Spotify album: https://open.spotify.com/album/2kNLnHMRIogxoQx3Aho2ZO ; CPA “About”: https://cpaphils.org/about.html ; *The Cordillera Review*: https://thecordillerareview.upb.edu.ph/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/TCR-IV-2_Saboy_Voicing-Ethnicity-Traditional-Referentiality-the-Ullalim-and-Kalinga-Ethnopop-doi.pdf ; ICTM 1999 abstracts: https://www.ictmusic.org/sites/default/files/documents/world%20conferences/books%20of%20abstracts/abstracts%201999%20ictm%20world%20conference.pdf .

### Further Cordillera cassette leads — not separate catalogue entries yet

- *Dong Dong Ay. Salidummay* [1992] and *Salidumay. Elalay* [1995] are independently listed in Yoneno-Reyes' audiovisual bibliography and the 2023 *Lakbayan* cassette inventory (the 1992 title is listed in both). Verify physical editions and audio before assigning separate IDs.
- The same exhibition includes Patatag cassettes (1986, 1989, 1990), local worker and protest-song releases, and multiple regional recordings. These are a route into a wider Philippine community cassette ecology, not automatically candidates for this project's discography.
- The next pass should include local-language and rural community artists as well as movement archives, while avoiding a simplistic equation of Indigenous music with outsider music.

## Philippines — Cordillera vernacular-country and local-language cassette ecology (2026-11-16)

**Status: ENVIRONMENT / RECORDING LEADS; no new catalogue ID assigned.** This is a separate, non-protest, non-prison strand after IM-042. Do not reduce Cordillera recorded music to DKK/Salidummay.

Jimmy Balud Fong's 2007 *Batawa: Constructing Identity through Country Music in the Philippine Cordillera* describes local-language country/pop songs as cultural products through which Cordillera makers represent contemporary life and identity. Fong traces a 1970s wave of Ibaloy country-style 45s, a 1990s resurgence of local-language cassette recordings, new singers who reportedly became recognised in their communities after album releases, and recording in makeshift Baguio studios. Named recording leads include “Benguet Country” by RJ Mero; *Ivadoy Jen Country Songs*, featuring songs popularised by Cesar Pasiw (including “Savong Shi Bahong” and “Nonta Panagbenga”); and the video CD *Kabenguet an* by Rio Felimon Cariño and D' Friends Band. Fong also describes “Batawa” as a popular song recorded by several artists and the name of a Baguio country-music bar. These are leads pending original media, full credits, dates and item-level audio.

A 2006 *From the Boondocks* post, “Benguet Country Singer,” identifies the video singer as Rio “Filemon” Cariño; the comments identify Cariño and company as producers and include local readers' recognition and requests for more videos. It is a useful small-web/community reception trail, but the original linked video page could not be fetched and no cassette is established.

### Kinnoboyan — named local-language songwriters

Gretchen Shagami C. Mangahas' study *Indigenous Songs: An Intercultural Communication Strategy for Social Values Development Among the Youth of Benguet, Philippines* describes the MPBC's 2006 “Magic Countryhan” search and the formation of Kinnoboyan from five finalists: Junie Rey Bag-ayan, Heintje Pis-oy, Gelasio Beray, Stephen Lael Senio and Christopher Longalong. Three members were Kankana-ey and two Ibaloi. The study says the band composed twelve songs (eight Kankana-ey, three Ibaloi, one English) and lists titles including “An-anusak,” “San Biyag,” “Pangaasim Koma,” “Tsismosa,” “Bay-am Kod Pay,” “Linmingling Ka,” “Kimat,” “Ed Ili,” “834,” “No Ayshi Ha” and “Seseskeren Taha.” It reports local radio request popularity and the group's stated aim to write original songs about personal experience rather than borrow or translate melodies.

**Specific release lead:** Apple Music lists *Inlove With You* by Junie Rey Bag-Ayan and Heintje Pis-Oy, 6 October 2014, 13 tracks. Qobuz gives 59:09 and credits Junie Rey Bag-Ayan as label/main artist and Heintje Pis-Oy as main artist. Track titles include “An-Anusak,” “Halsema Highway,” “Tsismosa,” “Ed Ili” and “Nu Ayshi Ha,” overlapping with the Kinnoboyan song corpus. Apple Music separately lists Junie Rey Bag-Ayan's “An-Anusak” single as 2009. This does **not** prove that the 2014 duo album is the Kinnoboyan band's 12-song album, a cassette reissue, or the same masters heard in the study. Keep as a lead; no physical cassette is established.

### Archive routes — context only, not catalogue items

- **UP Center for Ethnomusicology:** its official facilities page reports (as of 2014) 2,424 open reels, 191 cassettes and about 20 digital items in its unpublished collection, documenting music from about 87 ethnolinguistic groups; its published collection then included 500 cassettes. The archive says material can be accessed in person or online by request at upethno_archive.upd@up.edu.ph. This is a research route, not evidence for any specific recording. Request item-level catalogue references and access/ethics conditions.
- **Library of Congress AFC 1996/028:** one cassette, *The Mountains Ring Out Their Joy*, 25 Christian songs from the northern Cordilleras, mostly a cappella, recorded by Ron Wolcott and produced by the Asian Institute for Liturgy and Music in 1987; an accompanying songbook has musical and lyric transcriptions. Concrete extant cassette, but current evidence does not establish an outsider/DIY/marginal pathway, so retain as contextual/archive lead only.
- **UP Diliman DALFCT:** 749 legacy linguistic fieldwork cassettes across numerous Philippine languages; project aims to digitise and ethically disseminate/repatriate material. This is not a music archive by default; ask for item-level catalogue details and permissions before assuming any music is present.

**Assessment:** The Cordillera local-country scene is a promising under-documented community recording ecology, but it is not automatically outsider music. Do not assign an IM-ID until a particular recording is identified, an extant carrier/authorised audio is established, and a meaningful DIY, self-organised, under-documented or marginal-circulation pathway is shown. Detailed source and next-step notes: research/philippines-cordillera-country-cassette-ecology-2026-11-16.md.

Sources: Fong 2007 full paper https://ep.liu.se/ecp/025/013/ecp072513.pdf ; publisher record https://ep.liu.se/konferensartikel.aspx?Article_No=13&issue=25&series=ecp ; Mangahas study https://www.academia.edu/8081606/Indigenous_Songs_An_Intercutural_Communication_Strategy_for_Social_Values_Development_Among_the_Youth_of_Benguet_Philippines ; Apple Music *Inlove With You* https://music.apple.com/us/album/inlove-with-you/985918635 ; Qobuz https://www.qobuz.com/us-en/album/inlove-with-you-junie-rey-bag-ayan-heintje-pis-oy/mju39gbjrq6fc ; 2006 blog https://igorotblogger.com/benguet-country-singer/ ; UPCE https://upethno.upd.edu.ph/facilities/ ; LOC https://wwws.loc.gov/folklife/guides/Philippines.html ; DALFCT https://dalfct.carrd.co/ .


## UPCE Sagada community archive and ReCollection — Kankana-ey / Ibaloy field recordings (2026-11-16; contextual archive route)

UP Center for Ethnomusicology's 2025 account of “Collection and ReCollection” documents a community-controlled Sagada sound-archiving project run with Saint Mary's School of Sagada, Inc. from 11 February to 30 June 2025. Sagada is a Kankana-ey community. Thirty-seven participants gathered and processed metadata for 200 recordings (167 audio, 33 video), including interviews, chants, songs, instrumentals and recordings at community events. The community decided what to record, whom to interview and how the project should proceed; the stated principle is that community members record themselves and retain full control of the outcomes. Original recordings remain with SMSSI; UPCE holds an off-site copy available for room use only. This is an important model of community-led archiving, not automatically an Insane Music candidate.

UPCE's ReCollection programme also documents the repatriation of Maceda Collection recordings dating from 1953 onward to Sagada/Mountain Province in 2013, 1950s field recordings returned to Sagada in 2020, and 1987 Ramon P. Santos recordings made at the Bendian Festival in Kabayan, Benguet, repatriated in 2017 alongside The Vocal Repertoire from Kabayan. Elders recognised voices in excerpts. Public descriptions do not yet provide item-level catalogue numbers, performers, titles or carrier identifiers. Keep these as contextual recording corpora until a specific item is documented; do not assume resident authorship or outsider status.

Research route: UPCE says archive materials may be accessed in person or online by request at upethno_archive.upd@up.edu.ph; ask first for catalogue-level metadata, access restrictions and community-review requirements. For the 2025 Sagada archive, respect SMSSI's control and the UPCE room-use-only copy; do not presume UPCE can authorise access to originals. Detailed dossier: research/philippines-cordillera-country-cassette-ecology-2026-11-16.md.

Sources: https://upethno.upd.edu.ph/collection-and-recollection-recording-the-traditional-vocal-and-instrumental-repertoire-in-sagada/ ; https://upethno.upd.edu.ph/the-recollection-project-connecting-the-past-with-the-present/ ; https://upethno.upd.edu.ph/facilities/ . No audio auditioned, carrier inspected, contact made or rights cleared.


### Cordillera local-country / Abatan Records cassette network — 2026-11-16 follow-up

The 12 March 2013 Philippine Daily Inquirer report “In Cordillera, resilience vs piracy” identifies Elsie Gawidan Maliones as founder of Abatan Records in the 1970s and documents original cassette sales of Kankanaey and Ibaloy singers in Baguio during the 1990s. This adds a named local label/producer and an original-cassette distribution route to the earlier Fong/Telles map of Baguio-Benguet local-language country/pop. It does not provide an itemised catalogue or prove the survival of any specific tape. Keep as a discovery/access lead, not an Insane Music entry: local-language country music is not automatically outsider music. Next seek catalogue, sleeves, tracklists and item-level carrier/audio provenance; distinguish original cassettes from later bootleg CDs and VCDs. Source: https://newsinfo.inquirer.net/372451/in-cordillera-resilience-vs-piracy . Detailed dossier: research/philippines-cordillera-country-cassette-ecology-2026-11-16.md. No audio auditioned, carrier inspected, contact made or rights cleared.

## Ghana / Ata Kak — *Obaa Sima* (IM-046): home studio and cassette-source lineage (2026-11-17)

**Pathways:** self-taught practice; DIY/home recording and self-release; idiosyncratic musical practice. This is not an institutional recording and no psychiatric history is claimed.

Yaw Atta-Owusu, recording as Ata Kak, made the seven-track *Obaa Sima* in a home studio in Toronto using second-hand equipment and Twi-language rap/highlife/electronic elements. The artist's 2015 account describes approximately 50 copies manufactured/released in Ghana in 1994 and estimates that only about three sold. A later 2025 account by Awesome Tapes From Africa founder Brian Shimkovitz says he learned that the artist's brother sold “quite a few” copies and that Kejetia market residents remembered the songs at funerals and local dance parties. The exact sales and local reach are unresolved. The work's relevance is the specific home-studio production, independent cassette manufacture and unusual local/transnational synthesis, not an assumption that Ghanaian vernacular music is inherently “outsider.”

### Source-copy history

- The original master DAT is reported to have degraded.
- The 2015 authorised reissue used a cassette bought by Awesome Tapes From Africa founder Brian Shimkovitz at a Cape Coast roadside stall in 2002; the artist's own cassette and other located copies were reported as too damaged to use.
- The 2025 *Anniversary Remaster* is a separate transfer. Its official Bandcamp page says that in early 2024 Shimkovitz received a clean cassette copy from a new acquaintance in his Berlin neighbourhood. The copy's acquisition history, duplication generation and relationship to the 1994 Ghana run are not stated. Do not assume it is the original master or a first-generation 1994 cassette. The same 2025 page states that the original recording was made at a slower speed and that a slow-version download is included. Its credits attribute all songs to Yaw Atta-Owusu and state that the reissue is under exclusive licence from him; the page marks the release all rights reserved.
- The official 2025 page lists a seven-track release dated 7 November 2025, with digital files offered at 24-bit/96 kHz and a cassette edition. It describes the source as a new, cleaner / nearly pristine cassette. The page marks the release all rights reserved.

### Bounded source search and open questions

The searches site:ghanaweb.com Ata Kak "Obaa Sima" cassette, site:myjoyonline.com Ata Kak "Obaa Sima" Ghana and site:graphic.com.gh Ata Kak "Obaa Sima" cassette did not locate a relevant Ghanaian local-news or local-archive record; the returned “Obaa Sima” results were unrelated. These were English-language site queries; no Twi-language query was run, so local-language documentation remains an open search gap. This is not evidence that no Ghanaian documentation exists.

The 2015 and 2025 Bandcamp pages list slightly different track durations. The 2025 page also explicitly says the original recording was slower and includes a slow-version download. These are version-comparison clues only; no edits or transfer differences should be inferred without comparing the audio.

**Sources**
- Artist/label, 2015 edition and recording account: https://atakak.bandcamp.com/album/obaa-sima
- Artist/label, 2025 anniversary remaster and Shimkovitz's Berlin-copy account: https://atakak.bandcamp.com/album/obaa-sima-anniversary-remaster
- Ata Kak interview, originally published by *The 405*: https://medium.com/@martynpepperell/ata-kak-interview-originally-published-via-the-405-a2f23c98b1f4
- Reissue announcement: https://ra.co/news/27656
- 2025 cassette product record: https://finersounds.com/products/ata-kak-obaa-sima-anniversary-remaster-cassette-tape

No audio auditioned, no physical carrier inspected, no contact made and no rights cleared. **Next:** establish the Berlin cassette's provenance, then compare the authorised 2015 and 2025 transfer versions; keep both source copies distinct from the degraded master DAT.


## 2026-11-18 research update — Mágicos do Som and Tardes Nuestras

### IM-038 — Mágicos do Som

The apparent *Saúde Musical* title/date conflict is now best explained by **two separate CDs with the same title**. The 2007 UFF thesis and 2002 *Voices Resources* article document a first *Saúde Musical* CD (c. 2000), including “Preconceito” by Regina Lúcia Serrão; the 2013 municipal notice explicitly calls its ten-song *Saúde Musical* the second CD; the 2021 municipal retrospective confirms two CDs and identifies 2013 as the latest. The 1999 clipping reproduced in the thesis shows preparation, while the November 2000 clipping reports a launch on the 22nd; the exact date is not sufficiently clear to assert more than c. 2000. The two releases remain one group-level baseline lead, not two new leads. The first CD's historical MP3 link did not resolve; the thesis annex CD-ROM and the second CD have not been accessed. See research/magicos-do-som-im-038-2026-11-18.md.

### IM-049 — Tardes Nuestras opening theme

Qualified one new Argentina community-radio lead: a recurring rock theme used by *Tardes Nuestras*, a programme created by users and workers associated with Hospital Agudo Ávila / Centro Cultural Gomecito and broadcast by FM Aire Libre in Rosario. A 2017 UNR thesis says the theme was produced with musicians; a 2020 *La Capital* article quotes its opening lyric and confirms it starts the weekly show; the station's official page links recorded programmes through RadioCut. The song's title, authors and performer remain unknown, and resident authorship/performance is not established. The RadioCut route returned an access failure; no audio was auditioned. Count the recurring theme once, not each episode. See research/tardes-nuestras-im-049-2026-11-18.md.

Sources and access dates are recorded in the two dossiers. No contact, purchase, download, archive access or rights clearance was performed.


## 2026-11-18 follow-up — IM-039 Harmonia Enlouquece fifth-album metadata

The 2026 fifth album now has an exact platform item page and a more precise date/title crosswalk. Amazon Music lists **Esse 5° É Dos Infernos**, 13 tracks / 46 minutes, platform date 5 July 2026, ℗© Hamilton Assunção. The official state-health article from 8 April called the forthcoming album **O Quinto dos Infernos** and named “O Amor Chegou” as a Hamilton composition/highlight; the 15 August Agência Brasil launch report uses the same official album title, states 13 tracks and 11 unreleased tracks by Hamilton, and places the public launch at MuhCab. Treat 5 July as the platform-listed date and 15 August as the public launch event, not a date conflict.

The exact 13-track platform list is captured in research/harmonia-enlouquece-im-039-2026-11-18.md. “O Amor Chegou” is absent from that list; Apple Music's currently accessible artist page instead exposes a 2022 track “Hoje o Amor Chegou.” No link between those titles is established. The official group site reports 66 songs, while the 15 August report says 65; preserve both counts pending a master discography. The 11/13 Hamilton authorship statement does not establish authorship of the remaining two tracks.

This is metadata refinement only: retain IM-039 as context-only, with no new candidate or download request. Amazon Music is a streaming item page, not a direct audio/download URL; ℗© metadata is not reuse clearance. No audio auditioned or downloaded, no contact made, no rights cleared.


## IM-048 — Santo André CAPS-DQ *Viajar* chronology / circulation clarification (2026-11-18)

Two contemporaneous Diário do Grande ABC reports strengthen the record for the Santo André CAPS-DQ CD *Viajar – Ida e Volta no Caminho do Inconsciente Poético e Musical*: the 23 Nov 2001 launch notice describes nine songs and approximately 500 free copies for institutions/projects; the 13 Jan 2002 follow-up again gives the nine-track count, says workshop lyrics were written by students in 1999, and identifies volunteer regional arrangements and the CAPS-DQ choir on the title song. The reports distinguish participant-authored lyrics from guest interpretation; do not treat the CD as nine resident performances. Historical distribution is supported, but present carrier survival, complete sleeve data, current access and rights remain unresolved. No audio auditioned, no carrier inspected, no contact made and no rights cleared.

## 2026-11-19 — Curitiba DIY discovery and regional exclusion register

### Core lead IM-050 — Anões de Jardim, Demo (1993)

The band’s preserved Real Deal page says Anões de Jardim formed in January 1993, first played at Curitiba’s 92 Degrees, and recorded an eight-song first demo live at Studio Solo in July 1993; it explicitly describes the first demo’s independent distribution as weak. The 1994 follow-up Extra! had better circulation (the page says more than 500 copies) and an MTV clip, but that is a separate recording and must not be used to inflate the 1993 demo’s visibility.

A collector’s 11 December 2012 post identifies a private-collection cassette copy, lists eight tracks and an 18:51 running time, and records a 50-second lead-in on side B and a cut final track. A 4 July 1997 Folha de Londrina report independently places the band among Curitiba groups at National Garage and documents the local alternative circuit around the 92 Degrees venue, Studio Solo and the independent Bloody label. This supports pathway F/D as a specific low-circulation DIY scene, not a diagnosis or an assumption that every obscure band is an outsider.

Sources:
- Band/label history (preserved Real Deal page): https://www.oocities.org/sunsetstrip/4509/adj.htm
- Collector item record: https://demo-tapes-brasil.blogspot.com/2012/12/anoes-de-jardim-demo-1993.html
- Contemporaneous local press: https://www.folhadelondrina.com.br/folha-2/holofotes-sobre-o-underground-29576.html

### Context-only screens — not counted as core new leads

- **Radio La Colifata / Viva La Colifata Vol I–II (Buenos Aires, Argentina).** The current official site describes 20 tracks made from Colifata participants’ songs, poetry and improvisations in collaboration with Manu Chao; a 2009 Andina report also describes resident-generated material mixed with his musical bases. The official site gives 2005, while the contemporary report is dated July 2009; preserve this date/version conflict. The resident-generated content is important context, but the high-profile collaboration and broad circulation make this a context-only screen for the current obscurity target; no track-by-track resident authorship or exact release/master crosswalk was established. No audio was played.
  - https://www.vivalacolifata.org/
  - https://andina.pe/agencia/noticia-manu-chao-graba-nuevo-disco-hospital-psiquiatrico-argentino-245956.aspx
- **Leprosy Mission Choir, Mizoram, India.** The 2020 refereed Mizo Studies article describes this as a mission-formed gospel choir whose members are selected from different church denominations; it does not establish that the members are patients/residents or that the recordings were made by people living in the mission environment. Do not infer patient status from the choir’s name. Context only; no core lead counted.
  - https://mizostudiesmzu.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/12/Mizo-Studies-Vol.-IX-No.-4-October-December-2020.pdf
- **Band-Aid, Sewri TB Hospital, Mumbai, India.** A 2018 report documents five young people undergoing MDR-TB treatment forming a band and performing for other patients in the hospital. No item-level surviving recording or stable sound-recording object was located in this pass, so this remains a performance-context lead, not a main-discography candidate.
  - https://www.thequint.com/amp/story/fit/tb-patients-form-a-band-in-mumbai-sewri-hospital

Regional screening did not justify counting any of the three contextual screens. Continue the geographic rotation in a different region next run; do not count keyword hits or performances without an extant recording.



## Additional qualified institutional performance — ZeN at Bakırköy (IM-052)

- **Status:** VERIFIED recording / PROMISING visibility; main-discography candidate, medium priority.
- **Recording:** ZeN, *Bakırköy Akıl Hastanesi'nde*, live performance in 1998; original cassette/CD issued in 1999; 2023 Ada Müzik Turkish 2LP and separate Zel Zele European 2LP edition, the latter limited to 300 copies.
- **Environment connection:** a visiting underground improvisation band performed at Bakırköy Psychiatric Hospital for an audience including patients, nurses, doctors and visitors. Contemporary 1998 *Roll* reporting and Murat Ertel's first-person account document the event and the musicians' decision to adapt their performance to the room.
- **Classification:** A (music performed in an institutional setting) + F (underground scene). **Not resident-made, not patient-composed.** Keep the visitor/performance distinction visible in every catalogue summary.
- **Why it fits:** a scarce, niche Turkish underground live recording whose institutional context is directly documented; the 2023 limited reissue makes it less obscure than an unissued patient-made tape, so do not overstate priority.
- **Cautions / next steps:** verify the exact event time and inviting staff count; compare the 1999 cassette/CD track order with the 2023 Ada/Zel Zele LP variants; verify the original recording chain and master custody. No diagnosis or patient identity is inferred. No audio was played or downloaded and no rights were cleared.
- **Sources:** 1998 *Roll* interview https://birartibir.org/wp-content/uploads/ROLL_1998_021.pdf ; 1999 *Roll* coverage https://birartibir.org/wp-content/uploads/ROLL_1999_034.pdf ; participant interview https://www.psychedelicbabymag.com/2024/10/baba-zula-interview-murat-ertel.html ; label reissue https://zelzelerecords.bandcamp.com/album/zen-bak-rk-y-ak-l-hastanesinde


## 2026-11-19 — Chile / Hospital El Peral: Radio Estación del Paraíso (context-only screen)

A local-language source pass identified a music-bearing patient-run radio corpus and its documentary record. The 2000–2001 *Espacios locales y desarrollo ciudadanía* case study (pp. 302–303) says Radio El Peral was produced and operated by patients and that its October 2000 Santiago Amable broadcast mixed pre-recorded and live songs and poems. It describes twelve participants, including ten inpatients with programme sections, but names no songs, composers, performers or carriers. Do not infer that all broadcast music was participant-authored.

CineChile records Francisco Schultz's *La furia de los caballos sin patas* as a 67-minute 2001 documentary (DVCam / 16 mm; sound Roberto Martí; national premiere 29 November 2001). A 2011 OCEP post by radio-team psychologist Rubén Nilo says the film soundtrack includes radio transmissions and unreleased tracks by Shogun, Tobías Alcayota and Joe Villablanca. A 2024 public YouTube upload credits “Jeo Villablanca” and labels the film 2004; preserve the name/date differences and do not assume a separate soundtrack album.

The 2023 doctoral thesis by Nicolás Morales Sáez describes the original recordings and microprogrammes as last known at Hospital Barros Luco, with the team reporting that cassettes/discs became inaccessible after a 2003 lockout; the researcher did not locate the microprogrammes in Radio Tierra / Archivo Nacional records. This is an access/custody problem, not proof of destruction. No contact or archive request was made.

**Status:** context-only C lead, not a new catalogue item. No exact song or participant-created recording is yet identified. Keep documentary mix, broadcast masters, microprogrammes and external soundtrack tracks separate. No audio was played or downloaded and no rights were cleared.

Detailed dossier: research/radio-estacion-del-paraiso-el-peral-chile-2026-11-19.md.
